# Evidence pack: Supermassive black hole masses from gas dynamics (`smbh-gas-dynamics`)

Scope: nuclear molecular gas kinematics to weigh central black holes

Built from 103 non-held-out ALMA papers whose methods cards passed mechanical evidence verification (every quote below is verbatim from the paper). Years 2013-2026.
Matcher (regex over analysis steps): `black hole mass|smbh mass|\bm_?bh\b|gas[- ]dynamical|sphere of influence`
Tagging is by regex, so a few papers may be off-topic: ignore those.

## Aggregates over all papers

### Assumptions (bibcode: value)
- 2021MNRAS.504.4123N: Gas moves on circular orbits in a dynamically thin, cold disc (asymmetric drift ignored); axisymmetric Gaussian gas surface brightness profile; D_A = 68.4 Mpc
- 2021MNRAS.504.4123N: Bell & de Jong (2001) and Nguyen et al. (2019) colour-M/L relations; central saturated pixels in F814W interpolated from outer pixels
- 2021MNRAS.504.4123N: Virial equilibrium in the BLR; geometrically thin planar BLR disc with (h/R_BLR) ~ 0
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: Virialized, isotropic and spherically symmetric gas motions.
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: Solar metallicity for LEdd; AGN coronal synchrotron dominates ESO 253-G003 ∼100 GHz continuum.

### Limitations the authors admit
- 2021MNRAS.504.4123N: The ALMA synthesised beam (~0.3'' or ~100 pc) is ~30 times larger than the black hole sphere of influence r_SOI (~3 pc or ~0.01''), making this the lowest relative angular resolution M_BH measurement…
- 2021MNRAS.504.4123N: Central saturation in the HST ACS F814W image prevents an accurate pixel-by-pixel colour-based stellar mass-surface density measurement at the very centre where the SMBH potential dominates.
- 2021MNRAS.504.4123N: High-velocity residuals (~30 km s^-1) near the galaxy centre in both [CI](1-0) and 12CO(1-0) velocity fields may indicate non-circular motions, asymmetric rotation, or beam smearing, which could mean…
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: Only two single-AGN systems provide an incomplete, preliminary comparison sample.
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: SMBH masses and SoI radii depend on methodology; merger systems may depart from the MBH–σ* relation.
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: Most lower-bound SoIs are unresolved, limiting correlation constraints.
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: Galaxy-integrated r21 is used because robust 50–100 pc line ratios in major mergers are unavailable.
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: Global missing flux can inflate SoI gas fractions by ∼3 for ESO 253-G003 and ∼1.2–1.4 for other systems.
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: Some MCMC posteriors are bimodal or non-Gaussian.
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: Projected SoI gas masses and non-rotating gas masses are upper limits to enclosed and infalling gas masses, respectively.
- 2025ApJ...995..108J: The nuclear concentration versus activity trend is not statistically significant and needs a larger sample.
- 2026MNRAS.548ag546D: A central depression (~0.2 arcsec / ~19 pc radius) in the molecular gas is similar in size to the SMBH sphere of influence, preventing a clear Keplerian velocity rise within R_SoI.
- 2026MNRAS.548ag546D: Deprojection of the 2D MGE surface brightness to 3D volume density is mathematically non-unique, and the degeneracy between inclination and dynamical mass is severe at the low inclination of NGC 1387.
- 2026MNRAS.548ag546D: The heuristic rescaling of pixel uncertainties by (2N)^0.25 is not rigorously justified.
- 2026MNRAS.548ag546D: The MGE model assumes axisymmetry and provides a superficially poor fit across the non-axisymmetric bar region, and the axial ratio q'=0.98 was determined by eye.
- 2026MNRAS.548ag546D: Galaxy distance uncertainty is not included in the final SMBH mass uncertainty budget.
- 2019ApJ...881...10B: Model not a formally acceptable fit (chi^2_nu=1.179); correlated noise; local irregularities in velocity/dispersion fields
- 2019ApJ...881...10B: Distance disagreements among estimates imply M_BH may lie in (2.0-3.8)e9 Msun
- 2021ApJ...908...19B: The NGC 315 systematic error budget is provisional because no model is a formally acceptable fit
- 2021ApJ...908...19B: The NGC 4261 S/N does not permit a more general disk structure; degeneracies among M_BH, i and sigma_turb remain
- 2021ApJ...908...19B: M_BH scales linearly with the assumed distance, which is uncertain for NGC 315 and not folded into the error budget
- 2019ApJ...883..193N: Missing flux from lack of short baselines, so masses are lower limits
- 2019ApJ...883..193N: Residual is not a true chi2 because neighbouring pixels are correlated; modelling assumptions simple
- 2017ApJ...843L..35W: ~50% of data flagged due to antenna problems
- 2017ApJ...843L..35W: Conjugate points cause degeneracies; position uncertainties overestimated
- 2021MNRAS.503.5583B: Formal uncertainties on 3DBarolo dynamical masses do not include the significant uncertainty on galaxy inclination and are therefore likely under-estimated.
- 2021MNRAS.503.5583B: Considerable uncertainties exist in both dynamical and gas masses, as shown by discrepancies between 3DBarolo and exponential disk models and between CO and CI gas mass tracers.
- 2021MNRAS.503.5583B: Modest angular resolution of the ALMA observations (~0.8-1.0'') means that the smooth velocity gradient in J1234 could still be a merging system close to or soon after final coalescence, and individu…
- 2021MNRAS.503.5583B: The blue side of the broad CO(7-6) wing in G1234S cannot be traced because of contamination by the adjacent CI(2-1) emission line, and no X-ray/mid-IR data yet confirm an AGN in G1234S.
- 2021MNRAS.503.5583B: The Illustris-1 simulation volume ((106.5 cMpc)^3) is too small to contain >10^8 M_sun black holes at z ~ 6.
- 2021MNRAS.503.5583B: Sample size of only two heavily reddened quasars and lack of a matched control sample of unobscured UV-luminous quasars with resolved gas observations limit firm conclusions.
- 2015ApJ...811..115W: Fluxes of A, B, C not used owing to differential magnification (~30-50%)
- 2025PASJ...77..432U: Stellar mass profile outside R~8 kpc is extrapolated for lack of sensitivity
- 2025PASJ...77..432U: CO(1-0) is not well resolved by the JVLA, so the Toomre Q rests on assumed profile
- 2025PASJ...77..432U: Limited profile sampling prevents fitting a third (bar) component in the stellar profile
- 2025PASJ...77..432U: Limited NIRCam spatial coverage and few Gaia stars make absolute astrometry hard
- 2015ApJ...806...39O: Inclination uncertainty and possible warp or asymmetry not modelled
- 2026MNRAS.546ag037E: Scaling archival mm fluxes from 98, 104, and 350 GHz to 230 GHz assuming a flat spectral index (alpha = 0) introduces uncertainties of ~6% to 14%.
- 2026MNRAS.546ag037E: Non-simultaneous mm and X-ray observations (especially for A0620-00 where Chandra data is from 2013 and F_var = 0.83, and GRS 1915+105 with a 9-month gap) mean temporal variability can significantly …
- 2026MNRAS.546ag037E: Potential contribution from the donor star wind to the mm emission of Cygnus X-1 is not modeled.
- 2026MNRAS.546ag037E: Large uncertainties in the free parameters describing plasma physics in both ADAF and compact jet models (kept fixed to default values calibrated only for SMBHs) prevent strong conclusions about the …
- 2015MNRAS.452...88K: Low spatial resolution prevents determining inclination and detailed kinematics
- 2020ApJ...903...34A: Only two continuum points on the Rayleigh-Jeans tail, so dust temperature and SED shape unconstrained; dust mass has at least a factor of two systematic uncertainty
- 2020ApJ...903...34A: Black hole mass systematic errors (~0.5 dex) not included; virial calibration may be inadequate for WLQs
- 2020ApJ...903...34A: Shielding gas between disk and BLR cannot be excluded without X-ray spectroscopy
- 2024MNRAS.528L..76R: Primary sample is constructed based on data availability only and is not statistically complete.
- 2024MNRAS.528L..76R: In 3 out of 48 galaxies, the central mm emission is slightly resolved, making their flux measurements more uncertain.
- 2024MNRAS.528L..76R: Non-simultaneous nuclear mm and X-ray observations are subject to AGN time variability (factor of 2-3 over year timescales), which likely dominates the observed scatter.
- 2024MNRAS.528L..76R: Five X-ray observed sources lack Chandra data and could have L_X,2-10 slightly overestimated due to diffuse hot gas or X-ray binaries.
- 2024MNRAS.528L..76R: Default ADAF model parameters overpredict L_nu,mm at a given SMBH mass by ~0.5 dex, and plasma physics parameters in both ADAF and compact jet models are poorly constrained.
- 2024ApJ...966..132K: Models do not tightly constrain M_BH because the SOI is unresolved
- 2024ApJ...966..132K: The extinction correction is a simple method; proper treatment would need radiative transfer models
- 2024ApJ...966..132K: The fiducial models are not formally acceptable fits (reduced chi^2 above the 0.05 significance threshold)
- 2024ApJ...966..132K: The most appropriate alpha_CO for ETGs is unknown, so gas masses are approximations
- 2016ApJ...822L..28B: Mild systematic model-data mismatch; distance uncertainty not in error budget; S/N limiting; minor-axis kinematics unresolved by factor of two
- 2023ApJ...944L..18M: Interpolating a 1D rotation curve to estimate V_c can mis-estimate circular velocity where strong non-circular motions are present (such as in the barred regions of NGC 1365 and NGC 7496), leading to…
- 2023ApJ...944L..18M: In IC 5332, the 7m+tp moment-2 map at 1.5 kpc resolution contains unresolved bulk motion, requiring the use of a single minimum velocity dispersion (1.25 km s^-1) as an upper bound across the galaxy.
- 2023ApJ...944L..18M: With the chosen FilFinder parameters, many visibly long filaments are segmented, making the masks suitable for measuring spacings rather than individual filament lengths or geometries.
- 2023ApJ...944L..18M: The 1D azimuthal/radial spacing measurement ignores the covariance of radial and azimuthal spacings and does not measure spacings between spiral arm spurs.
- 2023ApJ...944L..18M: Omitting atomic gas and the dark matter halo from the background potential in the hydrostatic equilibrium equation may lead to an overestimation of the gas scale height and Jeans length.
- 2023ApJ...944L..18M: Lack of precise observational constraints on atomic gas surface density and velocity dispersion for NGC 1365 and NGC 7496 prevents a quantitative assessment of stability including atomic gas in those…
- 2017ApJ...849...91M: High-z sample biased to high luminosities
- 2016ApJ...816...37V: Inclination and peak rotation velocity cannot be independently determined at current resolution
- 2016ApJ...816...37V: FIR/TIR luminosities strongly model dependent; more photometry needed
- 2021ApJ...917...99Y: The high-resolution C43-8 ALMA configuration observations from Program 2018.1.00566.S were not yet completed.
- 2021ApJ...917...99Y: The inclination angle i of the host galaxy rotation axis remains unconstrained, leaving sin(i) dependencies in the dynamical mass, gas fraction, and SMBH-to-host mass ratio.
- 2021ApJ...917...99Y: The SIE + Sérsic lensing model is an over-simplification that leaves statistically significant residual clumps (4.9% of continuum peak, 8.1% of [C II] peak), and Table 1 uncertainties only include st…
- 2021ApJ...917...99Y: Simple image-plane inverse ray-tracing fails for multiply-imaged pixels inside the caustics, producing a likely artificial low-velocity component in the PV diagram, and suffers from beam smearing.
- 2013ApJ...773...44W: Limited resolution gives only constraints on size; morphology uncertain, large errors on minor axis and PA affect inclination and Mdyn.
- 2013ApJ...773...44W: [C II] may trace only the central starburst, underestimating bulge mass.
- 2025ApJ...995..150K: Only statistical errors are shown
- 2025ApJ...995..150K: Deeper dust continuum observations are needed to constrain dust-to-gas and dust-to-metal ratios
- 2025ApJ...995..150K: Higher-resolution ALMA data needed for the Himiko [CII] distribution and kinematics
- 2025ApJ...995..150K: Gas mass estimates have large uncertainty
- 2013ApJ...768L..38H: Beam too large to measure brightness temperature or resolve kinematics
- 2021MNRAS.503.1459B: The torus-to-SMBH mass ratio is fixed to M_torus/M_smbh = 0.02 rather than varied as a free parameter.
- 2021MNRAS.503.1459B: Simulations assume a dissipationless system and neglect cloud collisions and tidal shearing/merging.
- 2021MNRAS.503.1459B: Radiation pressure on cloud dynamics near the accretion disk is neglected.
- 2021MNRAS.503.1459B: Temperature distribution model uses a simple blackbody equilibrium approximation and neglects radiative transfer in a dusty medium within the torus.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: Ten-day ALMA duration and daily cadence cause windowing and aliasing in lag estimates.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: Negative JAVELIN lag partly depends on modelled rather than overlapping variability; response width unconstrained.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: PyROA lag results depend on initial parameters and are unstable.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: Candidate X-ray periods may reflect stochastic variability; the longer period is vulnerable to finite-duration and edge effects.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: Extended emission is too faint for spectral characterization.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: Diffuse SED components poorly constrained without comparable-resolution observations at other frequencies.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: Analytic synchrotron constraints have additional uncertainty because 100 GHz emission is not deeply optically thick.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: Bolometric correction is tentative because of large intrinsic dispersion.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: Direct scatter comparisons with lower-resolution campaigns are affected by non-variable extended mm emission.
- 2024A&A...690A.232S: The jet contribution and whether it varies remain open questions.
- 2026ApJ..1005...94N: The wide range of published distances to NGC 4061 (68–112 Mpc in NED) introduces a ~37% systematic uncertainty in M_BH, dominating the total error budget.
- 2026ApJ..1005...94N: Parameter degeneracies exist between M_BH and M/L_F814W, between y_cen and v_off (due to the beam size relative to the continuum peak), between inclination i and M/L_F814W, and between flux normaliza…
- 2026ApJ..1005...94N: Adopting the HST F555W band instead of F814W for the stellar mass model introduces an additional ~20% uncertainty in M_BH due to stronger dust attenuation in F555W.
- 2026ApJ..1005...94N: Direct event-horizon-scale imaging of the NGC 4061 SMBH (~0.4 uas) is impossible even with ngEHT (~10 uas resolution).
- 2019MNRAS.485.4359S: Central hole means no Keplerian rise observed; mass relies on stellar mass model
- 2019MNRAS.485.4359S: Model chi2 exceeds expectation, indicating unexplained features
- 2019MNRAS.485.4359S: Distance uncertainty (~10%) not included in the systematic error
- 2021A&A...654A..24B: Some flux may be lost due to high resolution and the lack of compact-array data
- 2021A&A...654A..24B: The conversion factor alpha_CO is highly uncertain
- 2021A&A...654A..24B: The 3DBAROLO fits for NGC 4845 velocity and dispersion are not optimal
- 2017ApJ...836....8T: M_dyn is uncertain: extended emission missed, inclination from marginally resolved sizes, beam smearing
- 2017ApJ...836....8T: Small sample limits interpretation
- 2017ApJ...845..138S: Inclination derived from only two successful rings; real uncertainty may be much larger
- 2021Sci...372.1201T: Spatial resolution (~0.2") is much larger than the black hole sphere of influence (0.02"), so the black hole mass and stellar bulge mass cannot be separated dynamically (allowing a black hole without…
- 2021Sci...372.1201T: Gas inflow into the galaxy center driven by spiral torques cannot be directly detected due to limited signal-to-noise ratio.
- 2021Sci...372.1201T: Pixel-to-pixel covariance matrix correction in the data cube was omitted because it is negligible compared to the sqrt(2N) log-likelihood scaling.
- 2013ApJ...770...13W: Source only marginally resolved; intrinsic size poorly constrained and inclination unknown
- 2013ApJ...770...13W: L_FIR uncertainties exclude dust temperature uncertainty
- 2025ApJ...989..160I: Small size, coarse MIRI resolution and contaminant prevent testing the two-component dusty starburst scenario
- 2025ApJ...989..160I: Shallow MIRI depth above 18 um prevents confirming the flattening
- 2025ApJ...989..160I: Depth estimates not corrected for aperture
- 2022MNRAS.514.6215Y: In-band spectral indices from the 12–18 GHz VLA data have large uncertainties due to the limited observing bandwidth and faintness of the two components.
- 2022MNRAS.514.6215Y: The ALMA 237 GHz size estimate of NGC 4395 may not be reliable because of the very limited image quality (SNR = 4.6).
- 2022MNRAS.514.6215Y: The broad-band spectral index estimate for component C uses non-simultaneous data with 10–50 per cent flux uncertainties, is only sensitive to large (>=30 per cent) variability, and may be biased by …
- 2022MNRAS.514.6215Y: Because of limited image sensitivity and near face-on disc geometry, no recognisable disc structure is seen in the radio maps.
- 2025A&A...701A..41D: Datasets are non-simultaneous over several years
- 2025A&A...701A..41D: One-zone homogeneous corona is most likely an oversimplification
- 2025A&A...701A..41D: MCG+08-11-11 corona SED poorly constrained so no robust fit
- 2025A&A...701A..41D: NGC 3227 corona properties only loosely constrained
- 2025MNRAS.537..520Z: Low S/N (~3 per synthesised beam per 20 km s^-1 channel) in the high-resolution cube impairs the direct constraint on inclination and prevents breaking the degeneracy between a PA warp and radial/non…
- 2025MNRAS.537..520Z: The relatively low inclination of the gas disc (38 deg) dominates the SMBH mass error budget (~55% of the uncertainty).

### 2021MNRAS.504.4123N: Black hole mass measurement using ALMA observations of [CI] and CO emissions in the Seyfert 1 galaxy NGC 7469 (2021; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Measure the supermassive black hole mass in the Seyfert 1 galaxy NGC 7469 using ALMA observations of [CI](1-0) and 12CO(1-0) gas kinematics, test whether [CI](1-0) is a better dynamical tracer than CO in AGNs, and constrain the broad-line …
- step 4: Fit 3D gas kinematics with KinMS and MCMC to measure M_BH
  - method: Simulate 3D gas cubes in KinMS using circular velocities from mge_circular_velocity (JAM package), radial PA profiles from Kinemetry, and a Gaussian radial gas surface brightness profile; fit 9 free parameters (log M_BH, M/L_F547M, sigma_g… [KinMS model: "The gas is assumed to move on circular orbits governed by a circular velocity curve, calculated from the `mge_circular_velocity` procedure within the Interactive Data Language (IDL) Jeans Anisotropic Modelling (cite: Cappellari08) package."] [KinMS model: "In practice, this is all accomplished by using the `Python` code `KINMSpy_MCMC`."]
  - software: KinMS, KINMSpy_MCMC, emcee, IDL, JAM (mge_circular_velocity)
  - parameter: Fit region = 60 x 60 pixels (6'' x 6'') and 60 velocity channels covering (-300, 300) km s^-1 (N = 60 x 60 x 60 constraints, P = 9 parameters); 3 x 10^5 MCMC iterations with 20% burn-in [Results of the default-mass model: "We performed the KinMS fit in an area of 60 pixels $\times$ 60 pixels ($6\ensuremath{''}\times6\ensuremath{''}\approx2\times2$ kpc$^2$)."] [Results of the default-mass model: "We limit the velocity channels ($-$300, 300) km s$^{-1}$ related to the systemic velocity of $\approx4850$ km s$^{-1}$ and $\approx4831$ km s$^{-1}$ for $^{12}$CO(1-0) and \[CI\](1-0), respectively."]
  - assumption: Gas moves on circular orbits in a dynamically thin, cold disc (asymmetric drift ignored); axisymmetric Gaussian gas surface brightness profile; D_A = 68.4 Mpc [KinMS model: "That means the dynamically asymmetric drift from turbulent velocity dispersion of the gas can be ignored (cite: Davis20)."] [KinMS model: "Here, we assumed the axisymmetric SBs for \[CI\](1-0) and $^{12}$CO(1-0) and modelled them using a Gaussian."]
  - fitting: Affine-invariant MCMC ensemble sampler (emcee); uniform priors in linear scale (log scale for M_BH); log-likelihood divided by sqrt(2N) (or rms multiplied by (2N)^(1/4)) to account for systematic/correlated noise [Results of the default-mass model: "The model started with an initial guess of parameters that are flat priors in linear scales, except for $M_{\rm BH}$ was in log-scale (to ensure efficient sampling of the posterior)."] [Results of the default-mass model: "Accordingly, in the Bayesian framework, we rescaled the model uncertainties by dividing the model $\log$ likelihood by $\sqrt{2N}$ or equivalently multiplying the `rms` by $(2N)^{1/4}$ (Table \[tab_alma\])."]
  - validation: Reduced chi-squared (1.94 for [CI](1-0), 1.91 for 12CO(1-0)), comparison of observed and model PVDs (with 0, lower, best-fit, and overly massive SMBHs), velocity residual maps (Data - Model), and posterior corner plots [Results of the default-mass model: "At $3\sigma$ CL, the best-fit model gives $M_{\rm BH}=1.78^{+2.69}_{-1.10}\times10^7$ M$_{\odot}$ and $M/L_{\rm F547M}=2.20^{+0.43}_{-0.40}$ (M$_{\odot}$/L$_\odot$) at $\chi^2_{\rm min,\;red}=1.94$."] [Results of the default-mass model: "To demonstrate how well the models describe the observables, we show the observed PVDs overlaid with the best-fit PVDs in Fig. 8 for \[CI\](1-0) and Fig. 9 for $^{12}$CO(1-0)."]
- step 5: Test systematic uncertainties in stellar and ISM mass models on M_BH
  - method: Re-run KinMS on [CI](1-0) kinematics allowing only M_BH and M/L_F547M (or mass-scaling factor Gamma) to vary while fixing other parameters to best-fit values, testing: (1) Bell & de Jong (2001) and Nguyen et al. (2019) colour-based MGE mas… [Other uncertainty sources on the M_BH e…: "To this end, we tested dynamical models with the atomic-\[CI\](1-0) kinematics and allowed only the $M_{\rm BH}$ and $M/L$$_{\rm F547M}$ changing as free parameters while fixed other parameters at their best-fitting values in Table \[fittable\]."] [The stellar-mass models account for spa…: "We converted the B01 and N19 mass-surface-density maps into their MGE forms with the central stellar mass in the centrally saturated spaxels interpolated from the outer pixels."]
  - software: KinMS, KINMSpy_MCMC, emcee, MgeFit
  - parameter: Search range for Gamma = (0.1, 2.0); X_CO = 1.9 x 10^20 cm^-2 (K km s^-1)^-1; X_[CI](1-0) = 2.1 x 10^20 cm^-2 (K km s^-1)^-1; sky background annulus (20-22)'' [(cite: Izumi20) CO-to- vs [CI]-to-H2 co…: "Based on dynamical modellings, I20 found the CO-to- and \[CI\]-to-H$_2$ conversion factor of $X_{\rm CO}=1.9\times10^{20}$ cm$^{-2}$ (K km s$^{-1}$)$^{-1}$ and $X_{\rm [CI](1-0)}=2.1\times10^{20}$ cm$^{-2}$ (K km s$^{-1}$)$^{-1}$, respectively"] [Table 4: "The search range for $\Gamma$ was set within (0.1–2.0), while the search ranges of $\log(M_{\rm BH}/{\rm M}_\odot)$ and $M/L$$_{\rm F547M}$ were kept the same as in Table \[fittable\]."]
  - assumption: Bell & de Jong (2001) and Nguyen et al. (2019) colour-M/L relations; central saturated pixels in F814W interpolated from outer pixels [The stellar-mass models account for spa…: "We converted the B01 and N19 mass-surface-density maps into their MGE forms with the central stellar mass in the centrally saturated spaxels interpolated from the outer pixels."]
  - fitting: 2-parameter MCMC fit in KinMS (log M_BH and M/L_F547M or Gamma) with other parameters fixed [Other uncertainty sources on the M_BH e…: "To this end, we tested dynamical models with the atomic-\[CI\](1-0) kinematics and allowed only the $M_{\rm BH}$ and $M/L$$_{\rm F547M}$ changing as free parameters while fixed other parameters at their best-fitting values in Table \[fittable\]."]
  - validation: All test M_BH values remain consistent within the 2-3 sigma uncertainties of the default [CI](1-0) model (Table 4) [(cite: Izumi20) CO-to- vs [CI]-to-H2 co…: "suggesting these adopted conversion factors have a significant impact on the derived $M_{\rm BH}$ but within $3\sigma$ uncertainties of our best-fit models in Section 4.3 and Table \[fittable\])."]
- step 6: Constrain the BLR virial factor f and inclination angle i
  - method: Combine the [CI](1-0) dynamical M_BH with reverberation-mapping R_BLR and Delta V measurements using the virial equation M_BH = f * R_BLR * (Delta V)^2 / G to solve for f, then derive the BLR inclination angle i using the planar thick-disc… [Constraining the BLR inclination angle: "Given our dynamical $M_{\rm BH}$ constraint of $1.78\times10^7$M$_{\odot}$ and its $1\sigma$ uncertainty (Table \[fittable\]) and account for the consistency with $1\sigma$ errors of $R_{\rm BLR}$ and $\Delta V$ (cite: Peterson14), we find $f=7.2^{+4.2}_{-3.4}$ for the BLR in NGC 7469 (replacing $\langle f \rangle$ by…"] [Constraining the BLR inclination angle: "We use this specific $f$ to infer the geometry of the BLR based on its analytical expression of a planar BLR of a thick disc, which is given by the expression:$f=\Bigg[4\Bigg(\sin^2i+\Big(\frac{h}{R_{\rm BLR}}\Big)^2\Bigg)\Bigg]^{-1}$"]
  - parameter: M_BH = 1.78 x 10^7 M_sun; h/R_BLR ~ 0 [Constraining the BLR inclination angle: "We thus consider the thin disc BLR model for the NGC 7469 AGN in which $(h/R_{\rm BLR})\approx0$, giving the BLR’s LOS inclination of $i\approx11.0^\circ$$_{-2.5}^{+2.2}$"]
  - assumption: Virial equilibrium in the BLR; geometrically thin planar BLR disc with (h/R_BLR) ~ 0 [Abstract: "The gas within the unresolved BLR thus has a Keplerian virial velocity component and the inclination of $i\approx11.0^\circ$$_{-2.5}^{+2.2}$, confirming its face-on orientation in a Seyfert 1 AGN by assuming a geometrically thin BLR model."]
  - fitting: Analytical calculation propagating 1-sigma uncertainties [Constraining the BLR inclination angle: "Given our dynamical $M_{\rm BH}$ constraint of $1.78\times10^7$M$_{\odot}$ and its $1\sigma$ uncertainty (Table \[fittable\]) and account for the consistency with $1\sigma$ errors of $R_{\rm BLR}$ and $\Delta V$ (cite: Peterson14), we find $f=7.2^{+4.2}_{-3.4}$ for the BLR in NGC 7469"]
  - validation: Comparison with canonical average <f> = 4.31 +- 1.05 and empirical M_BH-galaxy scaling relations [Constraining the BLR inclination angle: "All of these properties were subsumed into the average dimensionless scaling factor for RM AGNs: $\langle f \rangle=4.31\pm1.05$ (cite: Grier13a)."]
- result: Black hole mass M_BH from [CI](1-0) kinematics (default model) = 1.78e7 ± +2.69e7 / -1.10e7 (3 sigma) M_sun [Abstract: "Our dynamical model using the ${\rm [CI]}$(1-0) kinematics yields a $M_{\rm BH}=1.78^{+2.69}_{-1.10}\times10^7$ M$_{\odot}$"]

- limitation (authors): The ALMA synthesised beam (~0.3'' or ~100 pc) is ~30 times larger than the black hole sphere of influence r_SOI (~3 pc or ~0.01''), making this the lowest relative angular resolution M_BH measurement and susceptible to stellar-mass systema… [Results of the default-mass model: "Given $M_{\rm BH}\approx1.8\times10^7$ M$_{\odot}$ and $\sigma_\star\approx150$ km s$^{-1}$ (cite: Onken04) gives $r_{\rm SOI}\equiv GM_{\rm BH}/\sigma_\star^2\approx3$ pc ($\approx0.01\ensuremath{''}$), a factor of $\approx30$ times smaller than what our ALMA observations can resolve."]

### 2025ApJ...995..108J: Molecular Gas in Major Mergers Hosting Dual and Single AGNs at &lt;10 kpc Nuclear Separations (2025; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Measure molecular gas within SMBH spheres of influence, separate rotating and non-rotating gas, relate it to SMBH growth, and compare single and dual AGN.
- step 2: Define SMBH masses and aperture bounds
  - method: Combine literature SMBH masses with MUSE Ca II stellar dispersions; apply the MBH–σ* relation and derive lower- and upper-bound SoI radii. [Black Hole Masses & Sphere of Influences: "Masses of the central SMBHs were obtained from the literature where available. This is the case for NGC 6240, UGC 4211, and Mrk 739."] [Black Hole Masses & Sphere of Influences: "We then measured $\sigma_*$ using the Ca II triplet absorption lines in the rest-frame 8350-8730Å range, following the method outlined by (cite: koss22)."]
  - software: MUSE instrument pipeline
  - parameter: log(MBH/M⊙) = 4.38 log(σ*/200 km s−1) + 8.49. [Black Hole Masses & Sphere of Influences: "\text{log}\left(M_{\text{BH}}/M_\odot\right) = 4.38 \times \text{log}\left(\frac{\sigma_*}{200 \text{ km s$^{-1}$}}\right)+8.49,"]
  - parameter: Lower-bound rSoI = 33 pc (σ*/200 km s−1)^2.38; upper-bound approximately four times larger. [Black Hole Masses & Sphere of Influences: "r_{\text{SoI}}=33 \textrm{pc} \left(\frac{\sigma_*}{200 \textrm{ km s}^{-1}}\right)^{2.38}."] [Black Hole Masses & Sphere of Influences: "Based on the (cite: medling15) results for NGC 6240, we apply a factor of $\sim4$ to the lower-bound ($M_{\text{BH}}-\sigma_*$ SoI) to obtain our upper-bound SoI estimate."]
  - assumption: Virialized, isotropic and spherically symmetric gas motions. [Black Hole Masses & Sphere of Influences: "If we assume that the nuclei have virialized, isotropic, and spherically symmetric gas motions"]
  - validation: Repeat the analysis at both SoI extrema to address methodology and definition uncertainty. [Black Hole Masses & Sphere of Influences: "To account for this uncertainty, we define upper- and lower-bound estimates of $r_{\text{SoI}}$ and conduct the same analysis at both extrema."]
- step 7: Derive AGN luminosities and Eddington ratios
  - method: Use published bolometric luminosities or convert intrinsic 2–10 keV luminosities; for ESO 253-G003 infer X-rays from ∼100 GHz continuum; divide bolometric by Eddington luminosity. [Bolometric Luminosities: "For Mrk 739 and UGC 4211, AGN bolometric luminosities of each nucleus were reported in (cite: koss11) and (cite: koss23)."] [Bolometric Luminosities: "For the dual AGN system ESO 253-G003, we estimate intrinsic 2$-$10 keV X-ray luminosities from the ALMA continuum luminosities at $\sim$100 GHz (Droguett et al., in prep)."]
  - parameter: κ2–10 = 20. [Bolometric Luminosities: "We once again assume a bolometric correction of $\kappa_{2-10} = 20$"]
  - parameter: LEdd = 1.5×10^46 erg s−1 × MBH/(10^8 M⊙). [Eddington Ratios: "L_{\text{Edd}} = 1.5 \times 10^{46} \text{ erg s}^{-1} \frac{M_{\text{BH}}}{10^8 M_\odot}."]
  - assumption: Solar metallicity for LEdd; AGN coronal synchrotron dominates ESO 253-G003 ∼100 GHz continuum. [Eddington Ratios: "This definition is in accordance with (cite: Koss2022b) and assumes solar metallicity."] [Bolometric Luminosities: "At our $<$100 pc resolution, self-absorbed synchrotron emission from the AGN corona dominates the $\sim$100 GHz continuum"]
  - validation: Check consistency with luminosity-dependent bolometric corrections over the sample luminosity range. [Bolometric Luminosities: "While other studies have reported a luminosity dependence of the bolometric correction (cite: Duras2020), in the luminosity range covered by our sample"] [Bolometric Luminosities: "we obtain similar and consistent values for the bolometric correction."]
- step 9: Estimate potential short-term SMBH growth
  - method: Express non-rotating SoI gas as a potential increase in SMBH mass, using resolved measurements at each SoI bound. [Feeding the Supermassive Black Holes: "we can roughly estimate the short-term increase in $M_{\text{BH}}$ for each AGN."] [Feeding the Supermassive Black Holes: "These ranges were derived from systems with resolved SoI and do not include the gas mass upper limits presented in Table [6](#tab:mol_SOI)."]
  - parameter: αCO = 0.6 M⊙ (K km s−1 pc−2)−1. [Feeding the Supermassive Black Holes: "At the SoI lower-bound, assuming $\alpha_{\text{CO}}=0.6\,M_\odot$ (K km s$^{-1}$ pc$^{-2}$)$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: All non-rotating molecular gas within the SoI is eventually accreted. [Feeding the Supermassive Black Holes: "if we assume all non-rotating molecular gas within the SMBH SoI (see Table [6](#tab:mol_SOI)) to be accreted onto the SMBH eventually"]
  - validation: Repeat the upper-bound growth estimate omitting the NGC 6240S outlier. [Feeding the Supermassive Black Holes: "Omitting this outlier, the expected SMBH growth at upper-bounds range from a $\sim0-23\%$ mass increase, with the median remaining at 3%."]
- result: Median potential SMBH mass increase, lower-bound SoI = ∼1 ± not stated % [Feeding the Supermassive Black Holes: "these estimated mass increases range from $0-14$% and have a median of $\sim$1%."]
- result: Median potential SMBH mass increase, upper-bound SoI = 3 ± not stated % [Feeding the Supermassive Black Holes: "At the SoI upper-bound, the SMBHs are expected to grow by $\sim0-95\%$ with a median of 3%."]
- result: Potential SMBH mass increase range, upper-bound SoI excluding NGC 6240S = ∼0–23 ± not stated % [Feeding the Supermassive Black Holes: "Omitting this outlier, the expected SMBH growth at upper-bounds range from a $\sim0-23\%$ mass increase, with the median remaining at 3%."]
- limitation (authors): Only two single-AGN systems provide an incomplete, preliminary comparison sample. [Sample Selection: "Though not a complete comparison sample, the inclusion of these two single AGN will serve as a preliminary point of comparison"]
- limitation (authors): SMBH masses and SoI radii depend on methodology; merger systems may depart from the MBH–σ* relation. [Black Hole Masses & Sphere of Influences: "SMBH mass measurements can be highly uncertain, and vary substantially based on the method followed"]
- limitation (authors): Most lower-bound SoIs are unresolved, limiting correlation constraints. [Gas inside each SMBH’s sphere of influe…: "the SoI lower-bound is unresolved in a high fraction of sources, resulting in the molecular gas measurements being dominated by upper-limits."]
- not stated (card): Exact line-free channel selections for uvcontsub for each execution block and target.; Cycle 9 continuum polynomial order.; tclean cell size, image dimensions, deconvolver, masks, cleaning thresholds and iteration limits.; Primary-beam correction and any uv taper.; NGC 6240 calibration and imaging settings used for the archival cubes.; KinMS, KinMS_fitter and skySampler versions or commits.; Exac…

### 2026MNRAS.548ag546D: WISDOM project - XXVIII. Molecular gas measurement of the supermassive black hole mass of the galaxy NGC 1387 (2026; nearby_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Measure the supermassive black hole (SMBH) mass and stellar mass-to-light ratio of the early-type galaxy NGC 1387 using ALMA $^{12}$CO(2–1) molecular gas kinematics.
- step 4: Fit low-resolution CO data cube to constrain galaxy inclination prior
  - method: Forward model the low-resolution CO data cube using KINMS and GASTIMATOR (via KINMS_FITTER) with an analytical truncated exponential gas surface brightness profile, then smooth the inclination posterior with SCIKIT-LEARN KDE. [Low-resolution cube and inclination pri…: "Second, due to the lower resolution, the molecular gas disc appears smoother and more regular, allowing to model it assuming an infinitely-thin axisymmetric disc with an exponential radial surface brightness profile."] [Low-resolution cube and inclination pri…: "The MCMC chain in that first stage modelling had $200,000$ steps, yielding a best-fitting inclination $i=13.6\degree^{+0.9}_{-1.2}$."]
  - software: KINMS, GASTIMATOR, KINMS_FITTER, JAMPY, SCIKIT-LEARN
  - parameter: 11 free parameters (M_BH, M/L, R_0, R_hole, integrated flux, sigma_gas, PA, i, V_sys, RA offset, Dec offset), 200,000 MCMC steps (~10% burn-in), N = 188,777 constraints, uncertainty rescaling factor (2N)^0.25, KDE bandwidth = 0.07 [Low-resolution cube and inclination pri…: "This model has a total of $11$ free parameters: SMBH mass ($M_\text{BH}$), spatially-constant stellar mass-to-light ratio ($M/L$), molecular gas disc scale length ($R_0$), truncation radius ($R_{\text{hole}}$), integrated flux density and spatially-constant velocity dispersion ($\sigma_\text{gas}$), as well as the "nu…"] [Low-resolution cube and inclination pri…: "The MCMC chain in that first stage modelling had $200,000$ steps, yielding a best-fitting inclination $i=13.6\degree^{+0.9}_{-1.2}$."]
  - assumption: Infinitely-thin axisymmetric disc with an inner-truncated exponential radial surface brightness profile [Low-resolution cube and inclination pri…: "Second, due to the lower resolution, the molecular gas disc appears smoother and more regular, allowing to model it assuming an infinitely-thin axisymmetric disc with an exponential radial surface brightness profile."] [Low-resolution cube and inclination pri…: "Due to the small central depression in both the higher- and the lower-resolution data cube, we also allowed for the possibility of an inner truncation in the radial surface brightness profile."]
  - fitting: Bayesian MCMC using Gibbs sampler with adaptive stepping (GASTIMATOR), log-likelihood ln P proportional to -0.5 chi^2 with pixel uncertainties rescaled by (2N)^0.25; uniform priors (log-uniform for M_BH) [Modelling method: "The KINMS model and ALMA data cubes are then compared in a Bayesian way using the general Gibbs sampler with adaptive stepping GASTIMATOR[^6]."] [Low-resolution cube and inclination pri…: "The priors of all the parameters are uniform, apart from that of the SMBH mass which is uniform in the logarithm due to its large dynamic range."]
  - validation: Check 1D marginalisation histograms for a well-defined central peak and tapering edges indicating MCMC convergence [Low-resolution cube and inclination pri…: "The convergence of the chain is indicated by the fact that both histograms have a well-defined large central peak which tapers out towards the edges."]
- step 5: Fit high-resolution CO data cube using SKYSAMPLER to measure SMBH mass and stellar M/L
  - method: Sample the deconvolved clean components with SKYSAMPLER (5x10^6 particles), deproject with transformClouds, and forward model the high-resolution data cube with KINMS and GASTIMATOR using the KDE inclination prior and fixed continuum centr… [Molecular gas distribution: "SKYSAMPLER first obtains the kinematics-independent intrinsic distribution of the gas by integrating the point source model (i.e. the clean components yielded by the CASA task `tclean`) of the gas distribution along the velocity axis. This distribution is then sampled with $5\times10^6$ particles."] [Results: "Our final results are generated by comparing the high-resolution data cube described in Section [3.2](#section:highres data cube) to a KINMS model created using the stellar potential described in Section [4.2](#subsection:stellar potential) and SKYSAMPLER, together with the inclination prior derived in Section [4.4](#…"]
  - software: KINMS, SKYSAMPLER, GASTIMATOR, KINMS_FITTER, JAMPY
  - parameter: 5x10^6 particles, 7 free parameters (log M_BH [7, 11], M/L [0.0, 5.0], sigma_gas [0.0, 50.0], integrated intensity [0, 300], inclination [11.5, 20.0] with KDE prior, PA [0.0, 359.9], V_sys [1100, 1400]), centre fixed to 03h36m57.0335s -35d… [Results: "This model has a total of $7$ free parameters: SMBH mass, spatially-constant stellar mass-to-light ratio, molecular gas disc integrated flux density and spatially-constant molecular gas velocity dispersion, and the nuisance parameters position angle, inclination and systemic velocity."] [Results: "*Note.* The centre position is fixed to that of the unresolved continuum source, $03^{\text{h}}36^{\text{m}}57\rlap{.}^\text{s}0335$, $-35\degree 30\ensuremath{'} 23\rlap{.}''682$ (J2000.0)."]
  - assumption: Flat axisymmetric rotating gas disc, spatially-constant gas velocity dispersion, spatially-constant stellar M/L, kinematic centre coincident with unresolved continuum source, distance = 19.3 Mpc [Modelling method: "However, the dominant sources of uncertainties are in fact systematic, such as our assumption of a perfectly flat axisymmetric disc or our density deprojection."] [Results: "We have opted to fix the centre position that of the detected continuum emission, as the latter is consistent with the kinematic centre determined in the previous modelling step (low-resolution data cube) and the reduction of the model dimensionality leads to a decrease of the computing time."]
  - fitting: Bayesian MCMC (GASTIMATOR, 150,000 steps) with KDE prior on inclination, log-uniform prior on M_BH, uniform priors on remaining parameters, and pixel uncertainties rescaled by (2N)^0.25 [Results: "The SMBH mass prior is again uniform in the logarithm, the inclination prior is set by the previous low-resolution data cube modelling (see Section [4.4](#subsection: inclination prior)), and all remaining parameters again have uniform priors."] [Results: "The final MCMC chain in this second stage of our modelling had $150,000$ steps."]
  - validation: Comparison of PVD overlays and reduced chi^2 between best-fitting SMBH model (chi^2_red = 1.117), no-SMBH model (chi^2_red = 1.134), and overly-massive SMBH model (chi^2_red = 1.125) [Results: "The model with the best-fitting SMBH has $\chi^2_\text{red}=1.117$, whereas the no SMBH model has $\chi^2_\text{red}=1.134$ (see Table [4](#table:chi2 values))."] [Table 4: "| Model with over-massive SMBH | $1.125$ | | |"]
- step 6: Quantify systematic uncertainties on SMBH mass and stellar M/L
  - method: Run alternative dynamical models with (1) a dust-corrected F160W MGE derived using HST F110W-F160W colour excess, (2) fixed MGE axial ratios q' in [0.94, 1.00], and (3) a radially linearly-varying stellar M/L profile. [Systematic uncertainties: "For this we use a WFC3 F110W-filter ($J$-band) image alongside the F160W-filter ($H$-band) image."] [Systematic uncertainties: "To investigate the effect of our choice of fixed axial ratio on the final best-fitting parameters, we generated a series of best-fitting dynamical models whose MGE models had different fixed axial ratios in the range $0.94\leq q'\leq1.00$."]
  - software: MGEFIT, JAMPY, KINMS, GASTIMATOR, KINMS_FITTER, SKYSAMPLER
  - parameter: Intrinsic colour (J-H)_int = 0.75 (Vega), Galactic extinction curve R_V = 3.1; axial ratios q' = 0.94, 0.96, 0.98, 1.00; linear M/L radial gradient [Systematic uncertainties: "We then examine the outskirts of this image, away from the dustiest regions, to estimate the intrinsic $J-H$ colour of the galaxy, $(J-H)_\text{int}=0.75$."] [Systematic uncertainties: "This is then converted into an $H$-band extinction $A_H$ map by dividing it by the ratio of the total extinctions obtained from the standard Galactic extinction curve with a total-to-selective extinction ratio $R_V=3.1$ (cite: Fitzpatrick_1999), leading to an average $A_H$ of $0.14$ mag with a peak of $0.26$ mag."]
  - assumption: Galactic extinction law with R_V = 3.1; systematic uncertainty bounds defined by maximum upper and minimum lower deviations among all tested model variants [Systematic uncertainties: "For each parameter we adopt the maximum upper bound and minimum lower bound obtained among all model variants, therefore representing the largest deviations from the fiducial model permitted by the explored systematic tests."]
  - fitting: Same Bayesian MCMC framework as fiducial model for each systematic variant [Systematic uncertainties: "Lastly, this dust-corrected image is used to generate a new MGE model as in Section [4.2](#subsection:stellar potential), and in turn a new dynamical model."]
  - validation: Compare reduced chi^2 across variants (1.116 for dust correction, 1.108 for linearly-varying M/L) and verify consistency within 3-sigma statistical uncertainties [Table 4: "| Model with dust correction | $1.116$ | | |"] [Table 4: "| Model with linearly-varying $M/L$ | $1.108$ | | |"]
- result: SMBH mass (M_BH) = 1.10e8 ± +1.71e8/-0.95e8 [stat, 3sigma] +2.45e8/-1.09e8 [sys] M_odot [Abstract: "We infer a SMBH mass of $1.10^{+1.71}_{-0.95}[\text{stat},3\sigma]^{+2.45}_{-1.09}[\text{sys}]\times 10^8$ M$_\odot$ and a F160W-filter stellar mass-to-light ratio of $0.90^{+0.44}_{-0.35}[\text{stat}, 3\sigma]^{+0.46}_{-0.36}[\text{sys}]$ M$_\odot$/L$_{\odot,\text{F160W}}$."]
- result: Log SMBH mass log(M_BH/M_odot) = 8.04 ± -0.23/+0.18 (1sigma), -0.87/+0.41 (3sigma) dex [Results: "| $\log(M_\text{BH}/\text{M}_\odot)$ | $7$ | $\rightarrow$ | $11$ | $8.04$ | $-0.23,+0.18$ | $-0.87,+0.41$ |"]
- result: Stellar mass-to-light ratio (M/L_F160W) = 0.90 ± +0.44/-0.35 [stat, 3sigma] +0.46/-0.36 [sys] (-0.14/+0.16 at 1sigma) M_odot/L_odot,F160W [Abstract: "We infer a SMBH mass of $1.10^{+1.71}_{-0.95}[\text{stat},3\sigma]^{+2.45}_{-1.09}[\text{sys}]\times 10^8$ M$_\odot$ and a F160W-filter stellar mass-to-light ratio of $0.90^{+0.44}_{-0.35}[\text{stat}, 3\sigma]^{+0.46}_{-0.36}[\text{sys}]$ M$_\odot$/L$_{\odot,\text{F160W}}$."]
- result: Molecular gas velocity dispersion = 6.0 ± ±0.3 (1sigma), -0.8/+0.9 (3sigma) km s^-1 [Results: "| Velocity dispersion (km s$^{-1}$) | $0.0$ | $\rightarrow$ | $50.0$ | $6.0$ | $\pm0.3$ | $-0.8,+0.9$ |"]
- result: Molecular gas disc integrated intensity (model) = 264 ± ±9 (1sigma), -26/+25 (3sigma) Jy km s^-1 [Results: "| Integrated intensity (Jy km s$^{-1}$) | $0$ | $\rightarrow$ | $300$ | $264$ | $\pm9$ | $-26, +25$ |"]
- result: Inclination (fiducial high-res model) = 14.2 ± -1.1/+1.3 (1sigma), -2.6/+4.1 (3sigma) degree [Results: "| Inclination (degree) | $11.5$ | $\rightarrow$ | $20.0$ | $14.2$ | $-1.1, +1.3$ | $-2.6, +4.1$ |"]
- result: Position angle (fiducial high-res model) = 243.9 ± ±0.3 (1sigma), -1.0/+0.9 (3sigma) degree [Results: "| Position angle (degree) | $0.0$ | $\rightarrow$ | $359.9$ | $243.9$ | $\pm0.3$ | $-1.0, +0.9$ |"]
- result: Systemic velocity (fiducial high-res model) = 1267.2 ± ±0.3 (1sigma), -0.7/+0.8 (3sigma) km s^-1 [Results: "| Systemic velocity (km s$^{-1}$) | $1100$ | $\rightarrow$ | $1400$ | $1267.2$ | $\pm0.3$ | $-0.7, +0.8$ |"]
- result: Inclination (low-res cube fit) = 13.6 ± +0.9/-1.2 degree [Low-resolution cube and inclination pri…: "The MCMC chain in that first stage modelling had $200,000$ steps, yielding a best-fitting inclination $i=13.6\degree^{+0.9}_{-1.2}$."]
- result: SMBH sphere of influence radius (best-fit M_BH) = 16 (0.17) ± not stated pc (arcsec) [Best-fitting mass model: "Using the standard approximation of a SMBH SoI (see Section [2](#section:NGC1387)) and the best-fitting SMBH mass yields $R_\text{SoI}\approx16$ pc ($\approx0.17$ arcsec)."]
- result: Radius of equal mass contribution (R_eq) = 19 (0.20) ± not stated pc (arcsec) [Best-fitting mass model: "Figure [9](#fig:cummulative mass function) shows this radius of equal mass contribution to be $R_\text{eq}\approx19$ pc ($\approx0.20$ arcsec), slightly larger than $R_\text{SoI}$."]
- result: SMBH mass (dust-corrected MGE model) = 1.91e8 ± +1.64e8/-1.22e8 M_odot [Systematic uncertainties: "This leads to a best-fitting SMBH mass of $1.91^{+1.64}_{-1.22}\times10^8$ M$_\odot$ and a best-fitting $M/L$ of $0.86_{-0.26}^{+0.28}$ M$_\odot$/L$_{\odot,\text{F160W}}$, representing a $74$ per cent increase of the SMBH mass and a $5$ per cent decrease of the $M/L$ compared to our fiducial model."]
- result: SMBH mass (linearly-varying M/L model) = 4.3e7 ± +11.5e7/-4.2e7 M_odot [Systematic uncertainties: "Keeping all other parameters as before, a model with a radially linearly-varying $M/L$ yields a best-fitting SMBH mass of $4.3^{+11.5}_{-4.2}\times10^7$ M$_\odot$, a $61$ per cent decrease compared to out fiducial model, but nevertheless consistent with it within the $3\sigma$ statistical uncertainties reported in Tab…"]
- limitation (authors): A central depression (~0.2 arcsec / ~19 pc radius) in the molecular gas is similar in size to the SMBH sphere of influence, preventing a clear Keplerian velocity rise within R_SoI. [Line emission: "This depression is similar in extent to the predicted $R_\text{SoI}$, and thus prevents the innermost parts of the gravitational potential from being probed robustly. This is likely the reason why no clear Keplerian rise of the velocities is present within $R_\text{SoI}$."]
- limitation (authors): Deprojection of the 2D MGE surface brightness to 3D volume density is mathematically non-unique, and the degeneracy between inclination and dynamical mass is severe at the low inclination of NGC 1387. [Stellar potential: "The deprojection inferring the intrinsic density from the observed surface brightness is mathematically non unique and the degeneracy becomes particularly severe at low inclination (cite: Rybicki1987), as in our case."]
- limitation (authors): The heuristic rescaling of pixel uncertainties by (2N)^0.25 is not rigorously justified. [Modelling method: "Although not rigorously justified, this method has been adopted in several WISDOM papers and has been shown to yield sensible uncertainties consistent with those derived using a bootstrap approach to systematic effects in (cite: WISDOM_IV)."]
- not stated (card): Exact channel ranges or velocity intervals selected as line-free channels in the line spectral window for continuum imaging and uvcontsub.; How the cleaning masks ('regions containing emission') in tclean were constructed (e.g., auto-multithresh vs. interactive manual masking).; Exact relative visibility weights applied when combining the three 12-m tracks and one 7-m ACA track.; Exact HST observ…

### 2019ApJ...881...10B: A Precision Measurement of the Mass of the Black Hole in NGC 3258 from High-resolution ALMA Observations of Its Circumnuclear Disk (2019; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the mass of the supermassive black hole in NGC 3258, measured to high precision by fitting gas-dynamical models to high-resolution ALMA CO(2-1) kinematics, and how do dust extinction, disk warping and distance affect it?
- step 4: Fit flat-disk gas-dynamical models to the data cube
  - method: Forward model of thin flat disk in BH + extended mass potential, Gaussian line profiles, beam convolution on oversampled grid (s=4), chi^2 fit to the cube over block-averaged 5x5 pixel cells within r_fit=1'' [4.1.1 Method: "Models are optimized by $\chi^2$ minimization using a downhill simplex minimization method (cite: pre92) by fitting the calculated cubes directly to the ALMA data cube."]
  - software: IRAF STSDAS LUCY, custom modeling code
  - parameter: CO surface brightness map from 10 iterations of Richardson-Lucy deconvolution [4.1.1 Method: "applied ten iterations of the IRAF STSDAS Richardson-Lucy deconvolution (cite: ric72; luc74) task `LUCY` using the elliptical Gaussian synthesized beam."]
  - parameter: fit region 415 spatial pixels x 94 channels = 39010 data points; spectral range 2310-3273 km/s [4.1.1 Method: "The block-averaged fitting region used to compute $\chi^2$ contains 415 spatial pixels and 94 frequency channels, for a total of 39010 data points."]
  - assumption: spatially uniform M/L; gas disk mass not included; thin, cold disk with no asymmetric drift [4.1.1 Method: "We assume a spatially uniform $M/L$ ratio in our model calculations."]
  - fitting: chi^2 downhill simplex; at least nine free parameters (M_BH, Upsilon_H, x_c, y_c, i, Gamma, v_sys, sigma_turb, f_0); uniform or Gaussian radial sigma_turb [4.1.1 Method: "These basic models employ at least nine free parameters: the black hole mass $M_\mathrm{BH}$, the stellar $H$-band $M/L$ ratio $\Upsilon_H$"]
  - validation: oversampling factor tests (stable for s>=4); comparison with Cycle 2 fits [4.1.2 Flat disk model results: "we find that the best-fitting BH mass converges to stable values for $s\geq 4$"]
- step 7: Systematic error budget tests
  - method: Vary oversampling, block averaging, number of rings, fitting region, central CO hole, radial flow, turbulent pressure, distance (+-1 sigma), peculiar velocity; combine positive and negative terms in quadrature [4.5 Additional tests and error budget: "we separately combined in quadrature the positive and negative $\Delta \ensuremath{M_\mathrm{BH}}$ contributions listed above"]
  - parameter: distance D_L=35.8 and 28.0 Mpc for +-1 sigma [4.5 Additional tests and error budget: "(i.e., $D_L=35.8$ and 28.0 Mpc, corresponding to angular scales of 170 and 133 pc arcsec$^{-1}$, respectively)"]
  - assumption: M_BH proportional to adopted angular size distance [4.5 Additional tests and error budget: "the inferred BH mass should in principle be directly proportional to the assumed angular size distance"]
  - validation: each test refit with all free parameters [4.5 Additional tests and error budget: "After optimizing over all free parameters, we obtain best-fit BH masses that are $\Delta \ensuremath{M_\mathrm{BH}}= \pm 2.7\times10^8$ $M_\odot$ (or about 12%)"]
- result: M_BH range across dust-corrected MGE flat-disk models D1-D4 = (2.059-2.276)e9 ± not stated Msun [4.1.2 Flat disk model results: "The parameters for their best-fit model cubes converge to a range of values $\ensuremath{M_\mathrm{BH}}=(2.059-2.276)\times10^9$ $M_\odot$"]
- result: Mean sigma_turb/v_c = 0.030 ± not stated dimensionless [4.5 Additional tests and error budget: "a mean value $\langle\ensuremath{\sigma_\mathrm{turb}}/v_\mathrm{c}\rangle = 0.030$ averaged over the disk surface"]
- limitation (authors): Model not a formally acceptable fit (chi^2_nu=1.179); correlated noise; local irregularities in velocity/dispersion fields [4.4 Monte Carlo error analysis: "the final $\ensuremath{\chi^2_\nu}= 1.179$ for model F1 indicates that the model is not formally an acceptable fit to the data"]
- limitation (authors): Distance disagreements among estimates imply M_BH may lie in (2.0-3.8)e9 Msun [4.5 Additional tests and error budget: "the $M_\mathrm{BH}$ may plausibly lie in the range $(2.0-3.8)\times 10^9$ $M_\odot$ based on these other distance estimates"]
- not stated (card): Clean thresholds, mask definitions and multiscale scales for tclean; Line-free channel selection for uv continuum subtraction; Whether archival calibrated products or a re-run of the pipeline was used for Cycle 2 data; Details of the Cycle 2 data processing (in the earlier paper); Specifics of the fitting code (custom; not released) and starting guesses for the simplex; Ring-radius and v_ext init…

### 2021ApJ...908...19B: Black Hole Mass Measurements of Radio Galaxies NGC 315 and NGC 4261 Using ALMA CO Observations (2021; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Measure the black hole masses of the radio galaxies NGC 315 and NGC 4261 by forward-modeling ALMA CO(2-1) and CO(3-2) gas kinematics within the BH sphere of influence, with systematics from the stellar mass model (dust extinction) assessed
- step 2: Estimate nuclear dust extinction and build dust-corrected mass models
  - method: Embedded-screen model of an inclined thin dust disk compared with HST color excess maps; extinction-corrected central surface brightness from PSF-convolved Nuker fits, then re-fit as MGEs [Disk Extinction Modeling: "Our method assumes that a geometrically thin, inclined dust disk lies in the midplane of an oblate axisymmetric galaxy."] [Disk Extinction Modeling: "we corrected the central unmasked surface brightness values for extinction levels of $A_J=0.75$ and $1.50$ mag."]
  - software: GALFIT
  - parameter: NGC 315 dust disk i = 75 deg (from initial gas-dynamical models); NGC 4261 i = 64 deg [Disk Extinction Modeling: "for an inclination angle $i=75\ensuremath{^\circ}$ (inferred from our initial gas-dynamical models)"] [Disk Extinction Modeling: "Adopting $i=64\ensuremath{^\circ}$ from fits to the dust disk morphology (cite: ferrarese96)"]
  - parameter: NGC 315 Nuker fit: gamma=0.0, beta=1.48, r_b=1.42", alpha=1.54; corrected cases gamma=0.18 (A_J=0.75) and 0.33 (A_J=1.50) [Disk Extinction Modeling: "The best-fit model has inner and outer power-law slopes of $\gamma=0.0$ and $\beta=1.48$, respectively, with a break radius $\ensuremath{r_\mathrm{b}}=1.42\ensuremath{''}$ and a transition sharpness $\alpha=1.54$."] [Disk Extinction Modeling: "For the $A_J=0.75$ and $1.50$ mag cases, we found $\gamma=0.18$ and $0.33$, respectively."]
  - assumption: R_V = 3.1 [Disk Extinction Modeling: "Adopting a ratio of total-to-selective extinction of $R_V=3.1$ (cite: mathis90)"]
  - fitting: Nuker function (PSF-convolved) plus concentric PSF in GALFIT to the J-band mosaic for R<10" [Disk Extinction Modeling: "fitting the $J$-band mosaic for $R<10\ensuremath{''}$ using a PSF-convolved Nuker function (cite: lauer95) and a concentric PSF component in `GALFIT`"]
  - validation: Best-fit gamma for A_J = 1.50 mag lies at the core/power-law boundary of known galaxies [Disk Extinction Modeling: "The best-fit $\gamma$ for the $A_J=1.50$ mag case is at the boundary between those of core and power-law galaxies (cite: faber97)."]
- step 5: Fit thin-disk gas-dynamical models to the CO cubes for M_BH
  - method: Forward-model a thin flat rotating disk (BH plus MGE-derived stellar potential), populate a model cube with Gaussian line profiles, convolve with the beam, and fit the data cube directly [5.1 Method: "We used models of the CO gas rotation and intrinsic turbulent velocity dispersion ($\sigma_\mathrm{turb}$) to populate a model cube at each spatial location with Gaussian emergent line profiles."] [5.1 Method: "The models were fit directly to the ALMA CO data cubes, thereby allowing better characterization of model goodness-of-fit than if the fits were made to only moment maps."]
  - software: IRAF STSDAS lucy
  - parameter: Intrinsic flux map from ten iterations of Richardson-Lucy deconvolution of the Voronoi-binned zeroth moment map [5.1 Method: "We input the Voronoi-binned zeroth moment map and applied ten iterations of deconvolution, using the ALMA synthesized beam as the kernel."]
  - parameter: chi2 computed after 4x4 pixel block-averaging of data and model [5.1 Method: "we elected to compute the model goodness-of-fit after spatially block-averaging the data and model in $4\times 4$ pixel regions to create nearly beam-sized cells"]
  - parameter: NGC 315 fit region: semi-major axis 1.60", b/a = 0.26, PA 41 deg, 4400-5420 km/s, 10404 data points [NGC 315 Modeling Results: "The spatial fitting region has a semi-major axis of $\ensuremath{r_\mathrm{fit}}=1.60\ensuremath{''}$, an axis ratio of $b/a=0.26$, and a major-axis PA of $41\ensuremath{^\circ}$."]
  - parameter: NGC 4261 CO(2-1) fit region: r_fit = 1.00", b/a = 0.42, PA -20 deg, 1585-2805 km/s, 1178 data points [NGC 4261 Modeling Results: "with $\ensuremath{r_\mathrm{fit}}=1.00\ensuremath{''}$, an axis ratio of $b/a=0.42$, and a major-axis $\mathrm{PA}$ of $-20\ensuremath{^\circ}$"]
  - assumption: Thin, flat disk; gas self-gravity neglected in the primary models; sigma_turb either uniform, exponential or Gaussian in radius [Table 2 notes: "all of which treat the CO emission as arising from a thin, flat disk"] [5.1 Method: "we did not include the mass of the gas disk in the primary models A$-$E"]
  - fitting: chi2 minimization with a downhill simplex; 7-12 free parameters (M_BH, Upsilon, i, Gamma, sigma_turb parameters, kinematic center, v_sys, f0); no priors [5.1 Method: "Model parameters were optimized by $\chi^2$ minimization using a downhill simplex approach (cite: press92)."]
  - validation: Compare model first moment map and PVD with data; reduced chi2 of the fiducial fits (1.992 for NGC 315 B2; 1.263 for NGC 4261 C1); CO(3-2) cube fit as an independent check for NGC 4261 [NGC 315 Modeling Results: "the first moment map and PVD derived from the model B2 cube do match the observed CO kinematic behavior"] [NGC 4261 Modeling Results: "We investigated the stability of our CO(2$-$1) gas-dynamical modeling results by fitting the CO(3$-$2) data cube with thin-disk model E that is analogous to model C1."]
- step 7: Compare BH masses with scaling relations and resolve the sphere of influence
  - method: Compare measured M_BH with the M_BH-sigma, M_BH-L_bul and M_BH-M_bul relations; compute r_g as the radius where the BH and stellar contributions to v_c are equal [Resolving the BH Sphere of Influence: "we directly calculated $r_\mathrm{g}$ for models A$-$B3 by finding the radius where the various luminous mass contributions to $v_\mathrm{c}$ equal that from the BH"]
  - assumption: Colors J-K = 0.9 and H-K = 0.2 mag for an old, solar-metallicity SSP to convert to K-band bulge luminosity [BH Mass: "assumed colors of $J-K=0.9$ mag and $H-K=0.2$ mag for an old, solar metallicity SSP (cite: vazdekis10)"]
- result: NGC 315 model B2 reduced chi2 = 1.992 ± not stated dimensionless [NGC 315 Modeling Results: "Model B2 is not formally an acceptable fit with $\ensuremath{\chi^2}_\nu=1.992$"]
- result: NGC 315 J-band stellar M/L (model B2) = 1.87 ± 0.01 (stat, Table 3) Msun/Lsun [NGC 315 Modeling Results: "The best-fit $\ensuremath{\Upsilon_J}=1.87$ $M_\odot$/$L_\odot$is below the expected"]
- result: NGC 4261 model C1 chi2, dof and reduced chi2 = chi2 = 1476.7, N_dof = 1169, chi2_nu = 1.263 ± not stated dimensionless [NGC 4261 Modeling Results: "achieved $\ensuremath{\chi^2}=1476.7$ over $\ensuremath{N_\mathrm{dof}}=1169$ for $\ensuremath{\chi^2_\nu}=1.263$"]
- result: NGC 4261 H-band stellar M/L (model C1) = 1.62 ± +0.46/-0.40 (Table 3) Msun/Lsun [NGC 4261 Modeling Results: "The best-fit $\ensuremath{\Upsilon_H}=1.62$ $M_\odot$/$L_\odot$ is consistent with the expected"]
- result: NGC 4261 disk inclination (model C1) = 60.8 ± +2.8/-3.3 (Table 3) deg [NGC 4261 Modeling Results: "The best-fit $i=60.8\ensuremath{^\circ}$ is slightly lower than the value estimated by (cite: ferrarese96)"]
- result: NGC 4261 black hole mass from CO(3-2) (model E) = 1.47e9 ± not stated Msun [NGC 4261 Modeling Results: "Optimizing all remaining free parameters gives $\ensuremath{M_\mathrm{BH}}=1.47\times10^9$ $M_\odot$, $\ensuremath{\Upsilon_H}=2.18$ $M_\odot$/$L_\odot$, and $\sigma_1=35.6$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: NGC 4261 black hole mass with exponential sigma_turb (model D, i fixed) = 1.55e9 ± not stated Msun [NGC 4261 Modeling Results: "For model D, the best-fit BH mass decreases to $1.55\times10^9$ $M_\odot$ while $\Upsilon_H$ increases to 1.90 $M_\odot$/$L_\odot$."]
- result: NGC 315 uniform turbulent dispersion sigma_1 = ~15.3 ± +0.3/-0.2 (model B2, Table 3) km/s [NGC 315 Modeling Results: "All the NGC 315 models prefer a low $\sigma_1$ of $\sim$15.3 km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: NGC 4261 uniform turbulent dispersion sigma_1 (model C1) = 40.6 ± 6.1 (Table 3) km/s [NGC 4261 Modeling Results: "the model C1 best-fit $\sigma_1$ of $40.6$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: NGC 315 dust extinction at R~0.2" along the minor axis = A_V ~3-4 (A_J ~1-1.5) ± not stated mag [Disk Extinction Modeling: "suggests extinction values of $A_V\sim3-4$ mag ($A_J\sim1-1.5$ mag)"]
- result: NGC 4261 maximum dust extinction = A_H <~ 0.4 ± not stated mag [Disk Extinction Modeling: "The maximal observed color excesses are consistent with $A_H\lesssim0.4$ mag at all radii."]
- result: NGC 315 sphere of influence radius r_g = 0.67-0.80 ± not stated arcsec [Resolving the BH Sphere of Influence: "We find that $r_\mathrm{g}$ ranges between 0.67$''$$-$0.80$''$ with CO emission detected down to $\sim$(0.11$-$0.14)$r_\mathrm{g}$."]
- result: NGC 4261 sphere of influence radius r_g = 1.24-1.58 ± not stated arcsec [Resolving the BH Sphere of Influence: "models C1$-$E suggest $\ensuremath{r_\mathrm{g}}=1.24\ensuremath{''}-1.58\ensuremath{''}$"]
- result: NGC 315 M_BH predicted by the M_BH-sigma relation = 3.2-3.9e9 ± not stated Msun [BH Mass: "predicts BH masses in the range of $(3.2-3.9)\times 10^9$ $M_\odot$"]
- limitation (authors): The NGC 315 systematic error budget is provisional because no model is a formally acceptable fit [NGC 315 Modeling Results: "Our quoted systematic error budget for NGC 315 is provisional given that our models do not provide a formally acceptable fit to the data."]
- limitation (authors): The NGC 4261 S/N does not permit a more general disk structure; degeneracies among M_BH, i and sigma_turb remain [NGC 4261 Modeling Results: "The S/N of the NGC 4261 CO(2$-$1) data does not permit exploration of a more general disk structure"]
- limitation (authors): M_BH scales linearly with the assumed distance, which is uncertain for NGC 315 and not folded into the error budget [Introduction: "The derived $M_\mathrm{BH}$ scales linearly with the assumed distance, so any change to the distance will result in a commensurate shift in $M_\mathrm{BH}$."]
- not stated (card): Self-calibration solution intervals, number of rounds, reference antenna and which spectral windows were used for the continuum model; Line-free channels and polynomial order used for the uv continuum subtraction; tclean parameters: image size, niter, threshold, masks, multiscale or not, restoring beam handling, and CO(2-1)/CO(3-2) velocity frame and rest frequency for CO(3-2); Voronoi binning ta…

### 2019ApJ...883..193N: The ALMA Discovery of the Rotating Disk and Fast Outflow of Cold Molecular Gas in NGC 1275 (2019; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies, galaxy_clusters_and_sz; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the morphology and kinematics of the cold molecular gas in the inner kpc to ~30 pc of NGC 1275, how is the AGN fed, what do the blue-shifted absorption features imply, and what is the SMBH mass from gas dynamics?
- step 7: Fit gas disk kinematics for SMBH mass
  - method: KinMS models with circular velocity from MGE_circular_velocity, exponential disk; mpfit minimization then grid search over M_BH and inclination [5 Black Hole Mass: "We first searched for a set of parameters to minimize the difference between the observed data and the model by using `mpfit` that is developed in `IDL`."]
  - software: KinMS, mpfit, JAM, IDL
  - parameter: fitting area 1.55x1.55 arcsec, 4687-5580 km/s, 129x129x47 pixels, natural-weighted cube [5 Black Hole Mass: "thus $129\times129\times47$ pixels"]
  - assumption: axisymmetric potential, circular motion, exponential gas disk, uniform dispersion [5 Black Hole Mass: "assuming an axisymmetric potential and circular motion"]
  - fitting: chi2-like residual (obs-model)^2/rms^2; errors from residual < 9+chi2_min after rms scaled by (2N_dof)^(1/4) [5 Black Hole Mass: "we judge the error by allowing all parameters that realize residuals less than $9+\chi_{\rm min}^{2}$"]
  - validation: M/L varied 0.05, 0.5, 5.0; two gas masses; missing mass estimate 2e7 Msun [5 Black Hole Mass: "We confirm this by giving three different $M/L_{I}$ values, $0.05$, $0.50$ and $5.00$ to see negligible difference"]
- result: SMBH mass (gas 4e8 Msun) = 1.1e9 ± +0.3/-0.5 e9 Msun [5 Black Hole Mass: "$M_{\rm BH}=(1.1 ^{+0.3}_{-0.5})\times10^{9}M_{\ensuremath{\odot}}$ and inclination of $46^{+11}_{-6}$ deg"]
- result: SMBH mass (gas 5e7 Msun) = 1.2e9 ± +0.1/-0.6 e9 Msun [5 Black Hole Mass: "$M_{\rm BH}=(1.2^{+0.1}_{-0.6}) \times10^{9}M_{\ensuremath{\odot}}$ and inclination of $45^{+14}_{-5}$ deg"]
- limitation (authors): Missing flux from lack of short baselines, so masses are lower limits [4.1 Filaments: "Note that the estimated mass should be a lower limit since the total emissions cannot be recovered because of the lack of short interferometric baselines."]
- limitation (authors): Residual is not a true chi2 because neighbouring pixels are correlated; modelling assumptions simple [5 Black Hole Mass: "This residual is useful to judge the fitting results but is not equivalent to the $\chi^{2}$, as the neighboring pixels in the observed cube are correlated."]
- not stated (card): Image sizes, cell sizes, cleaning thresholds and masks for tclean; Line-free channel ranges for uvcontsub; Self-calibration solution parameters and reference model; Exact reduction of archival 2013.1.01102.S data; mpfit settings and grid spacing for the SMBH search; MGE fit parameters and Gaussian components; Target coordinates

### 2017ApJ...843L..35W: ALMA Observations of the Gravitational Lens SDP.9 (2017; high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the ALMA Band 6 continuum and CO(6-5) morphology of the lensed SMG SDP.9, what is the lens mass model, and can the central image be detected to constrain the SMBH mass?
- step 3: Fit lens light in HST
  - method: Two cored power-law profiles plus point source with linked centroids; then fix light and fit arcs for mass model [3 Lens Model: "The best fit comes from a combination two cored power-law profiles and a point source with their centroids linked"]
  - software: Glee
  - parameter: core radius uniform prior max 0.065 arcsec; SMBH mass lognormal prior 7.1-9.9 in log10 M [3 Lens Model: "The core radius is given a uniform prior with a maximum extent of 0.065$''$"]
  - assumption: Cored elliptical power-law mass with central point mass and external shear; Chabrier IMF [3 Lens Model: "We assume a cored elliptical power-law mass distribution with a central point mass (representing the SMBH) and external shear."]
  - fitting: Priors on structural parameters from HST fit used for ALMA fit [3 Lens Model: "we first fit to the extended arcs in the *HST* data and use the constraints on the structural parameters as priors"]
  - validation: Comparison with Dye+2014 model; also modeled with glafic with similar results [3 Lens Model: "We have also modeled the ALMA constraints with glafic (cite: oguri2010) and obtained similar results."]
- limitation (authors): ~50% of data flagged due to antenna problems [2.1 ALMA Data: "We note that $\sim$50 % of the data are flagged"]
- limitation (authors): Conjugate points cause degeneracies; position uncertainties overestimated [3 Lens Model: "The use of conjugate points leads to degeneracies in the lens model"]
- not stated (card): Line-free channels and channel range for uvcontsub; Image size and cell size; Clean mask and iteration/threshold details; Calibration pipeline vs manual and CASA calibration version; Locations of the conjugate points; Sampler and prior details for Glee fits; Which calibrator was bandpass; Velocity range used for integrated CO map

### 2021MNRAS.503.5583B: Resolving discs and mergers in z ∼ 2 heavily reddened quasars and their companion galaxies with ALMA (2021; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How are major physical processes governing massive galaxy formation (mergers, starbursts, black-hole growth, outflows) triggered during the dust-obscured quasar phase, and what effect do they have on the quasar host galaxies?
- step 5: Derive stellar masses, molecular gas fractions, and revised black hole masses
  - method: Combine dynamical masses with gas masses from Paper I to infer stellar masses via M_* = M_dyn - M_gas - M_BH and gas fractions M_gas / M_*; re-derive black hole masses using the Bongiorno et al. (2014) relation from Halpha FWHM and 2-10 ke… [Dynamical Masses, Sizes & Gas Fractions: "The gas fraction is traditionally defined as the ratio of the gas mass to the total stellar mass. In the absence of stellar mass estimates for our high-luminosity quasars, we infer the stellar masses from the dynamical masses via M$_\ast$=M$_{\rm{dyn}}-$M$_{\rm{gas}}-$M$_{\rm{BH}}$, as has been done in previous studie…"] [The M_BH-M_gal relation: "We then use the calibration in (cite: Bongiorno:14) to derive the new black-hole masses:"]
  - parameter: L_2-10keV = 1.1x10^45 erg/s (J1234) and 3.0x10^45 erg/s (J2315); gas masses from Paper I [The M_BH-M_gal relation: "These X-ray luminosities are L$_{\rm{2-10 keV}}=1.1\times10^{45}$ erg/s and $3.0\times10^{45}$ erg/s for J1234 and J2315 respectively."]
  - assumption: Disks are baryon-dominated out to 10 kpc; M_* = M_dyn - M_gas - M_BH [Dynamical Masses, Sizes & Gas Fractions: "It is reasonable to assume that the galaxy is baryon dominated even out to 10kpc, in which case there must be a substantial stellar component already in place with M$_\ast \sim 10^{11}$M$_\odot$ in this quasar."]
  - validation: Compared revised M_BH with previous optical-continuum-based estimates in Banerji et al. (2012, 2015), noting consistency within the ~0.5 dex systematic uncertainty. [The M_BH-M_gal relation: "The resulting black-hole masses for J1234 and J2315 are 4.1$\times$10$^9$M$_\odot$ and 2.8$\times$10$^9$M$_\odot$ respectively, which can be considered to be conservative estimates and are lower than the original black-hole masses presented in (cite: Banerji:12) and (cite: Banerji:15) for these quasars, although still…"]
- step 6: Compare M_BH-M_gal relation with Illustris simulations and track evolutionary slopes
  - method: Place J1234 and J2315 on the M_BH-M_gal plane alongside z ~ 4-6 quasars and Illustris z ~ 2 galaxies, and compute the mean evolutionary slope alpha in the log(M_BH)-log(M_*) plane for the 10 most massive black holes in Illustris from z = 6… [The M_BH-M_gal relation: "In Fig. [1](#fig:mbh_mdyn) we also show the total baryonic mass contained within 2$\times$ the stellar half mass radius, versus black hole mass for $z\sim2$ galaxies in the Illustris simulations (cite: Sijacki:15), which is described in more detail in the next Section."] [Comparison to Illustris: "Selecting the 10 most massive black holes in Illustris at $z1=2.5$ and tracking them forward to $z2=0.5$ results in a slope of $0.64 \pm 0.28$."]
  - parameter: 10 most massive black holes in Illustris-1 at z1 = 2.5 (tracked to z2 = 0.5) and at z1 = 6 (tracked to z2 = 2.5) [Comparison to Illustris: "Selecting the 10 most massive black holes in Illustris at $z1=2.5$ and tracking them forward to $z2=0.5$ results in a slope of $0.64 \pm 0.28$."] [Comparison to Illustris: "Selecting the 10 most massive black holes at $z1=6$ and tracking them forward to $z2=2.5$ results in a mean slope in the M$_{\rm{BH}}$-M$_{\rm{gal}}$ plane of $1.88 \pm 0.4$"]
  - assumption: Flat LambdaCDM cosmology with H0 = 70 km s^-1 Mpc^-1, Omega_M = 0.3, Omega_Lambda = 0.7 [Introduction: "Throughout the paper we assume a flat, $\Lambda$CDM cosmology with H$_0$=70 km s$^{-1}$ Mpc$^{-1}$, $\Omega_{\rm{M}}$=0.3 and $\Omega_{\Lambda}$=0.7."]
- result: Molecular gas fraction (ULASJ1234, extended exponential disk) = ~6 ± not stated % [Dynamical Masses, Sizes & Gas Fractions: "In the former case, we obtain gas fractions of $\sim$85% whereas the gas fraction relative to the extended disk model mass is more like $\sim$6%."]
- result: Molecular gas fraction (ULASJ1234, inner 3DBarolo) = ~85 ± not stated % [Dynamical Masses, Sizes & Gas Fractions: "In the former case, we obtain gas fractions of $\sim$85% whereas the gas fraction relative to the extended disk model mass is more like $\sim$6%."]
- result: Molecular gas fraction (G1234N) = 14-18 ± not stated % [Dynamical Masses, Sizes & Gas Fractions: "G1234N has a gas fraction of $\sim$14-18% whereas in G1234S the gas fraction is more uncertain owing to the discrepant gas masses from the CO and CI observations, and could be in the range 7-90%."]
- result: Molecular gas fraction (G1234S) = 7-90 ± not stated % [Dynamical Masses, Sizes & Gas Fractions: "G1234N has a gas fraction of $\sim$14-18% whereas in G1234S the gas fraction is more uncertain owing to the discrepant gas masses from the CO and CI observations, and could be in the range 7-90%."]
- result: Molecular gas fraction (J2315 system) = >45 ± not stated % [Dynamical Mass & Gas Fraction: "Combining this with the dynamical mass estimates above and estimating the stellar mass as M$_\ast$=M$_{\rm{dyn}}-$M$_{\rm{gas}}-$M$_{\rm{BH}}$ as was done in the case of J1234, we find very high gas fractions of $>$45%."]
- result: Black hole mass M_BH (ULASJ1234) = 4.1e9 ± 0.5 dex M_sun [The M_BH-M_gal relation: "The resulting black-hole masses for J1234 and J2315 are 4.1$\times$10$^9$M$_\odot$ and 2.8$\times$10$^9$M$_\odot$ respectively, which can be considered to be conservative estimates and are lower than the original black-hole masses presented in (cite: Banerji:12) and (cite: Banerji:15) for these quasars, although still…"]
- result: Black hole mass M_BH (ULASJ2315) = 2.8e9 ± 0.5 dex M_sun [The M_BH-M_gal relation: "The resulting black-hole masses for J1234 and J2315 are 4.1$\times$10$^9$M$_\odot$ and 2.8$\times$10$^9$M$_\odot$ respectively, which can be considered to be conservative estimates and are lower than the original black-hole masses presented in (cite: Banerji:12) and (cite: Banerji:15) for these quasars, although still…"]
- result: Mean evolutionary slope alpha in M_BH-M_gal plane (Illustris z1=2.5 to z2=0.5) = 0.64 ± 0.28 dimensionless [Comparison to Illustris: "Selecting the 10 most massive black holes in Illustris at $z1=2.5$ and tracking them forward to $z2=0.5$ results in a slope of $0.64 \pm 0.28$."]
- result: Mean evolutionary slope alpha in M_BH-M_gal plane (Illustris z1=6 to z2=2.5) = 1.88 ± 0.4 dimensionless [Comparison to Illustris: "Selecting the 10 most massive black holes at $z1=6$ and tracking them forward to $z2=2.5$ results in a mean slope in the M$_{\rm{BH}}$-M$_{\rm{gal}}$ plane of $1.88 \pm 0.4$, considerably steeper than the slope of the evolution from $z=2.5$ to $z=0.5$."]
- limitation (authors): Formal uncertainties on 3DBarolo dynamical masses do not include the significant uncertainty on galaxy inclination and are therefore likely under-estimated. [3DBarolo: "The uncertainties on the dynamical mass only include the output rotational model uncertainties from $^{\rm 3D}$Barolo ($\delta v_{\rm{rot, max}}$) and the width of the model ring ($\delta r_{\rm{max}}$) and the significant uncertainty on the inclination is not included."]
- limitation (authors): Considerable uncertainties exist in both dynamical and gas masses, as shown by discrepancies between 3DBarolo and exponential disk models and between CO and CI gas mass tracers. [Dynamical Masses, Sizes & Gas Fractions: "We caution however that there are considerable uncertainties in both the dynamical and gas masses as illustrated by the discrepancies in these estimates using different dynamical modelling methods and different gas mass tracers."]
- limitation (authors): Modest angular resolution of the ALMA observations (~0.8-1.0'') means that the smooth velocity gradient in J1234 could still be a merging system close to or soon after final coalescence, and individual velocity structures within the J2315 … [Dynamical Mass & Gas Fraction: "No velocity structure is evident in the individual galaxies at the current resolution, although we clearly see that the northern companion galaxy is blueshifted relative to the quasar host."]
- not stated (card): Exact frequency ranges or channel numbers used as line-free channels for uv-plane continuum subtraction and mfs continuum imaging; Stopping threshold, niter, and masking strategy used in CASA clean; Exact aperture diameters (within the stated 1-2'' range) used for extracting the 1D spectrum of each individual galaxy; Priors, number of walkers, and number of steps used in the emcee MCMC exponentia…

### 2015ApJ...811..115W: The Innermost Mass Distribution of the Gravitational Lens SDP.81 from ALMA Observations (2015; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; archival_reuse)
- question: Can the non-detection of the demagnified central image of lens SDP.81 in ALMA long-baseline CO maps constrain the lens galaxy's inner mass profile and SMBH mass?
- step 3: Fit lens mass models to image positions
  - method: Cored power-law (R_core 0.08'' or 0.15'' priors) and composite stellar+NFW models, plus SMBH point mass and external shear, using positions of three source features [3.3 Lens Mass Modeling: "We try two models for the mass distribution of the lens galaxy: a cored elliptical power-law mass distribution, and a composite model with separate stellar and dark matter components."]
  - software: Glee
  - parameter: MCMC chain length 5x10^5 [3.3 Lens Mass Modeling: "we first sample $L_{\rm pos}\times Q$ via MCMC of chain length $5\times10^5$, and then weight these samples by $L_{\rm flux}$"]
