  - assumption: SMBH at mass centroid; uniform prior on log M_BH [6.5, 9.5] [3.3.1 Cored Elliptical Power-Law Model: "We assume that the SMBH is located at the center of the mass distribution and adopt a uniform prior on its logarithmic mass"]
  - fitting: MCMC, position likelihood Gaussian [Lens Mass Modeling: "In practice, we first sample $L_{\rm pos}\times Q$ via MCMC of chain length $5\times10^5$, and then weight these samples by $L_{\rm flux}$."]
  - validation: rms image offset ~0.015'' for power-law models [4.1 Lens Modeling Results: "we can reproduce the observed image positions with an rms offset between the observed and predicted image positions of $\sim 0.015\ensuremath{''}$"]
- step 4: Constrain SMBH mass from central image non-detection
  - method: Weight samples by Gaussian non-detection likelihood on predicted flux of E = flux of D times mu_E/mu_D, for CO 5-4, 8-7, 10-9; mu ratio from mock three-clump source [3.3 Lens Mass Modeling: "predicted flux density of image E that is obtained by multiplying the observed integrated flux density of image D with the modeled magnification ratio $\mu_{E}/\mu_{D}$"]
  - software: Glee
  - validation: Results unaffected by 30-40% source size uncertainty and by satellite galaxy [3.3 Lens Mass Modeling: "Additional tests show that our results are unaffected by moderate ($\sim 30-40$%) uncertainties in the assumed source sizes."]
- result: Stellar M/L = 1.1 ± +0.1/-0.1 Msun/Lsun [4.1 Lens Modeling Results: "The predicted stellar mass-to-light (M/L) ratio is $1.1^{+0.1}_{-0.1}~\mathrm{M}_{\odot}~\mathrm{L}_{\odot}^{-1}$"]
- result: log(M_BH/Msun) preferred (R_core=0.15'') = >~8.5 ± not stated dex [abstract: "a black hole mass of $\mathrm{\log(M_{BH}/M_{\odot})} \gtrsim 8.5$ is preferred"]
- result: Dark matter fraction within Einstein radius = ~50 ± not stated percent [4.1 Lens Modeling Results: "The dark matter fraction within the Einstein radius is $\sim50$%"]
- limitation (authors): Fluxes of A, B, C not used owing to differential magnification (~30-50%) [3.1 Constraints From the Multiple Images: "we do not use the fluxes of these images as constraints due to the high uncertainty from differential magnification"]
- not stated (card): CLEAN parameters (weighting, robust, deconvolver, mask) for archival images; CO line velocity ranges and line-free channels for continuum subtraction; Exact archival image products used; Glee code availability and MCMC sampler details/burn-in; Rest frequencies of lines; How the E region and flux-extraction apertures were defined

### 2025PASJ...77..432U: ADF22-WEB: A giant barred spiral starburst galaxy in the z = 3.1 SSA22 protocluster core (2025; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How did the giant, rapidly rotating, barred spiral starburst ADF22.A1 (z=3.1, SSA22 proto-cluster core, obscured AGN) form its disk, bar and bulge, and how do its angular momentum and SMBH-bulge mass ratio compare with local galaxies?
- step 3: Measure integrated fluxes and fit the panchromatic SED
  - method: Elliptical-aperture NIRCam fluxes and curve-of-growth 870 um flux added to the existing X-ray-to-IR SED, refit with lightning to get SFR, M*, M_BH, Eddington ratio [Panchromatic SED Fit: "Total flux densities of ADF22.A1 in the NIRCam images were measured with an elliptical aperture"] [Panchromatic SED Fit: "We improve the fit by including the NIRCam and ALMA measurements."]
  - software: lightning
  - fitting: lightning SED fit simultaneously constraining star-formation heated dust and AGN dust emission; priors and sampler not stated [Panchromatic SED Fit: "This code allows us to simultaneously constrain emission from star-formation heated dust and AGN dust emission."]
  - validation: Estimates generally consistent with previous fits [Panchromatic SED Fit: "The estimates are generally consistent with the previous ones."]
- step 10: Compare SMBH-bulge ratio and predict descendant
  - method: Compare M_BH/M_*,total and M_BH/M_bulge with local spiral and elliptical relations; plot predicted z=0 descendant after converting gas to stars and losing 80% of j* [Co-evolution in the hidden early phase: "We investigated the relation between the SMBH mass and total stellar mass/bulge mass of ADF22.A1."] [Evolution into local massive ellipticals: "(ii) other process (additional inflow, mergers, outflows) does not affect the stellar mass of the descendant, and (iii) 80% of the specific stellar angular momentum is lost."]
  - assumption: Descendant: all molecular gas converted to stars; no other inflow, merger or outflow changes stellar mass; 80% of j* lost [Evolution into local massive ellipticals: "(ii) other process (additional inflow, mergers, outflows) does not affect the stellar mass of the descendant, and (iii) 80% of the specific stellar angular momentum is lost."]
- result: SFR (SED fit) = 610 ± +340/-270 Msun/yr [Panchromatic SED Fit: "The fit yields SFR=$610_{-270}^{+340}$ M$_\odot$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- result: log stellar mass = 11.4 ± +0.1/-0.2 log(Msun) [Panchromatic SED Fit: "log($M_*$/M$_\odot$)=11.4$^{+0.1}_{-0.2}$"]
- result: log SMBH mass = 8.5 ± +0.2/-0.3 log(Msun) [Panchromatic SED Fit: "log($M_{\rm BH}$/M$_\odot$)=8.5$^{+0.2}_{-0.3}$"]
- result: 870 um flux density = 15.5 ± 0.6 mJy [Panchromatic SED Fit: "We measure 870 $\mu$m flux density $S_{\rm 870}=15.5\pm0.6$ mJy based on a curve growth method."]
- limitation (authors): Stellar mass profile outside R~8 kpc is extrapolated for lack of sensitivity [Resolved stellar profile and specific s…: "is not sufficient to uncover the stellar mass surface distribution in outer parts due to the lack of sensitivity"]
- limitation (authors): CO(1-0) is not well resolved by the JVLA, so the Toomre Q rests on assumed profile [Molecular gas mass and Toomre-Q paramter: "The CO(1–0) emission is not well resolved with JVLA"]
- limitation (authors): Limited profile sampling prevents fitting a third (bar) component in the stellar profile [Resolved stellar profile and specific s…: "but the limited sampling of the profile prevent us from adding the third component."]
- not stated (card): Whether ALMA data were calibrated with the standard pipeline or manually, flux/bandpass/phase calibrators and any self-calibration for each project; Cell size, image size, niter/threshold, cleaning masks and deconvolver for all tclean runs; primary-beam correction; Continuum-subtraction method (e.g. uvcontsub order) and line-free channel ranges used for the [CII] cube; spectral setup of the Band …

### 2015ApJ...806...39O: A Measurement of the Black Hole Mass in NGC 1097 Using ALMA (2015; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the SMBH mass of NGC 1097 from dense molecular gas (HCN and HCO+ J=1-0) kinematics measured with ALMA?
- step 4: Chi-square grid fit of M_BH and M/L to PVD
  - method: Extract 106 points from observed PVD above 3 sigma ~8 mJy; grid M/L 4.80-5.35 (0.01), M_BH 0.5-2.5e8 (0.1e8); errors at Delta chi2<=9 [3.4: "the finer grid of model parameters is set to be from M/L$=4.80$ to 5.35 in steps of 0.01 and $M_{\rm BH}=0.5 \times 10^{8} M_{\odot}$ to $2.5 \times 10^{8} M_{\odot}$ in steps of $0.1 \times 10^{8} M_{\odot}$"]
  - parameter: kinematic PA 130±5 deg; velocity error = channel width 3.284 km/s plus Gaussian fit error [3.4: "is defined to be a sum in quadrature of the channel width (3.284 km s$^{-1}$) and the error from Gaussian fitting"]
  - fitting: chi-square grid, 2 free parameters, 104 dof, polynomial fit to Delta chi2 [3.4: "Note that the degree of freedom becomes 104, because we have two free parameters"]
  - validation: Repeated with HCO+, F190N profile, and inclination 51 deg [4.3: "We therefore repeat our method using the ${\rm HCO}^{+} (J=1-0)$ emission line."]
- result: SMBH mass (HCN, i=46 deg) = 1.40e8 ± +0.27/-0.32 e8 Msun [3.4: "the SMBH mass is thus estimated to be $1.40^{+0.27}_{-0.32} \times 10^{8} M_{\odot}$"]
- result: I-band M/L = 5.14 ± +0.03/-0.04 Msun/Lsun [3.4: "The M/L ratio is estimated to be $5.14^{+0.03}_{-0.04}$"]
- result: Minimum chi-square = 113 (reduced 1.09) ± not stated dimensionless [3.4: "The smallest chi-square value of 113 (reduced chi-square value is 1.09 when divided with the degree of freedom)"]
- result: SMBH mass (HCO+, i=46 deg) = 1.40e8 ± ±0.30e8 Msun [4.3: "estimate the SMBH mass to be $(1.40 \pm 0.30) \times 10^{8} M_{\odot}$"]
- result: SMBH mass at i=51 deg = 1.20e8 ± +0.35/-0.34 e8 Msun [4.2: "evaluate the SMBH mass to be $1.20^{+0.35}_{-0.34} \times 10^{8} M_{\odot}$"]
- limitation (authors): Inclination uncertainty and possible warp or asymmetry not modelled [4.2: "this galaxy could have a warped or a misaligned structure, which could be interesting to investigate but requires a calculation for an asymmetric potential field."]
- not stated (card): Continuum subtraction and line-free channels; Calibration details (calibrators, flux scale, pipeline vs manual); Image size and cell size, cleaning threshold, masking; KinMS parameters: gas disk radial profile, thickness, number of clouds; Details of MGE fit and HST image processing; Exact PVD extraction slit width; Spectral-line rest frequencies

### 2026MNRAS.546ag037E: Stellar-mass black holes on the millimetre Fundamental Plane of black hole accretion (2026; stellar_physics, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Whether the newly-discovered millimetre fundamental plane of black hole accretion holds for accreting stellar-mass black holes (X-ray binaries), and whether ADAF or compact jet models can explain the correlation for these sources.
- step 4: Fit the M_BH - L_nu,mm relation and the mm Fundamental Plane (mmFP)
  - method: Fit the combined SMBH + XRB sample (including new ACA data for Cygnus X-1 and archival 230 GHz data for GX 339-4) using LTS_LINEFIT and LTS_PLANEFIT, and evaluate Spearman rank correlation coefficients. [Results: "Following (cite: Ruffa24), we fitted the $M_{\rm BH}$ – $L_{\rm \nu,mm}$ and mmFP data, including the XRBs, using the LTS_LINEFIT and LTS_PLANEFIT routines of (cite: Cappellari2013), respectively."] [Results: "When performing these fits we only included the new ACA data for Cygnus X-1 and the archival data for GX 339-4 (due to its substantial flux variability in the ACA observations)."]
  - software: LTS_LINEFIT, LTS_PLANEFIT
  - fitting: Linear regression in log space using LTS_LINEFIT and LTS_PLANEFIT routines of Cappellari et al. (2013) [Results: "Following (cite: Ruffa24), we fitted the $M_{\rm BH}$ – $L_{\rm \nu,mm}$ and mmFP data, including the XRBs, using the LTS_LINEFIT and LTS_PLANEFIT routines of (cite: Cappellari2013), respectively."]
  - validation: Spearman rank correlation analysis (rho and p-values) and comparison of best-fitting coefficients and scatters with SMBH-only fits of Ruffa et al. (2024). [Results: "In both cases, the best-fitting coefficients agree well with those derived when fitting only the SMBHs (cite: Ruffa24), and both relations have similar or improved scatters compared to the SMBH-only fits."] [Results: "We also performed Spearman rank analyses to quantify the statistical significance of the correlations, and show the resulting correlation coefficients $\rho$ and $p$-values in the top-left corner of each panel of Figure 1 which are similar or better then the correlation coefficients in (cite: Ruffa24)."]
- step 5: Compare observations with ADAF and compact jet physical model grids
  - method: Generate mock SEDs using the LLAGN ADAF model of Pesce et al. (2021) and the BHJET compact jet model of Lucchini et al. (2022) across grids of BH mass and accretion rate / jet power, extract predicted 230 GHz and 2-10 keV luminosities, and… [ADAF model: "In short, we generate a set of model SEDs for a grid of SMBH masses ($10^{0}$ – $10^{10}$ M$_{\odot}$) and Eddington ratios $\dot{M}/\dot{M}_{\rm Edd}$ ($10^{-7}$ – $10^{-2}$), while all the other free parameters are kept at their defaults."] [ADAF model: "We then extract the predicted $230$ GHz and $2$ – $10$-keV luminosities, and compare them with the measured ones."]
  - software: BHJET
  - parameter: ADAF grid: M_BH = 10^0 - 10^10 M_sun, Mdot/Mdot_Edd = 10^-7 - 10^-2 [ADAF model: "In short, we generate a set of model SEDs for a grid of SMBH masses ($10^{0}$ – $10^{10}$ M$_{\odot}$) and Eddington ratios $\dot{M}/\dot{M}_{\rm Edd}$ ($10^{-7}$ – $10^{-2}$), while all the other free parameters are kept at their defaults."]
  - parameter: BHJET grid: M_BH = 10^0 - 10^10 M_sun, jet power = 10^-5.5 - 10^-1.5 L_Edd, inclinations i = 2.5 deg and 90 deg [Compact jet model: "In Figure 4, we show the $L_{\rm X,2-10}$ – $L_{\rm \nu,mm}$ relation with the resulting jet model grids overlaid in black and green as a function of $M_{\rm BH}$ ($10^{0}-10^{10}\,{\rm M_\odot}$) and jet power ($10^{-5.5}-10^{-1.5}{\rm L_{Edd}}$) for the extremes jet inclinations $2.5^{\circ}$ and $90^{\circ}$, respe…"]
  - assumption: All other model free parameters fixed at their default values calibrated for SMBHs [ADAF model: "As mentioned above, we kept all of these parameters fixed to their default values (presented and discussed in Appendix A of (cite: Pesce21), and calibrated only for SMBHs) for our analyses."] [Compact jet model: "Such inconclusive results are perhaps unsurprising, however, as compact jet models are substantially more complex than ADAFs, and similarly to them they have many free parameters (that we again left at their default values) that are essentially unconstrained."]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm power-law slope = 0.89 ± 0.04 dimensionless [Results: "\log_{10}\left(\frac{M_{\rm BH}}{\rm M_{\odot}}\right)=(0.89\pm0.04)\left[\log_{10}\left(\frac{L_{\rm \nu,mm}}{\mathrm{erg\,s}^{-1}}\right)-39\right] + (8.26\pm0.08)\,,"]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm power-law intercept = 8.26 ± 0.08 dex [Results: "\log_{10}\left(\frac{M_{\rm BH}}{\rm M_{\odot}}\right)=(0.89\pm0.04)\left[\log_{10}\left(\frac{L_{\rm \nu,mm}}{\mathrm{erg\,s}^{-1}}\right)-39\right] + (8.26\pm0.08)\,,"]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm observed scatter sigma_obs = 0.49 ± not stated dex [Results: "with an observed scatter ($\sigma_{\rm obs}$) of $0.49$ dex and an estimated intrinsic scatter ($\sigma_{\rm int}$) of $0.44\pm0.15$ dex."]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm intrinsic scatter sigma_int = 0.44 ± 0.15 dex [Results: "with an observed scatter ($\sigma_{\rm obs}$) of $0.49$ dex and an estimated intrinsic scatter ($\sigma_{\rm int}$) of $0.44\pm0.15$ dex."]
- result: mmFP L_X,2-10 slope coefficient = -0.22 ± 0.05 dimensionless [Results: "\nonumber \log_{10}\left(\frac{M_{\rm BH}}{\rm M_{\odot}}\right) = (-0.22\pm0.05)\left[\log_{10}\left(\frac{L_{\rm X,2-10}}{\mathrm{erg\,s}^{-1}}\right)-40\right] \\+ (0.97\pm0.05)\left[\log_{10}\left(\frac{L_{\nu,\rm mm}}{\mathrm{erg\,s}^{-1}}\right)-39\right] + (8.32\pm0.08)\,,"]
- result: mmFP L_nu,mm slope coefficient = 0.97 ± 0.05 dimensionless [Results: "\nonumber \log_{10}\left(\frac{M_{\rm BH}}{\rm M_{\odot}}\right) = (-0.22\pm0.05)\left[\log_{10}\left(\frac{L_{\rm X,2-10}}{\mathrm{erg\,s}^{-1}}\right)-40\right] \\+ (0.97\pm0.05)\left[\log_{10}\left(\frac{L_{\nu,\rm mm}}{\mathrm{erg\,s}^{-1}}\right)-39\right] + (8.32\pm0.08)\,,"]
- result: mmFP intercept coefficient = 8.32 ± 0.08 dex [Results: "\nonumber \log_{10}\left(\frac{M_{\rm BH}}{\rm M_{\odot}}\right) = (-0.22\pm0.05)\left[\log_{10}\left(\frac{L_{\rm X,2-10}}{\mathrm{erg\,s}^{-1}}\right)-40\right] \\+ (0.97\pm0.05)\left[\log_{10}\left(\frac{L_{\nu,\rm mm}}{\mathrm{erg\,s}^{-1}}\right)-39\right] + (8.32\pm0.08)\,,"]
- result: mmFP observed scatter sigma_obs = 0.42 ± not stated dex [Results: "with $\sigma_{\rm obs}=0.42$ dex and $\sigma_{\rm int}=0.36\pm0.07$ dex."]
- result: mmFP intrinsic scatter sigma_int = 0.36 ± 0.07 dex [Results: "with $\sigma_{\rm obs}=0.42$ dex and $\sigma_{\rm int}=0.36\pm0.07$ dex."]
- limitation (authors): Scaling archival mm fluxes from 98, 104, and 350 GHz to 230 GHz assuming a flat spectral index (alpha = 0) introduces uncertainties of ~6% to 14%. [Archival Observations: "Archival mm fluxes at frequencies other than $230$ GHz were scaled to that band assuming a flat spectral index ($\alpha=0$). This unavoidably introduces uncertainties in our comparisons."]
- limitation (authors): Non-simultaneous mm and X-ray observations (especially for A0620-00 where Chandra data is from 2013 and F_var = 0.83, and GRS 1915+105 with a 9-month gap) mean temporal variability can significantly impact source positions and increase sca… [Archival Observations: "This data was taken in 2013 meaning that A0620-00 is a source where variability can have an significant impact."]
- limitation (authors): Potential contribution from the donor star wind to the mm emission of Cygnus X-1 is not modeled. [Physical Drivers: "For Cygnus X-1 in the soft state and potentially in the hard state there might be some contribution to the mm emission from the donor star wind. This contribution should be investigated further."]
- not stated (card): Specific CASA versions used for each dataset; TCLEAN weighting scheme (e.g., Briggs robust parameter), cell size, image size, and stopping threshold; Exact method used to measure continuum flux densities (e.g., peak pixel vs Gaussian fit vs aperture integration) and upper limit threshold (e.g., 3-sigma) inherited from Ruffa et al. (2024); Assumed flux scale uncertainty when computing errors on L_…

### 2015MNRAS.452...88K: ALMA detection of a disc-dominated [C II] emission line at z=4.6 in the luminous QSO J1554+1937 (2015; agn_and_quasars, high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the origin (rotating disc vs bulge) of the broad [C II] line in the luminous z=4.6 QSO J1554+1937, and how does this affect its position relative to the local M-sigma relation?
- step 6: Compare with M-sigma
  - method: Place the source relative to the Kormendy & Ho relation with SMBH mass from Shen et al. for various inclinations [4.7: "We show the estimated locations for J1554+1937 for different potential values of the unknown disc inclination"]
  - assumption: intrinsic scatter 0.29 dex [4.7: "Given that the intrinsic scatter of the $M$–$\sigma$ relation is 0.29 dex"]
- result: Eddington ratio = 0.15 ± not stated dimensionless [4.6: "the Eddington ratio ($L_\mathrm{bol}/L_\mathrm{Edd}$) is 0.15"]
- limitation (authors): Low spatial resolution prevents determining inclination and detailed kinematics [4.4: "The poor spatial resolution prevents us from determining the inclination"]
- not stated (card): Channel width and cell/image size used in clean; Line-free channels used for continuum subtraction and continuum map; Clean threshold, masking and iterations; Self-calibration solution interval; How the moment maps and 20 per cent half-width were measured (visual); Details of the line profile model fitting/inclination integration; Number of execution blocks and observing date

### 2020ApJ...903...34A: Probing the Nature of High-redshift Weak Emission Line Quasars: A Young Quasar with a Starburst Host Galaxy (2020; agn_and_quasars, high_z_galaxies; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: What is the nature of the weak emission line quasar PSO J083+11 at z=6.34 (young quasar vs lensing vs soft continuum), and what are the properties of its host galaxy from ALMA [C II] and dust continuum?
- step 1: Fit GNIRS spectrum and derive black hole mass, Eddington ratio and line properties
  - method: Multi-component fit (power law + UV Fe II continuum, single Gaussian broad lines) with a modified PyQSOFit; Mg II virial mass from the single-epoch scaling relation; L_bol = 5.15 x lambda L_3000 [Modeling the emission lines and underly…: "The global continuum was modelled using combined power-law and UV Fe II templates"] [Modeling the emission lines and underly…: "After subtracting the best-fit continuum and scaled iron templates, each broad emission line was modeled with single Gaussian functions."]
  - software: PyQSOFit (modified), PypeIt, Molecfit
  - parameter: Rest-frame continuum windows: 1285-1290, 1315-1325, 1350-1370, 1445-1465, 1580-1650, 2140-2300, 2340-2400, 2420-2480, 2630-2710, 2745-2765, 2850-3000 A [Modeling the emission lines and underly…: "The following wavelength ranges were selected as continuum windows: $\lambda_\mathrm{rest}=$ 1285–1290 Å, 1315–1325 Å, 1350–1370 Å, 1445–1465 Å, 1580–1650 Å, 2140–2300 Å, 2340–2400 Å, 2420–2480 Å, 2630–2710 Å, 2745–2765 Å, and 2850–3000 Å."]
  - parameter: Bolometric correction L_bol = 5.15 x lambda L_3000 (Richards et al. 2006) [Modeling the emission lines and underly…: "Then, the bolometric luminosity $L_{\rm bol}$ was derived using the empirical correction from (cite: 2006ApJS..166..470R):"]
  - assumption: Virial theorem valid for the BLR dynamics; systematic mass errors ~0.5 dex not included [Black hole mass and Eddington ratio: "With the assumption that the virial theorem is valid for the BLR dynamics"] [Black hole mass and Eddington ratio: "we did not explicitly add the systematic errors which tend to be larger ($\approx 0.5$ dex) compared to random measurement errors"]
  - fitting: Monte Carlo: 1000 mock spectra with noise drawn from the noise spectrum; 16th/84th percentiles as limits [Modeling the emission lines and underly…: "Random flux density errors are drawn assuming a normal distribution using the noise spectrum, then applied to the raw spectrum with 1000 iterations."]
  - validation: Compared with values from the combined FIRE spectra [Black hole mass and Eddington ratio: "As a comparison, we derived $\log (M_{\rm BH}/M_\odot) = 9.06^{+0.24}_{-0.16}$ and $L_{\rm bol}/L_{\rm Edd} = 0.77_{-0.33}^{+0.33}$ from the combined FIRE spectra."]
- result: Black hole mass log(M_BH/Msun) (GNIRS) = 9.30 ± +0.16/-0.10 dex (Msun) [Black hole mass and Eddington ratio: "We obtained a black hole mass of $\log (M_{\rm BH}/M_\odot) = 9.30^{+0.16}_{-0.10}$ and normalized accretion rate of $L_{\rm bol}/L_{\rm Edd} = 0.51^{+0.13}_{-0.17}$ for the GNIRS spectrum."]
- result: Eddington ratio L_bol/L_Edd (GNIRS) = 0.51 ± +0.13/-0.17 dimensionless [Black hole mass and Eddington ratio: "We obtained a black hole mass of $\log (M_{\rm BH}/M_\odot) = 9.30^{+0.16}_{-0.10}$ and normalized accretion rate of $L_{\rm bol}/L_{\rm Edd} = 0.51^{+0.13}_{-0.17}$ for the GNIRS spectrum."]
- result: Mg II FWHM = 4140 ± +880/-430 km/s [Modeling the emission lines and underly…: "we found FWHM (Mg II) $=4140^{+880}_{-430}$ km s$^{-1}$ and EW (Mg II)$_\mathrm{rest}$ $=8.71^{+0.67}_{-0.64}$ Å"]
- result: Rest-frame EW(Mg II) = 8.71 ± +0.67/-0.64 Angstrom [Modeling the emission lines and underly…: "we found FWHM (Mg II) $=4140^{+880}_{-430}$ km s$^{-1}$ and EW (Mg II)$_\mathrm{rest}$ $=8.71^{+0.67}_{-0.64}$ Å"]
- result: UV power-law slope alpha_lambda = -1.66 ± +/-0.01 dimensionless [Soft continuum due to super-Eddington a…: "our measured power-law slope index is consistent with a typical quasar ($\alpha_\lambda = -1.66 \pm 0.01$)"]
- limitation (authors): Only two continuum points on the Rayleigh-Jeans tail, so dust temperature and SED shape unconstrained; dust mass has at least a factor of two systematic uncertainty [Star formation rate: "Note that we only have 2 data points of continuum measurements – at 244 and 258 GHz – both of them located on the Rayleigh–Jeans tail and not reaching the dust SED peak."]
- limitation (authors): Black hole mass systematic errors (~0.5 dex) not included; virial calibration may be inadequate for WLQs [Black hole mass and Eddington ratio: "However, please note that we did not explicitly add the systematic errors which tend to be larger ($\approx 0.5$ dex) compared to random measurement errors"]
- limitation (authors): Shielding gas between disk and BLR cannot be excluded without X-ray spectroscopy [Summary and Conclusion: "However, we cannot completely rule out the possibility of the presence of shielding gas between accretion disk and BLR due to the lack of X-ray spectroscopy."]
- not stated (card): CASA version and ALMA pipeline version/release used for calibration, and whether the archive products were used as delivered; Flux, bandpass and phase calibrators and the absolute flux scale uncertainty; Spectral window layout and which channels were treated as line-free for the median continuum model (and the [C II] channel range masked); tclean parameters not given: cell size, image size, decon…

### 2024MNRAS.528L..76R: A fundamental plane of black hole accretion at millimetre wavelengths (2024; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; archival_reuse)
- question: Whether a tight correlation (fundamental plane) exists at millimetre wavelengths between nuclear 1 mm luminosity, intrinsic 2-10 keV X-ray luminosity, and supermassive black hole mass across low- and high-luminosity AGN, and what physical …
- step 2: Compile X-ray luminosities and SMBH masses for primary and BASS samples
  - method: Retrieve intrinsic 2-10 keV X-ray luminosities and dynamical SMBH masses from literature; for galaxies without dynamical masses, estimate M_BH from stellar velocity dispersion using the M_BH-sigma_* relation of van den Bosch (2016), and re… [2 PRIMARY SAMPLE AND DATA: "The intrinsic (absorption-corrected) 2–10 keV luminosities ( 𝐿 X , 2−10 ) of the primary sample sources were retrieved from the literature, as detailed in Elford et al. (2023)."] [2 PRIMARY SAMPLE AND DATA: "For the remaining 17 galaxies, we estimated 𝑀 BH using the 𝑀 BH – 𝜎 ★ relation of van den Bosch (2016), where 𝜎 ★ is the stellar velocity dispersion within one effective radius."]
  - assumption: M_BH-sigma_* relation of van den Bosch (2016) applies to non-dynamical mass targets [2 PRIMARY SAMPLE AND DATA: "For the remaining 17 galaxies, we estimated 𝑀 BH using the 𝑀 BH – 𝜎 ★ relation of van den Bosch (2016), where 𝜎 ★ is the stellar velocity dispersion within one effective radius."]
  - validation: Verified minimal X-ray contamination from hot gas/XRBs in 5 non-Chandra sources using scaling laws, and checked that removing non-Chandra sources or non-dynamical M_BH sources does not change best-fitting parameters [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "While we verified that any such contamination should be minimal (based on the scaling laws of Grimm et al. 2003, Kim & Fabbiano 2004 and Boroson et al. 2011), we cannot rule it out entirely."] [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "In any case, removing these five sources does not make any relevant change in the best-fitting parameters of the mmFP. The same applies when removing the sources without a robust, dynamical 𝑀 BH estimate"]
- step 3: Fit 1D M_BH-L_nu,mm relation and 2D mm Fundamental Plane (M_BH-L_X,2-10-L_nu,mm)
  - method: Perform robust linear and plane regressions using lts_linefit and lts_planefit, and quantify statistical significance via Spearman rank correlation analyses. [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "A power law was fitted to the observed trend, using the lts_linefit routine (Cappellari et al. 2013)."] [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "In this case, we used the lts_planefit routine (Cappellari et al. 2013) to find the best-fitting plane in the (log 𝑀 BH, log 𝐿 X , 2−10, log 𝐿 𝜈, mm ) space:"]
  - software: lts_linefit, lts_planefit
  - fitting: Least-trimmed-squares (LTS) robust regression combined with least-squares fitting allowing for intrinsic scatter and uncertainties in all coordinates [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "This combines the least-trimmed-squares (LTS) robust regression technique (Rousseeuw 1984) with a least-squares fitting algorithm, and allows for intrinsic scatter and uncertainties in all coordinates."]
  - validation: Compared Bayesian Information Criterion between line and plane fits (Delta BIC >> 10) and tested compatibility with BASS sample [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "We verified that this multi-variate plane fit provides a significantly better predictor for 𝑀 BH than the simple line fit, having a Δ BIC >> 10 (where Δ BIC is the difference in the Bayesian information criterion between the line and plane fits)."] [3.1 BASS galaxies: "We performed a Spearman rank analysis to quantify the statistical significance of this relation for the BASS points alone, and verified that they do show a significant correlation ( 𝑝 = 0 . 002), but with a correlation coefficient ( 𝜌 =0.32) smaller than that of the primary sample."]
- step 4: Compare observed correlations with theoretical SED models (Torus, ADAF, Compact Jet)
  - method: Extract predicted 237.5 GHz and 2-10 keV luminosities from grids of extrapolated SKIRTOR torus models, Pesce et al. (2021) LLAGN ADAF models, and Lucchini et al. (2022) Bhjet compact jet models, and compare with observations in luminosity … [4.1 Torus model: "For each SKIRTOR SED model, we then extracted the predicted 1 mm (specifically, the luminosity at 237.5 GHz, that is the median ALMA continuum frequency for both the primary and BASS sources) and intrinsic 2-10 keV luminosities, and compared them with the measured ones."] [4.2 ADAF model: "We generated a set of model SEDs for a grid of SMBH masses (10 6 – 10 10 M ⊙ ) and Eddington ratios (10 −7 – 10 −2 ), while all the other free parameters were kept at the defaults discussed in Appendix A of Pesce et al. (2021)."]
  - software: SKIRTOR, LLAGN, Bhjet
  - parameter: SKIRTOR L_bol = 10^7.5 - 10^12.5 L_sun [4.1 Torus model: "We followed the prescriptions of Stalevski et al. (2012, 2016) to scale the models for different 𝐿 bol, in the range 10 7 . 5 – 10 12 . 5 L ⊙"]
  - parameter: ADAF grid: M_BH = 10^6 - 10^10 M_sun, Eddington ratios = 10^-7 - 10^-2, plus 0.5 dex M_BH offset [4.2 ADAF model: "We generated a set of model SEDs for a grid of SMBH masses (10 6 – 10 10 M ⊙ ) and Eddington ratios (10 −7 – 10 −2 ), while all the other free parameters were kept at the defaults discussed in Appendix A of Pesce et al. (2021)."] [4.2 ADAF model: "here we simply offset the model grid by a constant 0 . 5 dex in SMBH mass to align it with the observed correlation."]
  - parameter: Bhjet grid: M_BH = 10^6 - 10^10 M_sun, jet power = 10^-5.5 - 10^-0.5 L_Edd, inclination = 2.5 deg - 90 deg [4.3 Compact jet model: "generated a grid of models varying the SMBH mass (10 6 – 10 10 M ⊙ ), jet power (10 −5 . 5 – 10 −0 . 5 L Edd ) and jet inclination to the line-of-sight (2.5 ◦ – 90 ◦ )."]
  - assumption: Grey-body curve for mm emission and power-law for X-rays when extrapolating SKIRTOR models [4.1 Torus model: "(i.e. using the same grey-body curve for millimetre emission, and a power-law in the X-ray regime; Yang et al. 2020)."]
- step 5: Test for spurious correlation and biases from distance uncertainties
  - method: Run Monte Carlo simulations drawing uncorrelated M_BH and L from Gaussian distributions with distance errors, and re-fit correlations restricting to the 26/48 sources with accurate redshift-independent distances. [4.4 Distance uncertainties: "To test that this is not affecting our results, we performed a simple Monte Carlo simulation, drawing 𝑀 BH and the luminosities from independent Gaussian distributions that are truly uncorrelated, forcing a correlation to arise due to distance errors alone."] [4.4 Distance uncertainties: "We restricted our analysis to only those sources with the most accurate (redshift-independent) distances (26/48)"]
  - assumption: M_BH scales as D and L scales as D^2 [4.4 Distance uncertainties: "Both 𝑀 BH and luminosity measurements are systematically affected by the assumed galaxy distance 𝐷, with 𝑀 BH ∝ 𝐷 and 𝐿 ∝ 𝐷 2 ."]
  - validation: Confirmed distance errors >= 1.5 dex would be needed to fake the correlation (yielding flatter slopes of 0.5 and 0.25), and redshift-independent subsample yields even tighter intrinsic scatter (0.19 dex and 0.11 dex) [4.4 Distance uncertainties: "The magnitude of the distance errors required to reproduce the Spearman rank correlation coefficients in Fig. 1 turned out to be ≥1.5 dex, much higher than that of our primary sample sources"] [4.4 Distance uncertainties: "This led us to obtain much tighter correlations, with an intrinsic scatter of 0 . 19 dex for the 𝑀 BH − 𝐿 𝜈, mm relation and only 0 . 11 dex for the mmFP."]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm power-law slope = 0.79 ± 0.08 dimensionless [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE, Equation 1: "= (0 _._ 79 ± 0 _._ 08) log 10"]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm power-law intercept = 8.2 ± 0.1 dex [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE, Equation 1: "(8 _._ 2 ± 0 _._ 1)"]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm observed scatter (sigma_obs) = 0.55 ± not stated dex [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "with an observed scatter ( 𝜎 obs ) of 0 . 55 dex and an estimated intrinsic scatter ( 𝜎 int ) of 0 . 51±0 . 08 dex."]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm intrinsic scatter (sigma_int) = 0.51 ± 0.08 dex [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "with an observed scatter ( 𝜎 obs ) of 0 . 55 dex and an estimated intrinsic scatter ( 𝜎 int ) of 0 . 51±0 . 08 dex."]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm Spearman rank correlation coefficient (rho) = 0.79 ± not stated dimensionless [Figure 1 caption: "=0 .79 p=7 .37e-10"]
- result: M_BH - L_nu,mm Spearman rank p-value = 7.37e-10 ± not stated dimensionless [Figure 1 caption: "=0 .79 p=7 .37e-10"]
- result: mmFP log(L_X,2-10) coefficient = -0.23 ± 0.05 dimensionless [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE, Equation 2: "= (−0 _._ 23 ± 0 _._ 05) log 10"]
- result: mmFP log(L_nu,mm) coefficient = 0.95 ± 0.07 dimensionless [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE, Equation 2: "+(0 _._ 95 ± 0 _._ 07) log 10"]
- result: mmFP intercept = 8.35 ± 0.08 dex [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE, Equation 2: "(8 _._ 35 ± 0 _._ 08) _,_ (2)"]
- result: mmFP observed scatter (sigma_obs) = 0.45 ± not stated dex [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "with 𝜎 obs = 0 . 45 dex and 𝜎 int = 0 . 40 ± 0 . 07 dex."]
- result: mmFP intrinsic scatter (sigma_int) = 0.40 ± 0.07 dex [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "with 𝜎 obs = 0 . 45 dex and 𝜎 int = 0 . 40 ± 0 . 07 dex."]
- result: mmFP Spearman rank correlation coefficient (rho) = 0.88 ± not stated dimensionless [Figure 1 caption: "=0 .88 p=4 .09e-13"]
- result: mmFP Spearman rank p-value = 4.09e-13 ± not stated dimensionless [Figure 1 caption: "=0 .88 p=4 .09e-13"]
- result: BASS sample mmFP Spearman rank correlation coefficient (rho) = 0.32 ± not stated dimensionless [3.1 BASS galaxies: "and verified that they do show a significant correlation ( 𝑝 = 0 . 002), but with a correlation coefficient ( 𝜌 =0.32) smaller than that of the primary sample."]
- result: BASS sample mmFP Spearman rank p-value = 0.002 ± not stated dimensionless [3.1 BASS galaxies: "and verified that they do show a significant correlation ( 𝑝 = 0 . 002), but with a correlation coefficient ( 𝜌 =0.32) smaller than that of the primary sample."]
- result: Redshift-independent distance subsample M_BH - L_nu,mm intrinsic scatter = 0.19 ± not stated dex [4.4 Distance uncertainties: "This led us to obtain much tighter correlations, with an intrinsic scatter of 0 . 19 dex for the 𝑀 BH − 𝐿 𝜈, mm relation and only 0 . 11 dex for the mmFP."]
- result: Redshift-independent distance subsample mmFP intrinsic scatter = 0.11 ± not stated dex [4.4 Distance uncertainties: "This led us to obtain much tighter correlations, with an intrinsic scatter of 0 . 19 dex for the 𝑀 BH − 𝐿 𝜈, mm relation and only 0 . 11 dex for the mmFP."]
- result: Redshift-independent distance subsample M_BH - L_nu,mm Spearman rho = 0.84 ± not stated dimensionless [4.4 Distance uncertainties: "The corresponding Spearman rank coefficients are 𝜌 = 0 . 84 ( 𝑝 = 5 . 05 × 10 −7 ) and 𝜌 = 0 . 93 ( 𝑝 = 2 . 67 × 10 −9 ), respectively."]
- result: Redshift-independent distance subsample mmFP Spearman rho = 0.93 ± not stated dimensionless [4.4 Distance uncertainties: "The corresponding Spearman rank coefficients are 𝜌 = 0 . 84 ( 𝑝 = 5 . 05 × 10 −7 ) and 𝜌 = 0 . 93 ( 𝑝 = 2 . 67 × 10 −9 ), respectively."]
- limitation (authors): Primary sample is constructed based on data availability only and is not statistically complete. [2 PRIMARY SAMPLE AND DATA: "Crucially, although constructed based on data availability only (and thus not meant to be complete in any statistical sense), our primary sample spans four orders of magnitude in SMBH mass."]
- limitation (authors): In 3 out of 48 galaxies, the central mm emission is slightly resolved, making their flux measurements more uncertain. [2 PRIMARY SAMPLE AND DATA: "In 3 (out of 48) galaxies, the emission is slightly resolved, making our measurements more uncertain."]
- limitation (authors): Non-simultaneous nuclear mm and X-ray observations are subject to AGN time variability (factor of 2-3 over year timescales), which likely dominates the observed scatter. [3 THE MM FUNDAMENTAL PLANE: "Since the nuclear mm and X-ray emission from AGN is known to be time variable (typically by a factor of 2 – 3 over year timescales; Prieto et al. 2016; Fernández-Ontiveros et al. 2019; Behar et al. 2020), variability likely dominates the observed scatters (and thus the underlying correlations may be tighter)."]
- not stated (card): Exact CASA versions used for each individual ALMA project; tclean imaging parameters: weighting scheme (e.g., Briggs robust parameter), cell size, image size, cleaning threshold, and masking strategy; Specific frequency/channel ranges selected as line-free channels in each dataset; Whether primary beam correction or self-calibration was applied during continuum imaging; Exact observed frequency (…

### 2024ApJ...966..132K: Gas-dynamical Mass Measurements of the Supermassive Black Holes in the Early-type Galaxies NGC 4786 and NGC 5193 from ALMA and HST Observations (2024; nearby_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Measure the masses of the central supermassive black holes in the early-type galaxies NGC 4786 and NGC 5193 by fitting thin rotating-disk gas-dynamical models directly to ALMA CO(2-1) cubes, with HST H-band host-galaxy mass models
- step 6: Fit thin-disk gas-dynamical models to the ALMA cubes
  - method: Build circular-velocity field from BH (point mass), deprojected MGE stars (scaled by sqrt(Upsilon_H)) and gas disk; project to LOS with Gaussian line profiles; weight by a deconvolved CO flux map; convolve each channel with the synthesized… [4 Dynamical Modeling: "The BH masses are measured by modeling the observed gas kinematics in the ALMA data cubes with a thin, rotating disk model."] [4 Dynamical Modeling: "At each point in the disk, we model the emergent CO line profile as a Gaussian"]
  - software: JamPy, scikit-image, astropy, 3DBarolo
  - parameter: nine free parameters minimum: M_BH, Upsilon_H, i, Gamma, (x_c, y_c), F0, sigma_0 (plus systemic velocity) [4 Dynamical Modeling: "Our models use a minimum of nine free parameters which include BH mass $M_{\mathrm{BH}}$, $H$-band mass-to-light ratio $\Upsilon_H$, disk inclination angle $i$, disk position angle $\Gamma$"]
  - parameter: model grid oversampled by factor s = 3; five Richardson-Lucy iterations to deconvolve the flux map; data cubes block-averaged by a factor of 4 [4 Dynamical Modeling: "on a grid that is oversampled by a factor of $s = 3$ relative to the size of the spatial pixels in the data cube"] [4 Dynamical Modeling: "applied five iterations of the Richardson-Lucy algorithm implemented in the"]
  - parameter: fit regions: ellipse q = 0.50 with semimajor axis 0.55 arcsec (NGC 4786) and 1.0 arcsec (NGC 5193); 31 channels / 372 data points (NGC 4786), 55 channels / 3190 data points (NGC 5193) [4 Dynamical Modeling: "Our elliptical fitting regions have an axial ratio of $q = 0.50$ and semimajor axis lengths of $a = 0\hbox{\ensuremath{.\!\!^{\prime\prime}}}{55}$ and $a=1\hbox{\ensuremath{.\!\!^{\prime\prime}}}{0}$ for NGC 4786 and NGC 5193, respectively."] [4 Dynamical Modeling: "We fit our dynamical models to 31 frequency channels in the NGC 4786 data cube"]
  - assumption: MGE deprojection assumes oblate axisymmetric galaxies with inclinations of 70 deg (NGC 4786) and 60 deg (NGC 5193) from initial fits; distance fixed [4 Dynamical Modeling: "are oblate, axisymmetric, and have inclination angles of $70^{\circ}$ and $60^{\circ}$, respectively"]
  - assumption: dark matter neglected; spatially uniform turbulent dispersion sigma_0; thin gas disk [4 Dynamical Modeling: "We neglect the contribution of dark matter as the enclosed mass profiles on scales comparable to the circumnuclear disks are thought to be dominated by the stars."]
  - fitting: chi^2 minimization with a 3D noise model (per-channel rms of emission-free pixels divided by the primary beam); optimizer, priors and bounds not stated [4 Dynamical Modeling: "We first calculated the noise in each frequency channel as the standard deviation of emission-free pixels on the block-averaged scale."] [4 Dynamical Modeling: "The 3D noise model is then generated by dividing the uniform rms noise value by the primary beam for each frequency channel."]
  - validation: data and model compared via PVDs, moment maps and CO line profiles; reduced chi^2 reported with degrees of freedom [5.1 NGC 4786 Dynamical Modeling Results: "The comparisons highlight good overall agreement between the data and model."]
- step 8: Assess systematic uncertainties and build the error budget
  - method: Repeat the fiducial fit with changes in: host MGE (unmasked, dust-masked, dust-corrected), radial motion, radius-dependent velocity dispersion, fit region, gas mass removed, no block-averaging, no oversampling, 3DBarolo tilted-ring flux ma… [5.3 Error Budget of the Mass Measuremen…: "we took our statistically best-fit dynamical models and performed a number of systematic tests that involved changing aspects of the model construction."] [5.3 Error Budget of the Mass Measuremen…: "After adding the systematic uncertainties associated with the extinction correction in quadrature with the uncertainties associated with the systematic tests"]
  - software: 3DBarolo
  - parameter: fit region of semimajor axis ~1.25 ALMA resolution elements; oversampling factor set to 1; velocity dispersion sigma(r) = sigma_0 + sigma_1 exp[-(r-r_0)^2 / 2 mu^2] [5.3 Error Budget of the Mass Measuremen…: "ellipses with semimajor axes of approximately 1.25 ALMA resolution elements centered on the disk’s dynamical center"] [5.3 Error Budget of the Mass Measuremen…: "We set this factor to 1 to test the effect of building our models on a grid that is equal in size to the original ALMA spatial scale."]
  - assumption: dust-corrected MGE models are taken as the best estimate of the intrinsic host galaxy profile; radial motion via a toy model with parameter alpha [5.3 Error Budget of the Mass Measuremen…: "As stated earlier, the dust-corrected MGE models are our best estimate of the intrinsic host galaxy profile."]
  - validation: models with M_BH = 0 re-optimized to test whether a BH is needed; extinction-correction systematics bracket the plausible host profiles [5.3 Error Budget of the Mass Measuremen…: "we fixed $\ensuremath{M_\mathrm{BH}}= 0 \, M_{\odot}$ in our dynamical models and reoptimized with the dust-corrected MGE models"]
- step 9: Compare SOI resolution and scaling-relation predictions
  - method: Compute r_SOI where M_star(r) = M_BH for each MGE, form xi = 2 r_SOI / theta_FWHM, and compare M_BH ranges with M_BH-sigma, M_BH-r_b, M_BH-L_bulge,K and M_BH-M_bulge predictions [6.1 The Impact of Resolving the BH Sphe…: "we compare our measured $r_{\mathrm{SOI}}$ values to the average ALMA beam size through $\xi = 2r_{\mathrm{SOI}}/\theta_{\mathrm{FWHM}}$."] [6.2 Comparison to Predictions from BH S…: "we input our `GALFIT` value of"]
  - parameter: sigma_star 286 and 205 km/s from Hyperleda; H-K = 0.2 mag; M_sun,H = 3.37 mag, M_sun,K = 3.27 mag [6.2 Comparison to Predictions from BH S…: "assumed a color of $H-K$ = 0.2 mag based on stellar population models of an old stellar population with solar metallicity (cite: 2010MNRAS.404.1639V)"]
- result: M_BH NGC 4786 (fiducial model C), statistical and systematic = 5.0 x 10^8 Msun ± +/-0.2 (1 sigma statistical), +1.4/-1.3 (systematic); distance +/-0.75 (units of 10^8 Msun) 10^8 Msun [abstract: "= 5.0 \pm 0.2 \,[\mathrm{1\sigma \,statistical}] \,^{+1.4}_{-1.3} \,[\mathrm{systematic}]$ in NGC 4786"]
- result: M_BH NGC 5193 (fiducial model F), statistical and systematic = 1.4 x 10^8 Msun ± +/-0.03 (1 sigma statistical), +1.5/-0.1 (systematic); distance +/-0.1 (units of 10^8 Msun) 10^8 Msun [abstract: "= 1.4 \pm 0.03 \, [\mathrm{1\sigma\,statistical}] ^{+1.5}_{-0.1} \,[\mathrm{systematic}]$ in NGC 5193"]
- result: M_BH NGC 4786 range across unmasked, dust-masked, dust-corrected MGEs = 3.9 to 5.8 x 10^8 Msun, with reduced chi^2 between 1.421 and 1.488 over 363 degrees of freedom ± not stated 10^8 Msun [5.1 NGC 4786 Dynamical Modeling Results: "dynamical models A-C yield a range in BH mass that spans $(3.9-5.8) \times 10^8\, M_{\odot}$"]
- result: M_BH NGC 5193 range across unmasked, dust-masked, dust-corrected MGEs = 1.4 to 2.9 x 10^8 Msun; Upsilon_H 1.46-1.69; reduced chi^2 2.096-2.541 over 3181 degrees of freedom ± not stated 10^8 Msun [5.2 NGC 5193 Dynamical Modeling Results: "$\ensuremath{M_\mathrm{BH}}= (1.4-2.9) \times 10^8\, M_{\odot}$, $\Upsilon_H = 1.46 - 1.69\ M_\odot/L_\odot$"]
- result: Reduced chi^2 of fiducial model C (NGC 4786) = 1.421 ± not stated dimensionless [5.1 NGC 4786 Dynamical Modeling Results: "With $\ensuremath{\chi^2_{\mathrm{\nu}}}= 1.421$, model C is not a formally acceptable fit"]
- result: Turbulent velocity dispersion sigma_0 (NGC 4786 models) = 9.3-10.8 ± not stated km/s [5.1 NGC 4786 Dynamical Modeling Results: "The $\sigma_0$ parameter remains fairly low between $9.3 - 10.8 \, \mathrm{km\,s\ensuremath{^{-1}}}$"]
- result: Flux normalization F0 (NGC 4786 models) = 1.54-1.56 ± not stated dimensionless [5.1 NGC 4786 Dynamical Modeling Results: "The flux normalization factor $F_0$ is found to be between $1.54 - 1.56$ in our models"]
- result: Reduced chi^2 with M_BH = 0 (NGC 4786) = 1.974 (with Upsilon_H raised to 2.60 Msun/Lsun) ± not stated dimensionless [5.3 Error Budget of the Mass Measuremen…: "with $\ensuremath{\chi^2_{\mathrm{\nu}}}= 1.974$ in NGC 4786"]
- result: Reduced chi^2 with M_BH = 0 (NGC 5193) = 2.978 (with Upsilon_H = 1.58 Msun/Lsun) ± not stated dimensionless [5.3 Error Budget of the Mass Measuremen…: "much higher $\ensuremath{\chi^2_{\mathrm{\nu}}}= 2.978$"]
- result: Shift in M_BH (NGC 4786) from oversampling factor 1 and from the alternate flux map = -15% and +21% ± not stated percent [5.3 Error Budget of the Mass Measuremen…: "a 15% decrease due to the change in oversampling factor and a $21\%$ increase due to the choice of flux map"]
- result: Resolution figure of merit xi = 2 r_SOI / theta_FWHM = 1.6-1.9 (NGC 4786); 0.3-0.8 (NGC 5193) ± not stated dimensionless [6.1 The Impact of Resolving the BH Sphe…: "we find that $\xi = (1.6-1.9$) and for NGC 5193, we find $\xi = (0.3-0.8)$."]
- result: Projected BH SOI radius, NGC 4786 = 73 pc to 90 pc (0.24 to 0.30 arcsec) ± not stated pc [6.1 The Impact of Resolving the BH Sphe…: "73 pc ($0\hbox{\ensuremath{.\!\!^{\prime\prime}}}{24}$) and 90 pc ($0\hbox{\ensuremath{.\!\!^{\prime\prime}}}{30}$)"]
- result: Projected BH SOI radius, NGC 5193 = 11 pc to 26 pc (0.05 to 0.12 arcsec) ± not stated pc [6.1 The Impact of Resolving the BH Sphe…: "is between 11 pc ($0\hbox{\ensuremath{.\!\!^{\prime\prime}}}{05}$) and 26 pc ($0\hbox{\ensuremath{.\!\!^{\prime\prime}}}{12}$)"]
- result: M_BH predicted by the M_BH-sigma relation (NGC 4786; NGC 5193) = 1.5e9 Msun; 3.4e8 Msun ± not stated Msun [6.2 Comparison to Predictions from BH S…: "the relation predicts a BH mass of $\ensuremath{M_\mathrm{BH}}= 1.5 \times 10^9\, M_{\odot}$, whereas for NGC 5193, it predicts $3.4 \times 10^8\, M_{\odot}$"]
- limitation (authors): Models do not tightly constrain M_BH because the SOI is unresolved [abstract: "our models do not tightly constrain the central black hole mass in either galaxy"]
- limitation (authors): The extinction correction is a simple method; proper treatment would need radiative transfer models [3.2 Dust-Corrected MGE Models: "a proper treatment of determining the intrinsic stellar light distribution in the presence of a dusty circumnuclear disk likely requires the usage of radiative transfer models"]
- limitation (authors): The fiducial models are not formally acceptable fits (reduced chi^2 above the 0.05 significance threshold) [5.2 NGC 5193 Dynamical Modeling Results: "a formally acceptable fit would achieve $\ensuremath{\chi^2_{\mathrm{\nu}}}\leq 1.042$ assuming a level of significance of 0.05"]
- not stated (card): Calibrators (flux, bandpass, phase) and whether the archive pipeline or manual scripts were used for either dataset; Self-calibration, if any, and any flux-scale corrections; CLEAN settings: tclean task, deconvolver, threshold, niter, mask, uv taper, continuum-subtraction fit order and line-free channels; Robust weighting for NGC 5193 (only NGC 4786 states robust = 0.5) and the rms noise of each …

### 2016ApJ...822L..28B: Measurement of the Black Hole Mass in NGC 1332 from ALMA Observations at 0.044 arcsecond Resolution (2016; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the mass of the central black hole in NGC 1332, measured from CO(2-1) gas kinematics that resolve the BH's gravitational radius of influence?
- step 3: Thin-disk dynamical model fit to cube
  - method: Model circular-rotation disk in potential of BH plus stars, Gaussian profiles with turbulent dispersion, scaled to CO surface brightness, convolved with beam, rebinned to 0.04'', chi2 compared to data cube [3 Dynamical Modeling: "The goodness of fit (quantified by $\chi^2$) is computed by direct comparison of the model cube with the binned ALMA data."]
  - software: IDL amoeba
  - parameter: Free parameters: M_BH, Upsilon, sigma_turb, i, Gamma, v_sys, (x0,y0), f0 [3 Dynamical Modeling: "Free parameters include $M_\mathrm{BH}$, $\Upsilon$, $\sigma_\mathrm{turb}$, $i$, $\Gamma$, $\ensuremath{v_\mathrm{sys}}$, the dynamical center position $(x_0,y_0)$, and an overall normalization factor $f_0$"]
  - assumption: Uniform sigma_turb; dark matter and molecular gas mass neglected; distance 22.3 Mpc [3 Dynamical Modeling: "We neglect the contributions of dark matter and of molecular gas, both of which are $\lesssim10^8$ ${M}_{\odot}$ within $r=250$ pc"]
  - fitting: chi2 minimization with amoeba downhill simplex; confidence from delta chi2 [3 Dynamical Modeling: "We optimize the model fits using the `amoeba` downhill simplex method in IDL."]
  - validation: reduced chi2 checks; fits to varying r_fit [Modeling Results, Fits to smaller regio…: "The expected range on $\chi^2_{\nu}$ for 2515 degrees of freedom is $1.00\pm0.03$, so this result suggests that there is still a mild degree of systematic model-data mismatch within $\ensuremath{r_\mathrm{fit}}=50$ pc."]
- step 4: Fit smaller central regions to limit stellar-profile systematics
  - method: Fit within elliptical regions of semimajor axis r_fit; for r_fit=50 pc fix x0, y0, v_sys at best-fit values and grid in M_BH with five other parameters free [4.2 Fits to smaller regions: "we take the values of these three parameters and hold them fixed, calculating a grid of models with $\ensuremath{r_\mathrm{fit}}=50$ pc for a range of values of $M_\mathrm{BH}$ with the remaining five parameters free."]
  - software: IDL amoeba
  - parameter: r_fit = 50 pc, 45 spatial pixels, 2515 dof [4.2 Fits to smaller regions: "These constrained fits for $\ensuremath{r_\mathrm{fit}}=50$ pc (containing 45 spatial pixels) have 2515 degrees of freedom"]
  - fitting: 68.3% and 99% confidence from chi2 vs M_BH [Modeling Results, Fits to smaller regio…: "The 68.3% and 99% confidence ranges on $M_\mathrm{BH}$ are $(6.64_{-0.63}^{+0.65})\times10^8$ and $(6.64_{-1.66}^{+1.69})\times10^8$ ${M}_{\odot}$, respectively."]
  - validation: Smooth chi2 curve required; compared with Rusli stellar-dynamical and Humphrey X-ray masses [Modeling Results, Fits to smaller regio…: "Our goal is to fit models over the smallest possible spatial region that permits successful fits with well-defined confidence limits on $M_\mathrm{BH}$ (manifested by a smooth and regular curve of $\chi^2$ vs. $M_\mathrm{BH}$)."]
- result: M_BH (r_fit=50 pc, 68.3%) = 6.64e8 ± +0.65/-0.63 e8 Msun [abstract: "we find $\ensuremath{M_\mathrm{BH}}=(6.64_{-0.63}^{+0.65})\times10^8$ ${M}_{\odot}$"]
- result: M_BH (full disk fit) = 6.86e8 ± ±0.14e8 (68.3%) Msun [4.1 Fits to the full disk: "The 68.3% confidence region (corresponding to $\Delta\ensuremath{\chi^2}=1$) is $(6.86\pm0.14)\times10^8$ ${M}_{\odot}$."]
- result: Reduced chi2, r_fit=50 pc = 1.09 ± not stated dimensionless [4.2 Fits to smaller regions: "the best-fitting model has $\ensuremath{\chi^2}=2739.8$ and $\ensuremath{\chi^2_{\nu}}=1.09$"]
- result: sigma_turb (r_fit=50 pc) = 32.1 ± not stated km/s [4.2 Fits to smaller regions: "$\ensuremath{\sigma_\mathrm{turb}}=32.1$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: Gravitational radius of influence = 25 pc (0.23'') ± not stated pc [4.2 Fits to smaller regions: "This model implies $\ensuremath{r_\mathrm{g}}= 25$ pc or 0.23$''$"]
- limitation (authors): Mild systematic model-data mismatch; distance uncertainty not in error budget; S/N limiting; minor-axis kinematics unresolved by factor of two [4.2 Fits to smaller regions: "The error budget does not incorporate uncertainty in the distance to NGC 1332"]
- not stated (card): CASA version and calibration/imaging details (weighting, robust, cleaning, continuum subtraction); Array configurations, baselines and calibrators; Spectral window and channel selection used for chi2 (56 of 75 channels); How CO surface brightness map was made; Coordinates of the dynamical center and the target; IDL fitting code not released; amoeba convergence settings; Stellar luminosity profile…

### 2023ApJ...944L..18M: PHANGS-JWST First Results: Interstellar Medium Structure on the Turbulent Jeans Scale in Four Disk Galaxies Observed by JWST and the Atacama Large Millimeter/s… (2023; nearby_galaxies, milky_way_ism, star_formation; archival_reuse)
- question: Examine whether the multi-scale network of filamentary features in four nearby disk galaxies observed with JWST/MIRI is consistent with the fragmentation of the gas disk through gravitational instability on the Toomre, turbulent Jeans, or …
- step 1: Extract gas dynamical properties, surface densities, and velocity dispersions from PHANGS-ALMA CO(2-1) and mega-tables
  - method: Adopt rotation curves from CO velocity fields (NGC 628, NGC 1365, NGC 7496) or stellar-mass semi-empirical model (IC 5332) and interpolate onto the JWST map grid. Adopt 1-kpc hexagonal aperture molecular gas surface densities and 150-pc cl… [PHANGS-ALMA: "Each rotation curve is interpolated onto a finer radial grid so that each pixel (and resolution element) in the JWST map has an estimate of the circular velocity $V_c$ associated with it."] [PHANGS-ALMA: "We therefore adopt the ‘large-scale’ area-weighted average molecular gas surface density measured in 1-kpc hexagonal apertures for NGC 628, NGC 1365 and NGC 7496."]
  - parameter: 1-kpc hexagonal apertures; 150 pc scale for moment-0 and velocity dispersion (NGC 628, NGC 1365, NGC 7496) [PHANGS-ALMA: "In a given hex aperture, these values are calculated from the total flux traced in the 12m+7m+tp CO(2-1) 150 pc-scale integrated intensity (moment-0) map within the hex boundary, divided by the area of the aperture. (cite: sun22)"] [PHANGS-ALMA: "As our fiducial estimate of the molecular gas velocity dispersion in each aperture, we choose the average velocity dispersion measured specifically on 150 pc scales from the 12m+7m+tp CO(2-1) cubes for NGC 628, NGC 1365 and NGC 7496 (cite: sun22)."]
  - parameter: 1.5 kpc resolution convolved 7m+tp moment-0 map and minimum velocity dispersion sigma = 1.25 km s^-1 for IC 5332 [PHANGS-ALMA: "We therefore select the minimum velocity dispersion $\sigma=1.25$ km s$^{-1}$ as a realistic upper bound on the velocity dispersion in the molecular gas at all locations in the map of IC 5332."]
  - assumption: Aperture-dependent alpha_CO following Sun et al. (2022) [PHANGS-ALMA: "We then apply an $\alpha_{\mathrm CO}$ appropriate for each aperture following the prescription of (cite: sun22)."]
  - assumption: Principal fragmentation happens at/near the cloud scale (150 pc) [PHANGS-ALMA: "This assumes that principal fragmentation happens at/near the cloud scale rather than above it; any scale-dependent evolution in turbulent velocity dispersion is assumed to occur below the cloud scale, e.g. where it can lead to secondary fragmentation and eventually lead to star formation."]
  - assumption: Optional atomic gas in IC 5332: exponential decline from maximum 10 M_sun pc^-2 with 3.6 kpc scale length and sigma = 7 km s^-1 [PHANGS-ALMA: "In this case, the total gas surface density is calculated by combining the molecular surface density with an atomic gas surface density that is assumed to exponentially decline from a maximum value of 10 M$_{\odot}$ pc$^{-2}$ with a scale length of 3.6 kpc."] [PHANGS-ALMA: "When the atomic gas component included, the gas velocity dispersion is assigned a value of 7 km s$^{-1}$ (cite: leroy08)."]
  - validation: Checked impact of adopting an alternative global neutral gas velocity dispersion of sigma_gas = 11 km s^-1 [PHANGS-ALMA: "An alternative choice would be to adopt a single value representative of the neutral gas as a whole, e.g. $\sigma_{gas}\approx$ 11 km s$^{-1}$ following (cite: leroy08) and others. We comment on the impact of this choice later in $\S$ [4](#sec:results)."]
- result: Mean molecular gas surface density (Sigma_mol) in IC 5332 = 0.5 ± 0.3 – 1.4 (16th and 84th percentiles) M_odot pc^-2 [Table 1: "| $\Sigma_{mol}$(M$_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$) | 0.5 (0.3 – 1.4) | 11.2 (5.8 – 19.3) | 8 (3.0 – 59.0) | 7.4 (2.0 – 17.8) |"]
- result: Mean molecular gas surface density (Sigma_mol) in NGC 628 = 11.2 ± 5.8 – 19.3 (16th and 84th percentiles) M_odot pc^-2 [Table 1: "| $\Sigma_{mol}$(M$_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$) | 0.5 (0.3 – 1.4) | 11.2 (5.8 – 19.3) | 8 (3.0 – 59.0) | 7.4 (2.0 – 17.8) |"]
- result: Mean molecular gas surface density (Sigma_mol) in NGC 1365 = 8 ± 3.0 – 59.0 (16th and 84th percentiles) M_odot pc^-2 [Table 1: "| $\Sigma_{mol}$(M$_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$) | 0.5 (0.3 – 1.4) | 11.2 (5.8 – 19.3) | 8 (3.0 – 59.0) | 7.4 (2.0 – 17.8) |"]
- result: Mean molecular gas surface density (Sigma_mol) in NGC 7496 = 7.4 ± 2.0 – 17.8 (16th and 84th percentiles) M_odot pc^-2 [Table 1: "| $\Sigma_{mol}$(M$_\odot$ pc$^{-2}$) | 0.5 (0.3 – 1.4) | 11.2 (5.8 – 19.3) | 8 (3.0 – 59.0) | 7.4 (2.0 – 17.8) |"]
- result: Molecular gas velocity dispersion (sigma_mol) in IC 5332 = 1.25 ± not stated km s^-1 [Table 1: "| $\sigma_{mol}$(km s$^{-1}$) | 1.25 | 4.7 (3.9 – 5.5) | 8.7 (3.5 – 23) | 4.3 (3.4 – 6.2) |"]
- result: Mean molecular gas velocity dispersion (sigma_mol) in NGC 628 = 4.7 ± 3.9 – 5.5 (16th and 84th percentiles) km s^-1 [Table 1: "| $\sigma_{mol}$(km s$^{-1}$) | 1.25 | 4.7 (3.9 – 5.5) | 8.7 (3.5 – 23) | 4.3 (3.4 – 6.2) |"]
- result: Mean molecular gas velocity dispersion (sigma_mol) in NGC 1365 = 8.7 ± 3.5 – 23 (16th and 84th percentiles) km s^-1 [Table 1: "| $\sigma_{mol}$(km s$^{-1}$) | 1.25 | 4.7 (3.9 – 5.5) | 8.7 (3.5 – 23) | 4.3 (3.4 – 6.2) |"]
- result: Mean molecular gas velocity dispersion (sigma_mol) in NGC 7496 = 4.3 ± 3.4 – 6.2 (16th and 84th percentiles) km s^-1 [Table 1: "| $\sigma_{mol}$(km s$^{-1}$) | 1.25 | 4.7 (3.9 – 5.5) | 8.7 (3.5 – 23) | 4.3 (3.4 – 6.2) |"]
- limitation (authors): Interpolating a 1D rotation curve to estimate V_c can mis-estimate circular velocity where strong non-circular motions are present (such as in the barred regions of NGC 1365 and NGC 7496), leading to an underestimation of the Toomre length… [PHANGS-ALMA: "This approach may lead to mis-estimation of $V_c$ where strong non-circular motions are present and contribute to the measured rotation curve (within the barred regions of NGC 1365 and NGC 7496, for example). The result is that the Toomre length may be underestimated at these radii (see $\S$ [3.2](#sec:jeanstoomre))."]
- limitation (authors): In IC 5332, the 7m+tp moment-2 map at 1.5 kpc resolution contains unresolved bulk motion, requiring the use of a single minimum velocity dispersion (1.25 km s^-1) as an upper bound across the galaxy. [PHANGS-ALMA: "For IC 5332, we expect the velocity dispersion measured from the 7m+tp moment-2 map at 1.5 kpc resolution to contain unresolved bulk motion. - limitation (authors): With the chosen FilFinder parameters, many visibly long filaments are segmented, making the masks suitable for measuring spacings rather than individual filament lengths or geometries. ### 2021MNRAS.503.1459B: Dynamical model of an obscuring clumpy torus in AGNs - I. Velocity and velocity dispersion maps for interpretation of ALMA observations (2021; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; theory_or_simulation_compared_to_alma)
- question: Develop a dynamical N-body model of a self-gravitating obscuring clumpy torus in an AGN to construct velocity, velocity dispersion, and temperature maps and interpret ALMA and VLTI/MIDI observations.
- step 1: Perform N-body simulations of a self-gravitating clumpy torus around a SMBH
  - method: Direct N-body numerical integration using high-order Hermite integration and hierarchical block time steps in the phi-GPU code, evolving random initial Keplerian orbits with a polar wind cone cut-off up to 1000 orbital periods. [\varphi-GPU code: "This package uses a high order Hermite integration scheme and individual block time steps (the code supports time integration of particle orbits with schemes of 4$^{\rm th}$, 6$^{\rm th}$ and even 8$^{\rm th}$ order)."] [Results of N-body simulations: "Fig. [6](#fig:density_plot) shows the density plots of all $N$=128k clouds in projection on the meridian co-moving plane at $t = 0, 2, 48, 115, 320, 1000$ orbital periods."]
  - software: phi-GPU
  - parameter: Units: G = M_smbh = R = 1; N = 8k, 16k, 32k, 64k, 128k; softening parameter epsilon = 0.01; integration accuracy parameter tau = 0.01 [Initial conditions: "In the following we will use the system of units $G=M_{\text{smbh}}=R=1$, where $G$ is the gravitational constant and $M_{\text{smbh}}$ is the SMBH mass."] [Initial conditions: "The results of the simulations do not differ substantially by varying $\varepsilon$, so we use $\varepsilon = 0.01$ in the following simulations."]
  - parameter: Initial orbital elements: a_k in [0.6, 1.4], e_k in [0, 0.9], i_k in [0, pi/2 - theta], wind half-opening angle theta = 30 deg or 45 deg, Omega_k, omega_k, nu_k uniform in [0, 2pi] [Initial conditions: "We leave the half-opening angle $\theta$ of the wind cone as a free parameter, experimenting with two values ($30^\circ$, $45^\circ$)."] [Initial conditions: "In such a system of units we set the semi-major axes of particles in the range: $a_k=[0.6,..,1.4]$, so that the mean radius of the system in the equatorial plane $a_{\text{mean}}=R=1$ (an analogue of the major radius of the torus)."]
  - assumption: Dissipationless system (cloud collisions and radiation pressure on dynamics neglected), equal cloud masses m_k, torus-to-SMBH mass ratio M_torus / M_smbh = 0.02 [Initial conditions: "In our $N$-body simulations the cloud masses share the same value $m_k$. Resting on observational data, we also chose the torus–mass–to–SMBH–mass ratio $M_\text{torus}/M_{\text{smbh}} = 0.02$."] [Results of N-body simulations: "These oscillations have been derived under the assumption that the system is dissipationless."]
  - fitting: Gaussian fit along z-axis and log-normal fit along radial (rho) axis to the equilibrium cloud density distribution to determine effective radius r_eff containing 50% of particles. [Results of N-body simulations: "Now, we estimate the size of the resulting distribution by the best-fitting Gaussian function along the $z$-axis:"] [Results of N-body simulations: "and by log-normal law along the $\rho$ axis:"]
  - validation: Total energy conservation checked across 6 values of tau (0.020 to 0.001) on the N=8k model; total energy conserved up to a few x 10^-4 and virial parameter oscillates around zero. [Results of N-body simulations: "For this reason we start the $N$=8k model with 6 different values of $\tau$ = 0.020, 0.010, 0.007, 0.005, 0.002, 0.001."] [Results of N-body simulations: "The total energy is conserved with a good accuracy, up to a few $\times 10^{-4}$ for each numerical experiment corresponding to a given number of the clouds in the system (fig. [2](#fig:energy))."]
- limitation (authors): The torus-to-SMBH mass ratio is fixed to M_torus/M_smbh = 0.02 rather than varied as a free parameter. [Initial conditions: "Actually, the ratio $M_{\text{torus}}/M_{\text{smbh}}$ should be a free parameter."]
- limitation (authors): Simulations assume a dissipationless system and neglect cloud collisions and tidal shearing/merging. [Results of N-body simulations: "These oscillations have been derived under the assumption that the system is dissipationless. Were collisions included in the simulation, the result might be different."]
- limitation (authors): Radiation pressure on cloud dynamics near the accretion disk is neglected. [Box-orbits as an explanation for the os…: "Obviously, the dynamics of clouds located near the accretion disk can be also influenced by the radiation pressure (cite: Plewa2013; Venanzi2020). However we neglect here this effect, leaving its investigation to a future paper."]

[Evolution of mm/X-ray ratio & intrinsic…: "\log(\kappa_{2-10}) = C \times \log(\lambda_{\rm Edd})^2 + B \times \log(\lambda_{\rm Edd}) + A"] [Evolution of mm/X-ray ratio & intrinsic…: "where $C = 0.054 \pm 0.034$, $B = 0.309 \pm 0.095$ and $A = 1.538 \pm 0.063$."]

  - validation: Median correction compared with κ = 20 from (cite: VF09); large intrinsic dispersion acknowledged. [Evolution of mm/X-ray ratio & intrinsic…: "The $\kappa_{2-10}$ could be tentative due to the large intrinsic dispersion"] [Evolution of mm/X-ray ratio & intrinsic…: "the obtained value of the bolometric correction is very close to the commonly used value $\kappa = 20$ from (cite: VF09)."]

[Synchrotron emission from X-ray corona: "the fluxes from archival low-resolution observations (cite: Inoue18) represent strict upper limits to the flux coming from the inner region probed by the high-resolution ALMA observations."]

  - validation: A fixed rc = 40 bright-state model fails to reproduce the spectral index and requires unphysical non-thermal electron energy. ### 2026ApJ..1005...94N: Dynamical Evidence for a Billion Solar-mass Black Hole in Galaxy NGC 4061 from ALMA <SUP>12</SUP>CO(2─1) Kinematics (2026; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the dynamical mass of the supermassive black hole in the massive early-type galaxy NGC 4061, and does it resolve the discrepancy between previous indirect estimates based on conflicting velocity dispersion measurements?
- step 4: Fit 3D ALMA 12CO(2-1) data cube kinematics with KinMS and MCMC to measure SMBH mass and M/L
  - method: Forward-model the 3D CO data cube using KinMS with circular velocities computed via JamPy mge_vcirc from the combined SMBH + stellar MGE + ISM MGE potential, using both SkySampler CLEAN-component clouds and an analytic Gaussian disk profil… [4.1 KINMS Tool: "KinMS generates a mock data cube by simulating the gas distribution (Section 4.2) and kinematics while incorporating observational effects such as beam smearing, spatial and velocity binning, and LOS projection."] [4.1 KINMS Tool: "The circular velocity as a function of radius was computed using the `mge_vcirc` routine from the Jeans Anisotropic Modeling framework (cite: Cappellari08)."]
  - software: KinMS, SkySampler, sampleClouds, transformClouds, JamPy, mge_vcirc, AdaMet
  - parameter: 9 free parameters: log(M_BH), M/L_F814W, f, i, Gamma, sigma_gas, x_c, y_c, v_off; 10^6 SkySampler particles; Gaussian disk mu = 0.09'', sigma = 0.79''; N = 78,100 constraints; noise rescaling factor (2N)^0.25 ~ 20 [4.1 KINMS Tool: "Our adopted KINMS models fit the observed data by optimizing nine free parameters."] [4.2.1 Gas Distribution Assumed by SKYSA…: "We uniformly sampled the CLEAN components with $10^6$ gas particles using the `sampleClouds` routine, reproducing the observed CO surface brightness distribution after beam convolution."]
  - assumption: Circular orbits in a razor-thin disk (scale height d = 0''), constant intrinsic velocity dispersion sigma_gas, distance D = 107.2 Mpc [4.1 KINMS Tool: "In constructing the KinMS model, we assumed the $^{12}$CO(2$-$1) gas follows circular orbits around the galactic center under the combined gravitational potentials of the SMBH, stellar, and gas components."] [4.2.1 Gas Distribution Assumed by SKYSA…: "We therefore adopted a constant $\sigma_{\rm gas}$ and modeled the gas as a geometrically thin disk with zero scale height in our KINMS simulations."]
  - fitting: Adaptive Metropolis MCMC (AdaMet) with 10^5 iterations, 20% burn-in, logarithmic sampling for M_BH and linear sampling for other parameters, chi^2 likelihood with uncertainty rescaled by (2N)^0.25 [4.3 Bayesian Inference and Priors: "Each MCMC chain consisted of $10^5$ iterations, with the first 20% discarded as burn-in. The remaining samples were used to construct the posterior probability distribution functions (PDFs)."] [4.3 Bayesian Inference and Priors: "Because the search range of the $M_{\rm BH}$ parameter spans several orders of magnitude, it was sampled logarithmically, while all other parameters were sampled linearly."]
  - validation: Inspection of MCMC chains and posterior distributions, comparison of major/minor-axis PVDs, moment maps, velocity residuals (|V_residual| <= 15 km s^-1), integrated spectra, comparison against fixed M_BH = 0, 2.0e9, 4.5e9, and 1.2e10 M_sun… [4.4 Results: "In each modeling approach, whether using SKYSAMPLER or a Gaussian surface brightness distribution, Figure 6 also includes two comparative KINMS models. - step 5: Fit molecular-gas-only SMBH scaling relations (M_BH-sigma and M_BH-M_K,bulge)

  - assumption: Log-linear scaling relations with flat priors on fitting parameters [4.7 NGC 4061 Positions on the Molecular…: "All regressions were performed in log–linear space, with all quantities converted to logarithmic units before fitting, and a flat prior for fitting parameters was also applied."]
  - fitting: Linear regression in log-linear space using SciPy linregress [4.7 NGC 4061 Positions on the Molecular…: "We then recompiled these scaling relations using only the 35 molecular-gas-based $M_{\rm BH}$ measurements by performing linear regression analyses with the LINREGRESS routine from the SCIPY package (cite: Virtanen2020)."]
  - validation: Comparison against the canonical scaling relations of Kormendy & Ho (2013) and van den Bosch (2016) [4.7 NGC 4061 Positions on the Molecular…: "Our SMBH mass estimate for NGC 4061 is consistent within the $+1\sigma$ scatter of the $M_{\rm BH}$–$\sigma$ relations compiled by (cite: Bosch2016) and (cite: Kormendy13), as shown in Figure 16."]
