### 2019MNRAS.485.4359S: WISDOM project - IV. A molecular gas dynamical measurement of the supermassive black hole mass in NGC 524 (2019; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the SMBH mass of NGC 524 from forward-modelling the ALMA CO(2-1) molecular gas kinematics, and how do it compare with the stellar dynamical value and the M-sigma relation?
- step 4: Forward-model cube with MCMC to get SMBH mass
  - method: KinMS forward model of the central 6.4"x6.4" with Gibbs-sampling MCMC, chi-squared with covariance matrix; truncated exponential gas disc; fit SMBH mass, M/L, scale length, truncation radius, flux, PA, inclination, offsets [4.3 Bayesian parameter estimation: "We use the Gibbs sampling MCMC code `KinMS_MCMC`[^4] with adaptive stepping to explore the parameter space."]
  - software: KinMS, KinMS_MCMC
  - parameter: chain length 3e5 steps, first 10% burn-in [4.3 Bayesian parameter estimation: "The final chain length is $3\times10^5$ steps, with the first $10\%$ discarded as a burn-in phase."]
  - parameter: fit region 6.4"x6.4" (720 pc) [4.3.1 Pixel–to–pixel correlations: "We therefore only fit the central $6.4\ensuremath{''}\times6.4\ensuremath{''}$ ($720\,\mathrm{pc}\times720\,\mathrm{pc}$) region of our cube."]
  - assumption: Thin axisymmetric disc, circular motions, distance 23.3 Mpc, inclination fixed to 20 deg [4.5 SMBH mass: "we re-run the fit with a fixed inclination of $20\degree$, adopted from the above arguments."]
  - fitting: Likelihood lnP = -chi2/2 with chi2 divided by sqrt(2N); uniform priors (log-uniform for SMBH mass) within physical limits; circularised MGE used to free inclination [4.3 Bayesian parameter estimation: "Samples are then drawn from the posterior distribution described by the the log-likelihood function $\ln P=-\sfrac{\chi^2}{2}$."]
  - validation: Bootstrapping 16 half-cube subsets with MPFIT gave consistent values and uncertainties [4.6 Fit uncertainties: "The best-fitting values are consistent with those estimated by the MCMC procedure, and the uncertainty estimates are reassuringly very similar."]
- result: SMBH mass (fixed inclination, formal 3 sigma) = 4.0e8 ± +1.6/-1.5 e8 Msun [4.5 SMBH mass: "Our best-fitting SMBH mass is thus $4.0^{+1.6}_{-1.5}\times10^8\,\mathrm{M_\odot}$ ($3\sigma$ formal uncertainties) with a reduced $\chi^2$ of $\chi^2_\mathrm{red} = 1.84$."]
- result: Stellar M/L_I (fixed inclination) = 5.7 ± ±0.3 (3 sigma) Msun/Lsun,I [4.5 SMBH mass: "Our best-fitting mass-to-light ratio is $5.7\pm0.3\,\mathrm{\mathrm{M_\odot}/L_{\odot,I}}$ ($3\sigma$ formal uncertainties)."]
- result: Disc position angle = 39.6 ± ±1 deg [Table of MCMC results: "| Position angle ($\degree$) | $\phantom{0.}0\;\rightarrow\;359$ | 39.9 | 39.6 | $\pm1$ |"]
- result: Inclination from circularised MGE fit = 15 ± +8/-5 deg [4.5 SMBH mass: "we find an inclination of $15\degree^{+8\degree}_{-5\degree}$"]
- result: Sphere of influence radius = 36 pc (0.31") ± not stated pc [4.5 SMBH mass: "This yields a sphere of influence radius of $36\,\mathrm{pc}$ ($0.31\ensuremath{''}$)"]
- result: Truncation radius (fixed inclination) = 0.51 ± ±0.07 arcsec [Table of MCMC results: "| Truncation radius ($\ensuremath{''}$) | $\phantom{0.}0\;\rightarrow\;\phantom{0}10$ | 0.53 | 0.52 | $\pm0.07$ | $\phantom{0.}0\;\rightarrow\;\phantom{0}10$ | 0.51 | $\pm0.07$ |"]
- limitation (authors): Central hole means no Keplerian rise observed; mass relies on stellar mass model [4.7.3 Mass model: "Since the molecular gas disc has a central hole, we do not capture the Keplerian increase of the rotation velocities where the SMBH dominates the mass distribution."]
- limitation (authors): Model chi2 exceeds expectation, indicating unexplained features [4.8 Discussion: "We in fact find our best fitting model has a larger $\chi^2$, indicating there are features in the data unexplained by our relatively simple model."]
- limitation (authors): Distance uncertainty (~10%) not included in the systematic error [4.7.1 Distance: "but since this is a simple normalisation, we follow standard practice and do not include it in our systematic uncertainty."]
- not stated (card): Smooth-masking clip threshold and cube-smoothing details for moment maps; Line-free channel ranges for uvcontsub; CLEAN threshold, niter, and deconvolver used; Flux calibrator and flux-scale uncertainty; CASA/pipeline versions; Initial values, adaptive step settings and acceptance fraction for KinMS_MCMC; Mask used to define the fitted region in the cube and the number of pixels N; Details of the…

### 2021A&A...654A..24B: Molecular gas kinematics in the nuclear region of nearby Seyfert galaxies with ALMA (2021; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the CO(2-1) molecular gas kinematics in the central ~1 kpc of three Seyfert galaxies, and does it show non-circular motions linked to AGN fueling or feedback (versus a bar)?
- step 8: Compute gravity torques and resonances for NGC 4968
  - method: Derive the potential from the deprojected HST H-band stellar distribution via the Poisson equation, compute forces and torques on the CO gas per pixel, azimuthally average to get the relative angular momentum change per rotation [7.2 Torque computation for NGC 4968: "The forces are computed at each pixel, by derivating the potential, and then the torques are computed on the gas, taking into account the gas density at the given pixel."] [7.2 Torque computation for NGC 4968: "From the torque map we compute azimuthal averages and obtain the effective torque at each radius, per unit mass."]
  - parameter: Deprojection with inclination 60 deg and PA 250 deg; HST and CO images resampled to 0.03 arcsec = 6 pc pixels [7.2 Torque computation for NGC 4968: "We then de-projected the galaxy disc with the adopted inclination angle of 60$^\circ$ and PA 250$^\circ$."] [7.2 Torque computation for NGC 4968: "have been resampled to the same pixel size of 0.03 arcsec = 6 pc"]
  - parameter: Thin disc with scale ratio h_z/h_r = 1/12 [7.2 Torque computation for NGC 4968: "assuming a thin disc of scale ratio $h_z/h_r$ =1/12"]
  - assumption: Constant mass-to-luminosity ratio calibrated to the observed rotation curve; bulge assumed spherical and not deprojected [7.2 Torque computation for NGC 4968: "We assumed a constant mass-to-luminosity ratio and calibrated it to retrieve the observed rotation curve"] [7.2 Torque computation for NGC 4968: "we have first isolated the bulge, assumed to be spherical, which should not be de-projected."]
  - assumption: Black hole mass 7e6 Msun; corotation placed just outside the bar [7.1 Kinematical perturbations: "The black hole mass has been assumed to be 7$\times10^6$ M$_\odot$, about 0.2% of the mass of the bulge"] [7.1 Kinematical perturbations: "If we place the corotation just outside the bar, as it is frequently observed in barred spirals"]
  - validation: Torque map shows the expected butterfly (four-quadrant) pattern relative to the bar orientation [7.2 Torque computation for NGC 4968: "the torque map reveals the expected butterfly diagram (four-quadrant pattern) in relation to the bar orientation"]
- result: NGC 4968 bar pattern speed (corotation placed just outside the bar) = 52 ± not stated km/s/kpc [7.1 Kinematical perturbations: "the pattern speed of the bar is $\Omega_b$ = $52\, \rm{km\,s^{-1}\,kpc^{-1}}$"]
- result: NGC 4968 corotation radius and ILR radius = corotation 3.5 kpc; ILR 300 pc ± not stated kpc / pc [7.1 Kinematical perturbations: "putting corotation at 3.5 kpc and inner Lindblad resonance (ILR) at R = 300pc"]
- result: NGC 4968 peak relative angular momentum change in one rotation outside the ring = dL/L = -0.3 ± not stated dimensionless [7.2 Torque computation for NGC 4968: "peaks at dL/L = -0.3 in one rotation"]
- limitation (authors): Some flux may be lost due to high resolution and the lack of compact-array data [6 Gas and continuum distribution: "One may argue that some flux could be lost because of the high resolution of these observations and the lack of additional compact array observations."]
- limitation (authors): The conversion factor alpha_CO is highly uncertain [5 Line luminosity and molecular gas mas…: "is highly uncertain, it depends on metallicity and environment (cite: bol+13)"]
- limitation (authors): The 3DBAROLO fits for NGC 4845 velocity and dispersion are not optimal [3.1 3D modeling of the main rotating di…: "The Barolo fits for N4845 for the velocity and the velocity dispersion are not optimal"]
- not stated (card): Which spectral windows and which channels were treated as line-free for continuum subtraction and for the continuum image, and the polynomial order.; Whether the 10 and 30 km/s cubes were used for which galaxy and product (text gives ~10 and ~30 km/s, the observation log table gives 2.5 km/s).; Number of iterations, cleaning threshold and auto-multithresh parameters for each cube; robust = 2.0 is…

### 2017ApJ...836....8T: ALMA Observations Show Major Mergers Among the Host Galaxies of Fast-growing, High-redshift Supermassive Black Holes (2017; agn_and_quasars, high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Are major mergers important drivers of rapid early SMBH growth, as tested with ALMA continuum and [C II] observations of six z≃4.8 quasar hosts, three FIR-bright and three FIR-faint?
- step 5: Compare with SF main sequence and BH-host relations; assess mergers
  - method: Compare SFR vs M_dyn with the main-sequence relation; BH growth rate from L_bol with eta=0.1; compare M_BH/M_dyn with local ellipticals; identify companions via continuum and [C II] redshift [3.3.3 SMBH-host galaxy relations: "the radiative efficiency is assumed to be η = 0.1."]
  - software: MATLAB package for astronomy and astrophysics
  - assumption: Host mass grows only via star formation at CE01-based SFR [3.3.3 SMBH-host galaxy relations: "we assume that the mass grows only due to the formation of new stars at a rate determined by the CE01-based SFRs"]
  - validation: Chance-coincidence estimates from deep-survey number counts [3.1 Source detection and identification: "cannot be attributed to chance coincidence"]
- result: M_BH/M_dyn = 1/260 to 1/10 ± not stated dimensionless [3.3.3 SMBH-host galaxy relations: "which are in the range M BH /M dyn ∼ 1/260 − 1/10"]
- limitation (authors): M_dyn is uncertain: extended emission missed, inclination from marginally resolved sizes, beam smearing [3.3.2 Host and companion galaxy masses: "our ALMA data may not be able to detect the more extended lower surface brightness [C II ]-emitting regions, thus underestimating D [C II ]"]
- limitation (authors): Small sample limits interpretation [3.4 Major mergers among hosts of fast-g…: "The small size of our sample naturally limits the scope of our interpretation"]

### 2017ApJ...845..138S: Gas Dynamics of a Luminous z = 6.13 Quasar ULAS J1319+0950 Revealed by ALMA High-resolution Observations (2017; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the gas dynamics, dynamical mass and black hole to host mass ratio of the z=6.13 quasar J1319+0950 from high-resolution [C II] and dust continuum imaging?
- step 5: Derive M_dyn, M_BH and mass ratio
  - method: M_dyn from V_c at 3.2 kpc; M_BH from Mg II width and L3000 via Shen & Liu; compare with local ratio from Kormendy & Ho [3.2.3 M_BH-M_dyn relation: "The derived $M_{\rm BH}$/$M_{\rm dyn}$ ratio is 0.020, which is about 4 times higher than the present-day $M_{\rm BH}$/$M_{\rm bulge}$ ratio"]
  - assumption: Dynamical mass taken as bulge mass for local ratio [3.2.3 M_BH-M_dyn relation: "calculated taking our dynamical mass as the bulge mass by Equation 11 in (cite: Ho2013)"]
  - fitting: Linear continuum + Gaussian Mg II + Fe II template [Discussion, M_BH-M_dyn relation: "we fit the Gemini NIRI spectrum of ULAS J1319+0950 (cite: Mortlock2009) with a linear continuum, a Gaussian for Mg II line, and an ultraviolet Fe II template based on (cite: Shen2012)"]
  - validation: Errors from i = 26 and 48 deg [Discussion, M_BH-M_dyn relation: "And as a sequence, the $M_{\rm BH}$/$M_{\rm dyn}$ ratio would be 0.013 and 0.033, which are 2 and 7 times higher than the local values."]
- result: Dynamical mass within 3.2 kpc = 13.4 ± +7.8/-5.3 1e10 Msun [abstract: "13.4$_{-5.3}^{+7.8}$ $\times 10^{10}\,M_{\odot}$"]
- result: M_BH = 2.7e9 ± 0.6e9 Msun [3.2.3 M_BH-M_dyn relation: "is (2.7 $\pm$ 0.6) $\times$ 10$^{9} M_{\odot}$"]
- result: M_BH/M_dyn = 0.020 ± +0.013/-0.007 dimensionless [abstract: "0.020$_{-0.007}^{+0.013}$"]
- limitation (authors): Inclination derived from only two successful rings; real uncertainty may be much larger [3.2.2 Rotation curve: "the inclination angle ($i$) is calculated as the weighted mean of only two successful $i(R)$ values."]
- not stated (card): Cycle 0 execution blocks and calibration details; Image size, cell size, cleaning threshold and mask; Line-free channels for continuum subtraction; Rest-frame velocity zero point and moment-map pixel handling; How M_dyn is computed from V_c (formula, e.g. M = V^2 R/G); Initial values for ROTCUR fit; Continuum bandwidth/frequency

### 2021Sci...372.1201T: Spiral morphology in an intensely star-forming disk galaxy more than 12 billion years ago (2021; high_z_galaxies; archival_reuse)
- question: When in cosmic history did the distinct internal structures of spiral galaxies (stellar bulge, rotating disk, and spiral arms) form, and are they present in an intensely star-forming galaxy at z = 4.41?
- step 5: 3D gas dynamical modeling of bulge and disk masses
  - method: Generate gas particles from CLEAN components using SkySampler, build 3D dynamical model cubes in KinMS with a spherical de Vaucouleurs bulge + thick pressure-supported exponential disk gravitational potential, and fit directly to the obser… [Gas dynamical modeling: "We used the K IN MS code ( _40_ ) to produce a gas dynamical model cube which has the same beam size and velocity resolution as those in the observed cube (i.e. beam smearing effect was taken into account for the model cube)."] [Supplementary Materials, Gas dynamical …: "reproduce the [C II ] line intensity profile of BRI 1335-0417 by using the de-convolved CLEAN components that were provided by a task of tclean in C ASA ."]
  - software: KinMS, SkySampler, emcee, CASA
  - parameter: R_d = 1.83 kpc; velocity range -390 to +370 km s^-1; N ~ 4 x 10^4 constraints (dof = 42400); 8 free parameters; 180 walkers, 4000 steps, 600 burn-in steps [MCMC fitting: "The likelihood was computed using the observed cube over the velocity range from -390 km s [-1] to +370 km s [-1] with the velocity center 0 km s [-1] matched to the frequency of 351.470 GHz in barycentric frame."] [Results of the gas dynamical modeling: "We run the MCMC fitting with 180 walkers and 4000 steps. Because the first 600 steps are not converged to the posterior distribution (a burn-in phase) by MCMC sampling, these first steps were excluded from the analysis."]
  - assumption: Dark matter halo excluded within 5 kpc; black hole mass included in spherical de Vaucouleurs bulge; axisymmetric exponential disk with R_d = 1.83 kpc; isotropic spatially uniform velocity dispersion; hydrostatic asymmetric drift and disk t… [Gas dynamical modeling: "In this object, the [C II ] line emission is detected only up to 5 kpc, which is not sufficient to measure the mass distribution of the dark matter using the kinematics. We therefore excluded the dark matter mass distribution from our dynamical model."] [Rotation velocity of pressurized galact…: "Because the velocity dispersion is constant over this galaxy except in the central region [Fig. 1 (E and F) and Fig. S1(G and H)], we adopted an isotropic and spatially uniform velocity dispersion."]
  - fitting: Bayesian MCMC with emcee; Gaussian prior on inclination (37.8 ± 1.6°), log-uniform priors on M_bulge and M_disk, uniform priors on R_e, PA, dx, dy, dv; chi^2 scaled by sqrt(2N); reduced chi^2 = 1.69 (dof = 42400) [MCMC fitting: "We used log uniform prior distribution for the mass of the bulge _M_ bulge and disk _M_ disk to cover the multiple orders of magnitude, while uniform prior distribution for the other 5 parameters."] [MCMC fitting: "We used a method of scaling the standard Dc [2] by a factor of,(2 _N_ ) ( _68_ )."]
  - validation: Tested sensitivity of M_bulge and M_disk to varied fixed R_d values and to letting R_d be a free parameter (Fig. S4); checked integrated [C II] spectrum residuals for outflows (Fig. S5) [Results of the gas dynamical modeling: "We investigated the dependence of _M_ bulge and _M_ disk on the disk scale radius _R_ d using several different values of _R_ d . To compare with them, we also derived _M_ bulge and _M_ disk by adding the disk scale radius as a free parameter in our MCMC fitting."] [CO (J=7->6) outflow: "We could not find any evidence of bipolar outflow in [C II ] line emission, but this does not exclude the bipolar outflow seen in CO ( _J_ =7→6) line emission because the [C II ] line may not be strongly emitted from the outflowing material."]
- result: Central compact mass (M_bulge) = 8.9 ± +20.9/-3.7 10^9 M_sun [Table S2: "Central compact mass (10 [.] 𝑀 ⨀ ) _M_ bulge [10 [-9], 10 [4] ] 8.9 -3.7, +20.9"]
- result: Central compact mass effective radius (R_e) = <1.33 ± 95% confidence interval kpc [Table S2: "_R_ e - (<1.33)"]
- result: Disk mass (M_disk) = 4.9 ± +1.7/-2.5 10^10 M_sun [Table S2: "Disk mass (10 [/0] 𝑀 ⨀ ) _M_ disk [10 [-10], 10 [3] ] 4.9 -2.5, +1.7"]
- result: Dynamical model disk position angle (Gamma) = 7.6 ± +7.3/-7.1 degree [Table S2: "Position angle (degree) G [-20, 20] 7.6 -7.1, +7.3"]
- result: Dynamical model disk inclination (i) = 37.3 ± +3.0/-3.1 degree [Table S2: "Inclination angle (degree) _i_ (37.8, ±1.6) 37.3 -3.1, +3.0"]
- limitation (authors): Spatial resolution (~0.2") is much larger than the black hole sphere of influence (0.02"), so the black hole mass and stellar bulge mass cannot be separated dynamically (allowing a black hole without a bulge). [Gas dynamical modeling: "Because estimated _R_ SOI is much smaller than our spatial resolution, we cannot measure the circular velocity of black hole _v_ BH ( _r_ ) and bulge _v_ bulge ( _r_ ) separately. We thus included the black hole mass in the bulge."]
- limitation (authors): Gas inflow into the galaxy center driven by spiral torques cannot be directly detected due to limited signal-to-noise ratio. [main text: "Such gas inflow could not be directly detected in our data due to the limited signal to noise ratio."]
- limitation (authors): Pixel-to-pixel covariance matrix correction in the data cube was omitted because it is negligible compared to the sqrt(2N) log-likelihood scaling. [MCMC fitting: "The correction by the inverse covariance matrix for the pixel to pixel correlations ( _71_ ) in the observed data cube is not taken into account, because it is negligible compared to the effect by scaling the logarithmic likelihood."]
- not stated (card): Exact pixel size (cellsize in arcsec) and image dimensions in pixels used in tclean; Exact frequency ranges of the line-free channels selected by hif_findcont and around 352.3 GHz; Exact velocity range integrated to create the [C II] moment 0 map

### 2013ApJ...770...13W: Redshift 6.4 Host Galaxies of 10<SUP>8</SUP> Solar Mass Black Holes: Low Star Formation Rate and Dynamical Mass (2013; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the star formation rates, [C II] properties and dynamical masses of the host galaxies of two z=6.4 quasars with ~1e8 Msun black holes?
- step 5: Compare black hole mass to dynamical mass
  - method: Convert FWHM to sigma and use local M_BH-sigma relation of Gultekin et al. 2009 to predict M_BH [3.3 [C ii] dynamics and spatial extent: "Based on the local relationship (Gultekin et al. 2009) a galaxy with $\sigma=80$ km s$^{-1}$ would be expected to have $M_{\rm BH} \approx 3 \times 10^6 \,{\rm M}_\odot$"]
  - assumption: Gaussian, no inclination correction [3.3 [C ii] dynamics and spatial extent: "equivalent to $\sigma=80 \pm 8$ km s$^{-1}$ for a Gaussian and ignoring any inclination correction"]
- result: Predicted M_BH from sigma=80 km/s = ~3e6 ± not stated Msun [3.3 [C ii] dynamics and spatial extent: "would be expected to have $M_{\rm BH} \approx 3 \times 10^6 \,{\rm M}_\odot$, a factor of 25 lower"]
- limitation (authors): Source only marginally resolved; intrinsic size poorly constrained and inclination unknown [3.3 [C ii] dynamics and spatial extent: "The intrinsic source size is not well constrained as it is only marginally resolved"]
- limitation (authors): L_FIR uncertainties exclude dust temperature uncertainty [3.1 Far-infrared luminosity: "uncertainties on $L_{\rm FIR}$ (and inferred SFR) only include the flux measurement uncertainties, not that of the dust temperature."]
- not stated (card): Imaging weighting/robust, deconvolution and cleaning parameters; CASA version and calibration script/flux model for Uranus; Exact channel ranges for line, wings, and continuum spectral windows; Method and aperture for the 3.4 sigma continuum flux measurement and spectral extraction; How [C II] flux integration and line luminosity conversion were done; Fit details for the Gaussian plus constant sp…

### 2025ApJ...989..160I: MIDIS: MIRI Uncovers Virgil, the First Little Red Dot with Clear Detection of Its Host Galaxy at z ≃ 6.6 (2025; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Nature of Virgil, a MIRI extremely red object and Lyman-alpha emitter at z=6.63 in the HUDF: stellar populations versus AGN, and whether it is a Little Red Dot; ALMA (ASPECS) only supplies 1.2 mm and 3.0 mm non-detections
- step 11: Estimate AGN bolometric luminosity and black hole mass
  - method: L_AGN,bol from the dust-corrected AGN L(5100 A) using the best-fit CIGALE and synthesizer-AGN models; M_BH from the Eddington relation [Stellar Populations and AGN models: "we derive $L_{\rm AGN,bol}$ from the monochromatic luminosity at 5100Å (corrected for dust extinction) $L$(5100Å) as $L_{\rm AGN, bol} \approx 9 \cdot \lambda \cdot L$(5100Å)"] [Stellar Populations and AGN models: "we can infer $M_{\rm BH}$ via the relation $M_{\rm BH} = 7.9 \times 10^{-39} \times (L_{\rm AGN,bol} / \lambda_{\rm Edd})$"]
  - software: CIGALE, synthesizer-AGN
  - parameter: AGN contributes 40 percent of observed 5100 A emission; A(5100 A) ~ 3.3 mag (CIGALE) [Stellar Populations and AGN models: "at 5100Å (rest-frame) the AGN contributes only to 40 percent of the observed emission"] [Stellar Populations and AGN models: "dust attenuation is $A(5100{\rm \AA}) \approx 3.3\rm~mag$"]
  - assumption: Eddington ratio 0.1-1 [Stellar Populations and AGN models: "Considering typical values for the Eddington ratio $\lambda_{\rm Edd} = 0.1 - 1$"]
  - validation: Agreement with bolometric luminosities of z > 4 JWST AGNs [Stellar Populations and AGN models: "These estimates are in good agreement with the values of bolometric luminosity of AGNs at $z > 4$ recently studied with JWST"]
- result: AGN bolometric luminosity (CIGALE) = 1.1e45 ± not stated erg/s [Stellar Populations and AGN models: "we estimate $L_{\rm AGN}({\rm 5100\AA}) \approx 2.4 \times 10^{40}\rm~ erg~s^{-1}~\AA^{-1}$ that converts into an $L_{\rm AGN,bol} \approx 1.1 \times 10^{45}\rm~ erg~s^{-1}$"]
- result: AGN bolometric luminosity (synthesizer-AGN) = 8.9e44 ± not stated erg/s [Stellar Populations and AGN models: "and $L_{\rm AGN,bol} \approx 8.9 \times 10^{44}\rm~ erg~s^{-1}$"]
- result: Black hole mass for lambda_Edd = 1 and 0.1 = (7-9)e6 (lambda_Edd=1); (7-9)e7 (lambda_Edd=0.1) ± not stated Msun [Stellar Populations and AGN models: "$M_{\rm BH} \approx (7 - 9)\times 10^6\rm~ M_\odot$ ($\lambda_{\rm Edd} = 1$) to $M_{\rm BH} \approx (7 - 9) \times 10^7\rm~ M_\odot$ ($\lambda_{\rm Edd} = 0.1$)"]
- limitation (authors): Small size, coarse MIRI resolution and contaminant prevent testing the two-component dusty starburst scenario [Only Stellar Populations models: "prevent us, however, from further investigating this scenario based on the currently available data"]
- limitation (authors): Shallow MIRI depth above 18 um prevents confirming the flattening [The impact of long-wavelengths MIRI fil…: "The shallower depth of the available MIRI filters $\lambda > 18~\mu$m prevents us from robustly confirming the flattening on a longer wavelength range."]
- limitation (authors): Depth estimates not corrected for aperture [Introduction: "The depth estimates are not corrected for aperture."]
- not stated (card): ALMA part: how the 3 sigma upper limits (27.9 uJy at 1.2 mm, 4.2 uJy at 3.0 mm) were measured (aperture, position, beam or primary-beam treatment); they are given with a citation; ASPECS product version and which maps (mosaic, noise map, primary-beam corrected or not) were downloaded; no ALMA project codes are printed; SExtractor and SEP detection and deblending parameters beyond K=2.5 and minimu…

### 2022MNRAS.514.6215Y: Is there a sub-parsec-scale jet base in the nearby dwarf galaxy NGC 4395? (2022; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Whether the sub-mJy outflow-like feature E in the dwarf galaxy NGC 4395 is a continuous jet with a sub-parsec-scale jet base physically coupled with the intermediate-mass black hole accretion disc.
- step 4: Derive multi-frequency radio spectra, brightness temperatures, luminosity limits, and physical scale of the torus
  - method: Combine HSA, EVN, VLA, ALMA, and literature flux densities to fit power-law spectra S(nu) proportional to nu^alpha for components E and C, compute brightness temperatures and 5-sigma radio luminosity upper limits at the Gaia IMBH position,… [Introduction: "We define the spectral index $\alpha$ with the power-law spectrum $S(\nu) \propto \nu^{\alpha}$."] [Nature of the components E and C: "The broad-band radio spectrum can be described by a single power law function. The best-fitting model for the non-simultaneous spectrum between 1.4 and 237 GHz is $S_{\nu} = (0.32\pm0.08) \nu^{-0.12\pm0.08}$."]
  - assumption: Standard LambdaCDM cosmology with H0 = 71 km s^-1 Mpc^-1, Omega_m = 0.27, Omega_Lambda = 0.73, giving luminosity distance 4.5 Mpc (scale ~0.021 pc mas^-1 at 4.3 Mpc) [Introduction: "Throughout the paper, a standard $\Lambda$CDM cosmological model with H$_\mathrm{0}$ = 71 km s$^{-1}$ Mpc$^{-1}$, $\Omega_\mathrm{m}$ = 0.27, $\Omega_{\Lambda}$ = 0.73 is adopted. For our target, this gives a luminosity distance of 4.5 Mpc, fully in agreement with the Cepheid distance $4.3\pm0.3$ Mpc presented by (cit…"]
  - fitting: Single power-law fit to non-simultaneous multi-frequency flux densities. [Nature of the components E and C: "The broad-band radio spectrum can be described by a single power law function. The best-fitting model for the non-simultaneous spectrum between 1.4 and 237 GHz is $S_{\nu} = (0.32\pm0.08) \nu^{-0.12\pm0.08}$."]
  - validation: Compared derived spectral index of E (-0.64 ± 0.05) with earlier arcsec-resolution measurement (-0.60 ± 0.08) by Ho & Ulvestad (2001). [Multi-frequency imaging results: "Assuming no significant time variability, we can derive a spectral index of $\alpha=-0.64\pm0.05$ for the component E in Fig. 4. This is in agreement with the early report $\alpha=-0.60\pm0.08$ observed by (cite: Ho2001) between 1.4 and 5 GHz with their arcsec-resolution images."]
- result: Component E average brightness temperature at 1.4 GHz = 2.3e6 ± ±0.4e6 K [Multi-frequency imaging results: "All these results are consistent with the HSA imaging results reported previously by (cite: Wrobel2006) and further confirms that it is a non-thermal component with an average brightness temperature of $T_{\rm b}=(2.3\pm0.4) \times10^6$ K at 1.4 GHz."]
- result: Component E multi-frequency spectral index between 1.4 and 15 GHz = -0.64 ± ±0.05 dimensionless [Multi-frequency imaging results: "Assuming no significant time variability, we can derive a spectral index of $\alpha=-0.64\pm0.05$ for the component E in Fig. 4."]
- result: Component C broad-band spectral index between 1.4 and 237 GHz = -0.12 ± ±0.08 dimensionless [Nature of the components E and C: "The best-fitting model for the non-simultaneous spectrum between 1.4 and 237 GHz is $S_{\nu} = (0.32\pm0.08) \nu^{-0.12\pm0.08}$."]
- result: Component C average brightness temperature at 15 GHz = 56 ± ±9 K [Nature of the components E and C: "Firstly, its average brightness temperatures are very low: $T_{\rm b}=56\pm9$ K in the 15 GHz VLA map and $\leq5.9\times10^5$ K in the 1.4-GHz HSA and 5-GHz EVN maps."]
- result: Component C radio luminosity at 15 GHz = 8.6e34 ± ±1.0e34 erg s^-1 [Implications: "The component C has a radio luminosity of $L_{\rm R} = (8.6 \pm 1.0) \times 10^{34}$ erg s$^{-1}$ at 15 GHz."]
- result: 5-sigma upper limit on compact feature peak brightness at 5 GHz (EVN) = <=0.035 ± not stated mJy beam^-1 [Multi-frequency imaging results: "This gives us a 5$\sigma$ upper limit of 0.035 mJy beam$^{-1}$ ($T_{\rm b}= 5.9\times10^5$ K) for the peak brightness at 5 GHz of any compact feature."]
- result: 5-sigma upper limit on jet base radio luminosity at 1.4 GHz = <=1.3e33 ± not stated erg s^-1 [Implications: "If there is a compact jet base formed by the central accreting IMBH of NGC 4395, the HSA and EVN non-detections at the *Gaia* position allow us to set the 5$\sigma$ upper limits on its luminosity, $L_{\rm R} = \nu L_{\nu} = 1.3\times10^{33}$ erg s$^{-1}$ at 1.4 GHz and $L_{\rm R} = 4.7\times10^{33}$ erg s$^{-1}$ at 5.…"]
- result: 5-sigma upper limit on jet base radio luminosity at 5.0 GHz = <=4.7e33 ± not stated erg s^-1 [Implications: "If there is a compact jet base formed by the central accreting IMBH of NGC 4395, the HSA and EVN non-detections at the *Gaia* position allow us to set the 5$\sigma$ upper limits on its luminosity, $L_{\rm R} = \nu L_{\nu} = 1.3\times10^{33}$ erg s$^{-1}$ at 1.4 GHz and $L_{\rm R} = 4.7\times10^{33}$ erg s$^{-1}$ at 5.…"]
- result: Upper limit on transverse speed of component E = <0.01 ± not stated c [Nature of the components E and C: "The non-detection of the significant offset between the two HSA observations supports that the component E has a very low transverse speed, $<$0.01 $c$, where $c$ is the light speed."]
- limitation (authors): In-band spectral indices from the 12–18 GHz VLA data have large uncertainties due to the limited observing bandwidth and faintness of the two components. [Multi-frequency imaging results: "The estimates of the in-band spectral indices have a large uncertainty because of the very limited observing bandwidth and the faintness of the two components."]
- limitation (authors): The ALMA 237 GHz size estimate of NGC 4395 may not be reliable because of the very limited image quality (SNR = 4.6). [Multi-frequency imaging results: "It is a slightly resolved feature with a size of ($0.89 \pm 0.21$) $\times$ ($0.72 \pm 0.14$) mas. Because of the very limited image quality (SNR = 4.6), the size estimate may not be reliable."]
- limitation (authors): The broad-band spectral index estimate for component C uses non-simultaneous data with 10–50 per cent flux uncertainties, is only sensitive to large (>=30 per cent) variability, and may be biased by different image resolutions and possible… [Nature of the components E and C: "Because of the large uncertainty (10–50 percent) of these flux density measurements, the spectral index estimate is only sensitive to large ($\ensuremath{\gtrsim}$30 per cent) flux density variability. We caution that the estimate also has a certain bias because of different image resolutions and possible non-uniform …"]
- not stated (card): ALMA imaging parameters: deconvolution task (e.g., tclean), weighting scheme/robust parameter, cell size, image size, masking, and synthesized beam dimensions for each epoch and the combined image.; Exact spectral windows and line-free channel ranges selected to construct the 6.9 GHz ALMA continuum.; Why the 2018 August 28 ALMA epoch is omitted from the individual detection flux density reporting…

### 2025A&A...701A..41D: Millimeter emission from supermassive black hole coronae (2025; agn_and_quasars; theory_or_simulation_compared_to_alma)
- question: Provide a framework to derive physical properties of SMBH coronae by modelling radio-to-far-infrared SEDs, including the mm synchrotron emission, and test it on a sample of radio-quiet AGN
- step 2: Fit the multi-component SED with the corona model
  - method: Total SED = corona + free-free + diffuse synchrotron + dust, fitted with Bilby using the emcee MCMC sampler; fits done in log for quantities spanning orders of magnitude; epsilon_B tied to delta and T_c tied to tau_T when no X-ray determin… [2.2 Parameter fitting: "for fitting the SED using the standard sampler for Markov Chain Monte Carlo"] [2.2 Parameter fitting: "We note that for quantities that can span several orders of magnitude, we fit the logarithm of the quantity."]
  - software: Bilby, emcee, astropy, Margalit & Quataert thermal-synchrotron code, sed_fitter_BHcorona
  - parameter: Fixed per source: M_BH, and often tau_T, kT_c, p, alpha_sy, beta, nu_tau1 (e.g. beta = 1.78, nu_tau1 = 800 GHz for GRS 1734-292) [B.1 GRS 1734-292: "so we fix $\beta=1.78$ and $\nu_{\tau_1}=800$ GHz"]
  - parameter: Free parameters mainly r_c and log delta; parameter ranges listed in the Table of model parameters [2.2 Parameter fitting: "We summarise all the model parameters and the range of values that they can take in Table"]
  - assumption: eta = 1 (equipartition between non-thermal particles and magnetic field), xi_e,p = 40, so epsilon_B = 41 delta [2.1 Corona emission: "We additionally fix $\eta=1$, corresponding to an energy equipartition condition between non-thermal particles and magnetic fields."] [2.1 Corona emission: "In this way, we remove a free parameter by the condition $\epsilon_B = 41 \, \delta$."]
  - assumption: Corona treated as a spherical one-zone region, components independent and summed; f-f absorption, AME and dust absorption of the corona neglected [2 Emission model: "We assume that these components are independent of each other and that the total emission is simply the sum of all components."] [2 Emission model: "We thus neglect this effect in our model fitting."]
  - assumption: Luminosity distance from Planck18 cosmology in astropy [2.1 Corona emission: "to convert $z$ to a luminosity distance"]
  - fitting: Bilby with emcee MCMC; errors are 16th-84th percentile of the posterior; priors given in the parameter table (ranges); likelihood form not stated [2.2 Parameter fitting: "the quoted errorbars correspond to the 16th–84th percentile range"]
  - validation: Sizes checked against an analytic estimate of Shablovinskaya et al. 2024, and results checked against Inoue & Doi 2018 with the same fixed parameters [2.2 Parameter fitting: "we verified that the sizes derived with this approach agree well with the simple analytical estimate from Eq. 4 in (cite: Shablovinskaya2024)"] [2.2 Parameter fitting: "we confirmed that our results are consistent with those reported by (cite: Inoue2018) when fixing the same parameters as in their work"]
- step 4: Explore parameter dependence of the corona SED
  - method: Vary one parameter at a time around a reference case, then with tied parameters, then vary M_BH and redshift (mu = 10) [3.1 Parameter exploration: "First, we vary one parameter at a time."]
  - parameter: Reference case: D = 100 Mpc, M_BH = 1e8 Msun, tau_T = 0.25, kT_c = 166 keV, r_c = 140, epsilon_B = 1.0, delta = 0.01, p = 2.7 [3.1 Parameter exploration: "$D = 100$ Mpc, $M_\mathrm{BH} = 10^8$ M$_\odot$, $\tau_\mathrm{T}=0.25$, $kT_\mathrm{c}=166$ keV, $r_\mathrm{c}=140$, $\epsilon_B=1.0$, $\delta=0.01$, and $p=2.7$"]
- step 5: Model the M_BH versus 220 GHz luminosity correlation
  - method: Compute model coronal emission at rest-frame 220 GHz over a range of M_BH and overlay on the empirical correlation of Ruffa et al. 2024, varying delta and r_c [3.4 M_BH-L_220GHz correlation modelling: "we can use our corona emission model to calculate the corona emission at a rest-frame frequency of 220 GHz"]
  - validation: Qualitative comparison with the spread of the observed correlation [3.4 M_BH-L_220GHz correlation modelling: "we can broadly explain the spread on the observed correlation when taking into account the parameter space available in $\delta$ and $r_\mathrm{c}$"]
- result: GRS 1734-292 corona radius r_c = 112 ± +/-14 R_g [B.1 GRS 1734-292: "We obtain $r_\mathrm{c}=112 \pm 14$ ($R_\mathrm{c}\approx0.5$ ld)"]
- result: GRS 1734-292 log delta = -1.74 ± +/-0.13 dimensionless [B.1 GRS 1734-292: "$\log{\delta}=-1.74 \pm 0.13$"]
- result: GRS 1734-292 r_c in the higher-flux epoch (delta fixed) = 178 ± +/-9 R_g [B.1 GRS 1734-292: "the fit yields $r_\mathrm{c}=178 \pm 9$"]
- result: IC 4329A corona radius r_c = 261 ± +141/-45 R_g [B.2 IC 4329A: "We obtain $r_\mathrm{c}=261^{+141}_{-45}$ and $\log{\delta}=-2.25^{+0.24}_{-0.48}$"]
- result: IC 4329A log delta = -2.25 ± +0.24/-0.48 dimensionless [B.2 IC 4329A: "$\log{\delta}=-2.25^{+0.24}_{-0.48}$"]
- result: MCG-06-30-15 corona radius r_c = 115 ± +/-6 R_g [B.3 MCG-06-30-15: "We obtain $r_\mathrm{c}=115\pm6$ ($R_\mathrm{c}\approx 0.13$ ld) and $\log{\delta}=-1.98 \pm 0.06$"]
- result: MCG-06-30-15 log delta = -1.98 ± +/-0.06 dimensionless [B.3 MCG-06-30-15: "$\log{\delta}=-1.98 \pm 0.06$"]
- result: NGC 985 corona radius r_c = 111 ± +33/-14 R_g [B.5 NGC 985: "$r_\mathrm{c}=111^{+33}_{-14}$ ($R_\mathrm{c}\approx 1.4$ ld) and $\log{\delta}=-2.03^{+0.13}_{-0.22}$"]
- result: NGC 985 log delta = -2.03 ± +0.13/-0.22 dimensionless [B.5 NGC 985: "$\log{\delta}=-2.03^{+0.13}_{-0.22}$"]
- result: NGC 1068 corona radius r_c = 67.5 ± +/-5.5 R_g [B.6 NGC 1068: "We obtain $r_\mathrm{c}=67.5 \pm 5.5$ ($R_\mathrm{c}\approx 0.065$ ld) and $\log{\delta}=-0.98 \pm 0.13$"]
- result: NGC 1068 log delta = -0.98 ± +/-0.13 dimensionless [B.6 NGC 1068: "$\log{\delta}=-0.98 \pm 0.13$"]
- result: NGC 3227 corona radius r_c = 67 ± +48/-26 R_g [B.7 NGC 3227: "$r_\mathrm{c}=67^{+48}_{-26}$ ($R_\mathrm{c}\approx 0.06$ ld) and $\log{\delta}=-2.07^{+0.56}_{-0.48}$"]
- result: NGC 3227 log delta = -2.07 ± +0.56/-0.48 dimensionless [B.7 NGC 3227: "$\log{\delta}=-2.07^{+0.56}_{-0.48}$"]

### 2025MNRAS.537..520Z: WISDOM Project - XXII. A 5 per cent precision CO-dynamical supermassive black hole mass measurement in the galaxy NGC 383 (2025; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Measure the supermassive black hole mass in the nearby lenticular galaxy NGC 383 with unprecedented spatial resolution and precision using ALMA CO(2-1) dynamical modelling.
- step 4: Sample the deconvolved molecular gas distribution and fit the 3D dynamical model to the CO data cube to measure M_BH
  - method: Sample the deconvolved tclean CLEAN components into 4.1 × 10^6 gas particles using SkySampler, re-fit the intermediate-resolution cube to derive informative priors on inclination and M/L, and fit the unmasked high-resolution CO data cube u… [3.2 Gas distribution: "Using the SkySampler tool [8] developed by Smith et al. (2019), we thus uniformly sample the (deconvolved) CLEAN components produced by the CASA task `tclean`, and generate 4 _._ 1 × 10 [6] gas particles that exactly replicate the observed CO surface brightness distribution when convolved by the synthesised beam."] [3.4 Prior distributions: "We then adopt the posterior distributions of the inclination and the inner and outer _𝑀_ / _𝐿_ as priors when fitting the high-resolution data cube."]
  - software: SkySampler, KinMS
  - parameter: 4.1 × 10^6 gas particles; 10 free parameters; N = 404,722 detected pixels; uncertainty rescaling factor (2N)^0.25; 10^5 MCMC steps [3 DYNAMICAL MODELLING: "When computing the _𝜒_ [2], we rescale the uncertainties of the data cube by a factor of (2 _𝑁_ ) [0] _[.]_ [25], where _𝑁_ = 404 _,_ 722 is the number of pixels with detected emission, defined as the pixels included in the mask of Section 2.4."] [3.2 Gas distribution: "Using the SkySampler tool [8] developed by Smith et al. (2019), we thus uniformly sample the (deconvolved) CLEAN components produced by the CASA task `tclean`, and generate 4 _._ 1 × 10 [6] gas particles that exactly replicate the observed CO surface brightness distribution when convolved by the synthesised beam."]
  - assumption: Circular gas orbits in an unwarped thin disc; spatially constant gas velocity dispersion; diagonal covariance matrix; morphological centre coincides with phase centre while kinematic centre is offset [3 DYNAMICAL MODELLING: "In addition, we assume a diagonal covariance matrix, ignoring the correlation between nearby pixels."] [3.1 Mass model: "To generate a model of the gas kinematics, we assume that the gas particles are in circular motions (we explore the validity of this assumption in Section 4.1), with the circular velocity curve determined"]
  - fitting: MCMC chi^2 minimisation (10^5 steps) with chi^2 uncertainties rescaled by (2N)^0.25; posterior distributions from intermediate-resolution fit used as priors on inclination, M/L_inner, and M/L_outer; flat logarithmic prior on M_BH; flat pri… [3.4 Prior distributions: "We then adopt the posterior distributions of the inclination and the inner and outer _𝑀_ / _𝐿_ as priors when fitting the high-resolution data cube."] [3.4 Prior distributions: "We use flat priors for all other parameters except the SMBH mass, for which we use a flat logarithmic prior."]
  - validation: Bootstrapping across four spatial quadrants of the data cube; testing a spatially-constant M/L model; comparing reduced chi^2 against no-SMBH and North et al. (2019) models [3.5 Results: "Indeed, our best-fitting model has a reduced _𝜒_ [2] ( _𝜒_ r [2] ) after rescaling of 1 _._ 59 for the high-resolution data cube and 1 _._ 20 for the intermediate-resolution data cube, better than the _𝜒_ r [2] of the best-fitting model of North et al. (2019), that are 1 _._ 97 for the high-resolution data cube and 1 …"] [4.2.1 chi^2 rescaling: "We divide the data cube into four subsamples, each one a spatial quadrant of the data cube, bounded by planes of fixed position angles."]
- step 5: Model the central velocity twist using tilted-ring and parametric warp/radial-motion models to test impact on M_BH
  - method: Re-image the CO data cube with natural weighting (robust = 2.0), fit non-parametric tilted-ring warp and radial motion models using 3DBarolo 3DFIT, and fit parametric PA warp and linear radial motion models using KinMS. [4.1 Warps and/or non-circular motions: "Instead, we re-image the data with Briggs weighting and a robust parameter of 2 _._ 0 (equivalent to natural weighting), to maximize the _𝑆_ / _𝑁_ ."] [4.1.1 Tilted-ring models: "To create a non-parametric model of a PA warp and/or non-circular motions, we first use the `3DFIT` task of the [3D] Barolo package [9] (Di Teodoro & Fraternali 2015) to fit a tilted-ring model to the data cube."]
  - software: CASA, tclean, 3DBarolo (3DFIT), KinMS
  - parameter: 3DBarolo: 20 rings of width 0.06 arcsec out to 1.2 arcsec, z_0 = 0, TWOSTAGE=true, mask clipped at 1 sigma_RMS; KinMS: 12 free parameters for warp model (PA_inner, PA_outer, R_change) and radial motion model (V_rad,0, R_cutoff) [4.1.1 Tilted-ring models: "We divide the central 1 [′′] _._ 2 in radius of the cube into 20 rings, each with a width of 0 [′′] _._ 06 (approximately the FWHM of the synthesised beam’s minor axis), fully covering and sufficiently sampling the region of the velocity twist."] [4.1.1 Tilted-ring models: "We set "TWOSTAGE=true" so that all parameters vary freely in the first fitting stage, but the geometric parameters _𝑥_ 0, _𝑦_ 0 and _𝑉_ sys of each ring are regularised and kept fixed (by default to the median of the 20 rings) in the second fitting stage."]
  - assumption: Infinitely thin disc (z_0 = 0) in 3DBarolo; either pure PA warp (V_rad = 0) or pure linear radial motion with fixed PA/inclination [4.1.1 Tilted-ring models: "We normalise the gas surface density using the azimuthal average of the observed 12 CO(2-1) intensity in each ring and fix _𝑧_ 0 to be zero (infinitely thin disc approximation), leaving only eight free parameters."]
  - fitting: Two-stage tilted-ring fit in 3DBarolo (Bezier interpolation for warp, median PA/Inc for radial motion) and MCMC chi^2 fit in KinMS evaluated with BIC [4.1.1 Tilted-ring models: "In the first fit (warp model), the PA and Inc of each ring are regularised by Bezier interpolation in the second fitting stage, allowing them to vary across rings (i.e. create a warp), but the radial velocity is fixed to _𝑉_ rad = 0."] [4.1.2 Parametric models: "Considering instead the Bayesian information criterion (BIC), defined as BIC ≡ _𝑘_ ln _𝑁_ − 2 ln _𝑃_, where _𝑘_ is the number of free parameters, _𝑁_ the number of constraints, and −2 ln _𝑃_ is the _𝜒_ [2] defined in Section 3, the warp model is actually less preferred than the regular disc model."]
  - validation: Compared BIC, reduced chi^2, and velocity residuals across regular disc, warp, and radial motion models, and tested masking out the central velocity twist region [4.1.3 Implications for the SMBH mass: "All the models discussed above yield almost identical SMBH masses _𝑀_ BH ≈ 3 _._ 6 × 10 [9] M ⊙, and the best-fitting _𝑀_ BH remains unchanged even if we mask out the twist region during the fit."]
- result: Supermassive black hole mass (M_BH) = 3.58e9 ± ±0.19e9 M_sun [abstract: "By forward modelling the mass distribution and ALMA data cube, we infer a SMBH mass of (3 _._ 58 ± 0 _._ 19) × 10 [9] M ⊙ (1 _𝜎_ confidence interval), more precise (5%) but consistent within ≈ 1 _._ 4 _𝜎_ with the previous measurement using lower-resolution molecular gas data."]
- result: Logarithmic SMBH mass log(M_BH/M_sun) (high-resolution fit) = 9.55 ± ±0.02 (1sigma), ±0.07 (3sigma) dex [Table 4: "log ( _𝑀_ BH /M ⊙ ) 8 _._ 70 – 9 _._ 95 9 _._ 60 ± 0 _._ 05 −0 _._ 11, +0 _._ 15 9 _._ 55 ± 0 _._ 02 ± 0 _._ 07"]

### 2017MNRAS.468.4675D: WISDOM Project - II. Molecular gas measurement of the supermassive black hole mass in NGC 4697 (2017; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the SMBH mass in NGC4697 from molecular gas kinematics, and does it agree with stellar-kinematic measurements?
- step 5: Forward-model cube and sample posterior
  - method: KinMS models fitted to whole cube with MCMC (Gibbs sampling, adaptive stepping), full analytic covariance matrix likelihood, LDL^T factorisation [Bayesian analysis: "we utilize an MCMC method with Gibbs sampling and adaptive stepping to explore the parameter space"]
  - software: KinMS, KINMS_MCMC, emcee
  - parameter: 10 free parameters; priors flat, SMBH mass flat in log between 10^4.8 and 10^9.8 Msun; M/L 0.1-4.0; final chain 10^5 steps, 10% burn-in [Fitting process: "we ran the best chain 10$^5$ steps (with a 10% burn-in) to produce our final posterior probability distribution"]
  - assumption: Exponential gas disc, purely circular motion, spatially constant velocity dispersion [Gas kinematics: "In this work we always assume that the gas is in circular motion"]
  - fitting: Log-likelihood from chi2 with full covariance matrix; best fit is median of the marginalised posterior; 68 and 99% CL [Covariance matrix and likelihood: "We use a standard logarithmic likelihood function based on the $\chi^2$ distribution"]
  - validation: Cross-checked with KinMSpy + emcee; same best-fit values [Bayesian analysis: "These tests showed that our algorithm performs well, and finds the same best-fit values and parameter ranges."]
- result: SMBH mass = 1.3e8 ± +0.18/-0.17 (99% CL, incl. AGN systematic) Msun [abstract: "(1.3$_{-0.17}^{+0.18}$) $\times$10$^8$ M$_{\odot}$"]
- result: i-band mass-to-light ratio = 2.14 ± +0.04/-0.05 (99% CL) Msun/Lsun [abstract: "2.14$_{-0.05}^{+0.04}$ M$_{\odot}$/L$_{\odot}$"]
- result: Reduced chi2 of best model = 1.02 ± not stated dimensionless [Bayesian analysis: "The best model is an excellent fit ($\chi^2_{\rm red}$ = 1.02)."]
- result: Gas disc inclination = 76.1 ± -0.4/+0.5 (68%) deg [Table 2: "Inclination ($^\circ$) | 72.0 | $\rightarrow$ | 89.0 | 76.1"]
- limitation (authors): Gas velocity dispersion is poorly constrained with 10 km/s channels [Gas velocity dispersion: "not well constrained given our channel width of 10 km s$^{-1}$"]
- not stated (card): Number of clean iterations and exact mask definition; Channel range used for the line-free continuum; Hanning smoothing details and exact moment-map parameters; KINMS_MCMC code is unpublished (Davis et al., in prep.); Gibbs sampler settings and convergence criteria not given; Number of walkers/chains and the fitted cube spatial region; Exact MGE fitting settings and dust masking regions in the HS…

### 2023A&A...673A..46B: Another X-ray UFO without a momentum-boosted molecular outflow. ALMA CO(1-0) observations of the galaxy pair IRAS 05054+1718 (2023; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Investigate the presence of a large-scale molecular outflow in the galaxy pair IRAS 05054+1718 (NED01 and NED02) and test the AGN feedback blast-wave scenario by comparing molecular outflow and X-ray ultra-fast outflow energetics.
- step 6: Compute X-ray wind and putative molecular outflow energetics and test feedback scenarios
  - method: Compute X-ray wind mass outflow rate, kinetic power, and momentum rate assuming biconical escape-radius launch; compute putative molecular outflow dynamical timescale, mass outflow rate, momentum rate, and kinetic power from CO residual co… [The X-ray disk wind energetics of NED01: "Following the same arguments presented in many works on disk winds (e.g. (cite: Nardini2015; ReevesBraito2019; Braito2021)), we estimate the mass outflow rate of the X-ray wind using the equation"] [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "Using the molecular gas mass inferred from the CO(1-0) luminosity reported in Table 3, we computed the mass-loss rate ($\dot{M}^{mol}_{out}=M^{mol}_{out}/\tau_{dyn}$), momentum rate ($v\dot{M}^{mol}_{out}$) and kinetic power ($0.5\dot{M}^{mol}_{out}v^2$) for each of the two CO residual spectral features"]
  - parameter: X-ray wind: mu = 1.2, Omega/4pi = 0.5, R_min = 2 G M_BH / v^2, accretion efficiency eta = 0.1, M_BH ~ 10^8 M_sun [The X-ray disk wind energetics of NED01: "$\mu$ is a constant factor set to $\mu=n_{\rm H}/n_{\rm e}=1.2$ for solar abundances"] [The X-ray disk wind energetics of NED01: "We assume that the wind subtends $\Omega/4\pi = 0.5$."]
  - parameter: Molecular outflow: tau_dyn = R / v_out; alpha_CO = 2.1 ± 1.2 M_sun (K km s^-1 pc^2)^-1 (and 0.8 ± 1.2 for Fig. 17 comparison) [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "For each spectral feature, we set $v_{out}$ equal to the Gaussian central velocity, the outflow radius $R$ as the projected distance from the disk centre (see Table 3 and 4 for NED01 and NED02, respectively), and compute the dynamical timescale of the outflow as $\tau_{dyn}^{mol}=R/v_{out}$."] [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "Since (cite: 2020Marasco; 2021Tozzi) adopted $\alpha_{\rm CO} = 0.8 \pm 1.2$ M$_{\odot}$ (K km s$^{-1}$ pc$^2$)$^{-1}$ (cite: 1998Downes; 2013Carilli; 2013Bolatto) in their analysis, we re-derived the energetics applying this conversion factor, obtaining $\dot{p}^{mol}_{out}/\dot{p}^{X-ray}_{out}\sim0.3$."]
  - assumption: Cosmology: H0 = 67.8 km s^-1 Mpc^-1, Omega_Lambda = 0.692, Omega_M = 0.308 [Introduction: "Throughout the paper we adopt a standard $\Lambda$CDM cosmological model with $H_0=67.8$ km s$^{-1}$ Mpc$^{-1}$, $\Omega_{\Lambda}=0.692$, and $\Omega_M=0.308$ (cite: 2016PlanckCollaboration)."]
  - assumption: Maximum coupling efficiency of ~10% for star formation feedback [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "According to (cite: 2005Veilleux), assuming a maximum coupling efficiency of $\sim10\%$, star formation feedback in galaxies with the SFRs inferred for NED01 and NED02 would drive weak outflows, with mass loss rates of $\dot{M}_{out}<1$ M$_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- result: Putative molecular outflow total mass-loss rate in NED01 (upper limit) = 19 ± 14 M_sun yr^-1 [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "The total properties of the putative outflow in NED01 were computed by summing the contribution of all the residual components obtaining a total mass-loss rate of $\dot M^{mol}_{out}=\sum_{i} M^{mol}_{out,i}=19\pm14$ M$_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- result: Putative molecular outflow total momentum rate in NED01 (upper limit) = 2.7 ± 2.4 10^34 g cm s^-2 [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "| $\dot{p}^{mol}_{out}$ \[$10^{34}g~cm~s^{-2}$\] | 2.4 (2.2) | 0.3 (0.2) | 2.7 (2.4) |"]
- result: Putative molecular outflow total kinetic power in NED01 (upper limit) = 3.2 ± 2.2 10^41 erg s^-1 [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "| $\dot{E}^{mol}_{out}$ \[$10^{41}~erg~s^{-1}$\] | 3 (2) | 0.22 (0.15) | 3.2 (2.2) |"]
- result: Putative molecular outflow total mass-loss rate in NED02 = 20 ± 16 M_sun yr^-1 [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "| $\dot{M}^{mol}_{out}$$^{\dag}$ \[$M_{\odot}~yr^{-1}$\] | 17 (15) | 3 (2) | 20 (16) |"]
- result: Putative molecular outflow total momentum rate in NED02 = 2.0 ± 1.7 10^34 g cm s^-2 [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "| $\dot{p}^{mol}_{out}$ \[$10^{34}~g~cm~s^{-2}$\] | 1.8 (1.6) | 0.13 (0.09) | 2.0 (1.7) |"]
- result: Putative molecular outflow total kinetic power in NED02 = 1.6 ± 1.3 10^41 erg s^-1 [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "| $\dot{E}^{mol}_{out}$ \[$10^{41}~erg~s^{-1}$\] | 1.5 (1.3) | 0.05 (0.04) | 1.6 (1.3) |"]
- result: X-ray disk wind momentum rate in NED01 (NuSTAR) = 4 ± 2 10^34 g cm s^-2 [The X-ray disk wind energetics of NED01: "The momentum rate of the X-ray wind is $\dot p_{out}^{X-ray}\sim (4\pm2)\times 10^{34}$ g cm s$^{-2}$ from *NuSTAR*"]
- result: Ratio of molecular to X-ray wind momentum rates in NED01 = 0.67 ± not stated dimensionless [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "The corresponding ratio of the momentum rates of the molecular and X-ray winds is $\dot{p}^{mol}_{out}/\dot{p}^{X-ray}_{out}\sim0.67$, consistent with unity within the large uncertainties."]
- result: CO-derived star formation rate of NED01 = 2.5 ± 0.3 M_sun yr^-1 [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "we estimate a SFR$_{NED01}$($L^{\prime}_{CO}$)=$2.5\pm0.3$ M$_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$ for NED01"]
- result: CO-derived star formation rate of NED02 = 3.6 ± 0.5 M_sun yr^-1 [Hypothesis I: Molecular outflows: "For NED02, using the same procedure, we estimate from the total CO(1-0) luminosity SFR$_{NED02}$($L^{\prime}_{CO}$) = $3.6\pm0.5$ M$_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- limitation (authors): The CO-to-H2 conversion factor alpha_CO in molecular outflows is poorly constrained. [Introduction: "For different ISM environments, such as the massive molecular outflows discovered in local starbursts and AGN host galaxies (cite: 2020Veilleux), the $\alpha_{\rm CO}$ parameter is very poorly constrained."]
- limitation (authors): NED02 is located close to the edge of the ALMA primary beam FWHM, resulting in lower CO(1-0) sensitivity, and its compact size means its morphology and kinematics are more affected by beam smearing.
